--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next ---


# Fegg Coding Guide

:::info

This is the guide for **Fegg 5**. If your project still uses Fegg 4 (`fegg/` and `code/` directories), switch to **Fegg 4.x** in the version dropdown in the navbar.

:::

## About Fegg

Fegg is a small PHP framework, originally created by [Genies, Inc.](https://github.com/genies-inc/Fegg) and extended by LionHeart with a CMS foundation (login, media and image management, post types).

LionHeart projects start from the skeleton repository [lionheart-group/lh-fegg-skelton](https://github.com/lionheart-group/lh-fegg-skelton). It contains Fegg, the CMS, sample pages (top page, news, contact form) and the frontend build setup.

## What is new in Fegg 5

- **PHP 7.4 to 8.5** are supported.
- **Core and site are separated.**
  - `htdocs/_fegg/core/` is the **core**: the framework and the CMS foundation shared by every project. Do not edit it. `fegg-cli core:update` replaces it as a whole, the same way you update WordPress itself.
  - `htdocs/_fegg/site/` is the **site**: your project's controllers, templates and settings. Updates never touch it.
  - To change something the core provides (a CMS template, a CMS screen, a setting), override it from `site/`.
- **Namespaces and autoloading.** Controllers are `Site\Controller\...` classes and framework classes are `Fegg\...` (`Fegg\Model`, `Fegg\Validation`, `Fegg\Tool\Mail`, ...). Create classes with `new`; `getClass()` is no longer needed.
- **Errors fail loudly.** Database errors throw `Fegg\Database\QueryException` and missing templates throw `Fegg\Template\TemplateException`. Uncaught, both become a logged HTTP 500 (the error page `site/template/error/500.html` can be your own) instead of a message printed into the page. `Fegg\Log` writes the application log.
- **Per-server settings and secrets** come from `site/config/env.php` (not committed) or environment variables, read with `feggEnv()`. `FEGG_ENV` (`production` unless set) decides whether developers see error details.
- **Secure by default.** Security headers on every response, sessions only when needed (`HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, strict mode), CSRF tokens in the CMS, login throttling, upload content checks.
- **SQLite or MySQL.** `fegg-cli migration` works on both.
- **Command line tools.** `fegg-cli` checks templates (`template:lint`), lists the database schema (`db:schema`), manages CMS users (`user:create`), updates the core (`core:update`) and checks that `_fegg/` cannot be read from the web (`doctor`).

Fegg 4 code (`MyApplication`, `getClass()`, controllers without a namespace, `Model`, `DB_pdo`, `LH_FW`) still runs through a compatibility layer. Every use is logged as `Fegg deprecated: ...` in the PHP error log, and the layer will be removed in Fegg 6.

## Where to start

- [Directory structure](./first/cont.mdx): what lives in `core/` and `site/`
- [Settings](./first/config.mdx): `site/config/settings.php`, `env.php` and the database connection
- [fegg-cli](./first/cli.mdx): command line tools
- [Updating the core](./first/update.mdx)
- [Migrating a Fegg 3 / 4 project](./first/migration.mdx)
- [URL](./basic/url.mdx) and [Controller](./basic/controller.mdx): how a request reaches your code
- [Security](./basic/security.mdx) and [Logging and errors](./basic/logging.mdx)


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/url ---


# URL

## URL and Controller class

Fegg maps URL segments to a controller class, a method and its arguments:

```
https://example.com/class/method/param1/param2/
```

1. Controller class (`site/controller/Class.php`, first letter in upper case)
2. Method of the controller class
3. Arguments for the method

When the method segment is empty, `index()` is called. When no segment names a controller, the `Index` controller of that directory is used.

Sub directories of `site/controller/` are part of the URL and of the namespace:

| URL                     | File                                       | Called                                   |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |
| `/`                     | `site/controller/Index.php`                | `Site\Controller\Index::index()`         |
| `/news/`                | `site/controller/News.php`                 | `Site\Controller\News::index()`          |
| `/news/detail/12/`      | `site/controller/News.php`                 | `Site\Controller\News::detail('12')`     |
| `/cms/news/edit/3/`     | `site/controller/Cms/News.php`             | `Site\Controller\Cms\News::edit('3')`    |
| `/cms/media/`           | `core/controller/Cms/Media.php` (no site class) | `Fegg\Controller\Cms\Media::index()` |

- The site (`Site\Controller\...`) is searched first, then the core (`Fegg\Controller\...`). A site class with the same name replaces the core's CMS screen (see [Controller](./controller.mdx#extending-a-core-controller)).
- Segments are walked from the left; the first one that names a controller file wins over a directory with the same name.
- Controller and directory names must be valid PHP class names (letters, digits, `_`). For URLs like `/about-us/`, use a [route](#routing).
- Abstract classes (the base `Controller` classes) are never called from a URL.
- Only the controller's own public methods are actions: methods of the base classes, methods named like a method of `Fegg\Application` (`mail`, `in`, `redirect` ...), static methods and names starting with `_` are 404 (see [Controller](./controller.mdx#which-methods-are-actions)).
- If no controller or no method matches, the `NotFound` controller renders the 404 page.

Arguments are always strings.

## Example

```bash title="URL"
https://example.com/post/edit/1/
```

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Post.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

class Post extends Controller
{
    public function edit($id)
    {
        echo $id; // Result: 1
    }
}
```

## Trailing slash

URLs without an extension are normalised to end with `/`. A `GET` or `HEAD` request to `/news/detail/1` is redirected (301) to `/news/detail/1/`, keeping the query string. Other methods (`POST`, ...) are handled in place without a redirect. URLs with an extension (`/news/post_1.html`) are not changed.

Write links in templates with the trailing slash to avoid the extra redirect.

## Routing

To use URLs that do not follow the class/method structure, add routes to **`htdocs/_fegg/site/config/route.php`**:

```php
$route['from'] = 'to';
```

The key is the URL (without the leading `/`) and the value is the `class/method/arguments` path it is mapped to.

- Routes without wildcards are checked first. Then the routes are tried in the order they are written, and the first match wins. Put specific routes above general ones.
- A route without an extension matches the URL with or without a trailing slash (`'contact/'` matches `/contact` and `/contact/`).
- When no route matches, the URL itself is used as the path.

### Wildcards

| Wildcard | Matches |
| :------- | :------ |
| `:num`   | Digits only (`[0-9]+`) |
| `:any`   | One segment: any characters except `/` |

Refer to the matched values with `$1`, `$2`, ... in the value.

### Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/route.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

// Handle index.html (in the skeleton by default)
$route[':any/index.html'] = '$1/index';
// https://example.com/news/index.html -> Site\Controller\News::index()

$route['news/post_:num.html'] = 'news/detail/$1';
// https://example.com/news/post_1.html -> Site\Controller\News::detail('1')

$route['item/:any/'] = 'product/item_lookup/$1';
// https://example.com/item/foobar/ -> Site\Controller\Product::item_lookup('foobar')

$route['contact/']             = 'contact/index';
$route['contact/confirm.html'] = 'contact/confirm';
// https://example.com/contact/confirm.html -> Site\Controller\Contact::confirm()
```

## Static pages

Pages that have no data of their own do not need a controller. Route them to the skeleton's `Site\Controller\Page`, which renders the template named in the route:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/route.php"
$route['about/']          = 'page/render/about';      // site/template/about.tpl
$route['guide/:any.html'] = 'page/render/guide/$1';   // site/template/guide/*.tpl
```

`Page::render()` joins its arguments into the template path. If the template does not exist, it redirects to the top page.

### Route-only controllers

`Page` implements the marker interface `Fegg\Http\RouteOnly`. A controller that implements it is **only called through a route in `route.php`**. The default `/class/method/arguments` mapping skips it, so `/page/render/about/` typed in the browser is a 404. This way a visitor cannot choose which template `Page` renders; only the routes you write can.

Use `RouteOnly` for your own controllers whose arguments must not come straight from the URL:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Landing.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Http\RouteOnly;

class Landing extends Controller implements RouteOnly
{
    public function show($campaign)
    {
        $this->page['campaign'] = $campaign;
        $this->displayPage('landing/' . $campaign);
    }
}
```

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/route.php"
$route['campaign/spring/'] = 'landing/show/spring';
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/controller ---


# Controller

## About Controller

### What is a Controller?

A controller is the class that handles a request. Fegg picks the controller class and its method from the URL (see [URL](./url.mdx)).

```
https://example.com/foo/bar/
```

For this URL, Fegg loads **`htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Foo.php`** and calls its `bar()` method:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Foo.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

class Foo extends Controller
{
    public function bar()
    {
        // process here...
    }
}
```

### File, namespace and class

Every controller file follows the same rules:

- The file is in `htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/`, and the file name is the class name (`Foo.php` declares `class Foo`).
- The namespace is `Site\Controller`. **Sub directories are namespaces**: `site/controller/Cms/News.php` declares `namespace Site\Controller\Cms;` and `class News`.
- The first line after the namespace is `defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;`. It is required in every PHP file under `htdocs/_fegg/`: it stops the file from running when someone requests it directly by URL.
- The class extends `Controller`, the project's base class.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Cms/News.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller\Cms;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Cms\Helper;
use Fegg\Model;
use Site\Controller\Controller; // the base class is in the parent namespace

class News extends Controller
{
    // ...
}
```

### Base classes

There are two kinds of controllers, with a base class each: **public pages** and **CMS screens**.

```
Fegg\Application                                  core/src/Application.php
└ Fegg\Controller\Controller (abstract)            core/controller/Controller.php
  ├ Site\Controller\Controller (abstract)          site/controller/Controller.php       public pages
  │ └ Site\Controller\Index, News, Contact, ...
  └ Fegg\Controller\Cms\Controller (abstract)      core/controller/Cms/Controller.php   CMS screens (core)
    ├ Fegg\Controller\Cms\Image, Media, Login, ...
    └ Site\Controller\Cms\Controller (abstract)    site/controller/Cms/Controller.php   CMS screens (project)
      └ Site\Controller\Cms\News, Lab, ...
```

- `Fegg\Application` provides the methods you call with `$this->...`: [request parameters](./parameters/index.mdx), templates (`displayPage()`, ...), `redirect()`, `loadConfig()`, ...
- `Fegg\Controller\Controller` (core) loads `site/config/category_info.php` and `site/config/site_info.php` in its `__init()`, and declares the properties `$_model` and `$_lh` used by the CMS.

### Which methods are actions

Only a controller's own actions can be called from a URL. A method is an action when it is:

- `public` and not `static`,
- declared in the controller itself (or a parent that is not one of the base classes above). Methods declared in `Fegg\Controller\Controller`, `Site\Controller\Controller` and the two CMS base classes are never actions, even when a controller overrides them,
- not named like a method of `Fegg\Application`: `mail`, `in`, `redirect`, `halt`, `setSession`, `setCookie`, `displayPage` ... are 404 as URL segments,
- not starting with `_` (`__init()`, `_validatePostedData()`).

Anything else answers 404. Put shared helpers in a base class as `protected` methods, and write an action used by several controllers in each of them (it can call a shared helper). Fegg 4 called any method the controller had, including `setSession()` and `mail()` of the framework: this is a Fegg 5 change.
- `Site\Controller\Controller` (site) is yours: the base of the **public pages**. Put what every public page needs here (shared assigns, ...). It replaces `MyApplication` of Fegg 4.
- `Fegg\Controller\Cms\Controller` (core) and `Site\Controller\Cms\Controller` (site) are the bases of the **CMS screens**: see [CMS screens](#cms-screens). Put what every CMS screen of the project needs in `Site\Controller\Cms\Controller`.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Controller.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

abstract class Controller extends \Fegg\Controller\Controller
{
    public function __init()
    {
        parent::__init();

        // Shared by every page of the site
        $this->page['year'] = date('Y');
    }
}
```

Abstract classes are never called from a URL.

### Constructor

A constructor is optional. If you write one, call the parent constructor: it loads the settings, sets up error handling and the language.

```php
public function __construct()
{
    parent::__construct();
}
```

### Initialize

`__init()` is called after the constructor, before the method of the URL. Because the instance already exists, you can use the `Application` methods through `$this` (for example `$this->in()`), which you should not do in the constructor.

**Always call `parent::__init()` first.** Otherwise `site_info.php` and `category_info.php` are not loaded.

```php
public function __init()
{
    parent::__init();

    $this->_lh = new Helper();
    $this->_model = new Model(false, array(
        'table'   => 'cms_news',
        'id'      => 'news_id',
        'visible' => 'visible',
    ));
}
```

:::note

You can omit `__init()` when the controller does not need it. The parent `__init()` is then called automatically.

:::

### Method

The second segment of the URL is the method name.

```php
// Called by https://example.com/foo/bar/
public function bar()
{
    // any process
}
```

If the second segment is empty, `index()` is called.

```php
// Called by https://example.com/foo/ or https://example.com/foo/index/
public function index()
{
    // any process
}
```

Every **public** method can be called from a URL. Make helper methods `private` or `protected` so that they cannot.

```php
// https://example.com/foo/_common/ does NOT run this method
private function _common()
{
    // shared process
}
```

If the method does not exist, the 404 page is shown.

### Arguments

The segments after the method are passed to the method as strings.

```php
// $param1 is "bar" and $param2 is "baz"
//   for https://example.com/foo/show/bar/baz/
public function show($param1 = '', $param2 = '')
{
    echo $param1; // bar
    echo $param2; // baz
}
```

Give the arguments a default value: a visitor can always open the URL with fewer segments, and a missing required argument is an error (HTTP 500).

### Ending a response

[displayPage()](./templates/methods/displaypage.mdx), [redirect()](./others/redirect/redirect.mdx), `requirePost()`, Basic authentication and the CMS login check end the response by throwing `Fegg\Http\Halt`. The dispatcher catches it, writes the session and sends the output. Code after them does not run, as before, but Fegg 5 never calls `exit`.

To stop a controller yourself, call `$this->halt()` instead of `exit`. It prints its argument, if any, and ends the response the same way:

```php
public function ping()
{
    header('Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8');
    $this->halt('pong');   // not: echo 'pong'; exit;
}
```

With `halt()` the dispatcher finishes the request itself (it writes the session and sends the buffered output), and the code stays testable. `exit` still stops the script, but skips all of that.

`Halt` extends `\Error`, not `\Exception`, so `catch (\Exception $e)` does not stop a redirect. A **`catch (\Throwable $e)`** around `displayPage()` or `redirect()` would catch it: rethrow it first.

```php
try {
    $this->_import($file);
    $this->redirect('/cms/import/done/');
} catch (\Fegg\Http\Halt $halt) {
    throw $halt;   // the redirect: let it through
} catch (\Throwable $e) {
    \Fegg\Log::error('Import failed', array('exception' => $e));
    $this->displayPage('cms/import/error');
}
```

## Using other classes

Classes are autoloaded. Import them with `use`, or write the full name with a leading `\`, and create them with `new`:

```php
use Fegg\Cms\Helper;
use Fegg\Model;
use Fegg\Validation;

$this->_lh   = new Helper('news');
$validation  = new Validation();
$upload      = new \Fegg\Tool\Upload();
$cart        = new \Site\Shop\Cart();   // htdocs/_fegg/site/src/Shop/Cart.php
```

Inside a namespace, **prefix PHP's own classes and constants with `\`**. Without it, PHP looks for the class in the current namespace (`Site\Controller\Exception`) and fails.

```php
throw new \Exception('Something went wrong');
$date = new \DateTime('now');
$statement->bindValue(1, $id, \PDO::PARAM_INT);
```

Catch Fegg's exceptions by their full name, for example `\Fegg\Database\QueryException` or `\Fegg\Template\TemplateException`.

:::note

`$this->getClass()` still works for Fegg 4 code, but it is deprecated (each call writes `Fegg deprecated: ...` to the PHP error log) and will be removed in Fegg 6.

:::

## Extending a core controller

The CMS screens provided by the core (`/cms/login/`, `/cms/media/`, `/cms/image/`, ...) are in `htdocs/_fegg/core/controller/Cms/`. Do not edit them. To change one, put a class with the same name in `site/controller/Cms/` that extends the core class, and write only the methods you change. The site class is found first, so it replaces the core one for its URL.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Cms/Media.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller\Cms;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

// Changes only the /cms/media/ list
class Media extends \Fegg\Controller\Cms\Media
{
    public function index($message = null)
    {
        // project-specific processing
        parent::index($message);
    }
}
```

Keep the parent method's signature (the same arguments with the same defaults).

## CMS screens

A CMS screen extends `Site\Controller\Cms\Controller` (in `site/controller/Cms/`, where `Controller` is that class without a `use`). Before your method runs, the base class:

- sends a visitor who is not logged in to the login page,
- creates `$this->_lh` (`Fegg\Cms\Helper`) for the section set in `$_cmsSection`, and sets `$this->page['prefix']` and `$this->page['prev_num']`,
- sends `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`,
- checks the CSRF token of every POST request: a POST without the session's token gets a 403 page.

:::danger Every action that changes data calls `requirePost()` first

The CSRF token is only checked on POST. An action that saves, deletes or sorts must start with `$this->requirePost()`: without it, the action also runs on a GET, so a link or an image on another site can change data while a CMS user is logged in. With it, a GET gets a 405.

This is new in Fegg 5. CMS controllers copied from a Fegg 4 project do not have it: add it to every `update`, `create`, `delete` ... (see [Migrating from v3 / v4](../first/migration.mdx)).

:::

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Cms/Event.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller\Cms;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Model;

class Event extends Controller
{
    // The URL segment under /cms/ (/cms/event/...)
    protected $_cmsSection = 'event';

    public function __init()
    {
        parent::__init();
        $this->_model = new Model(true, array('table' => 'cms_event', 'id' => 'event_id'));
    }

    public function update()
    {
        // Required in every action that changes data (a link or a URL typed in the browser gets a 405)
        $this->requirePost();
        // validate $this->in('title', 'POST') ..., then insertRow() / updateRow()
    }

    public function delete($internalId)
    {
        $this->requirePost();
        $this->_model->delete($internalId);
        $this->redirect($this->_lh->getBackPath('index/DELETED'));
    }
}
```

### Forms and the CSRF token

- A form that prints `{{ hidden }}` sends the token already (the base class adds it to the hidden values).
- Any other POST form adds it with `{{ include '/cms/parts/csrf' }}`.
- Delete buttons are small POST forms, not links. `link-alert` asks for confirmation before sending:

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/cms/event/lists.tpl"
<form class="link-alert" action="{{ $site.prefix }}/delete/{{ $post[$page.this_id] }}" method="post" data-message="削除してもよろしいですか？" style="display: inline;">
    {{ include '/cms/parts/csrf' }}
    <button type="submit" class="c-button is-micro is-danger">削除</button>
</form>
```

- JavaScript can read the token from `<meta name="csrf-token">` and send it as the `cms_csrf` POST field.
- The token is the same for the whole session (several tabs work) and changes when you log in.

### Login and logout

The login screens are core controllers (`Fegg\Controller\Cms\Login`, `Logout`):

- `/cms/login/auth` accepts POST only. Deleted users (`valid = 0`) cannot log in, and old password hashes are upgraded at login.
- Every CMS request checks that the session's user still exists in `cms_user` (`valid = 1`): deleting a user ends their sessions at once.
- **Login throttling**: every attempt is recorded before the password is checked. After 5 failed logins for one login ID from one address, or 20 from one address, logins from that address are refused for 15 minutes. The failures are stored in the core table `cms_login_attempt`: run `fegg-cli migration` after updating. Without the table the login still works, unthrottled, and a warning is logged.
- **Logout is a POST** with the CSRF token, so another site cannot log a user out. `/cms/logout/` opened as a link shows a logout button; links with the class `js-cms-logout` post directly (`htdocs/cms/js/base.js`). If you copied the CMS frame templates to `site/template/cms/`, add that class to the logout link.
- CMS users are created with [`fegg-cli user:create`](../first/cli.mdx#cms-users). `/cms/register/` needs a CMS login and [developer mode](../first/config.mdx#developer-mode).

## Template of Controller class

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Index.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Cms\Helper;
use Fegg\Model;

/**
 * Index Class
 * This file name should be Index.php
 */
class Index extends Controller
{
    /**
     * Initialize
     * Runs after the constructor, so Application methods such as $this->in() can be used.
     * Optional. Always call parent::__init() first.
     */
    public function __init()
    {
        parent::__init();

        $this->_lh = new Helper();
        $this->_model = new Model(false, array(
            'table'   => 'cms_news',
            'id'      => 'news_id',
            'visible' => 'visible',
        ));
    }

    /**
     * Shared process
     * Private, so it cannot be called from a URL.
     */
    private function _common()
    {
    }

    /**
     * Default page
     * Called when the URL has no method segment.
     */
    public function index()
    {
        $this->_common();

        $this->page['news'] = $this->_model
            ->order('post_date', 'desc')
            ->limit(5)
            ->all();

        // Display the compiled template site/template/index.tpl
        $this->displayPage('index');
    }
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/security ---


# Security

> What Fegg does for you, and what to set per project

Most protections are on by default. This page lists them and the settings that change them.

## Secrets and environments

Passwords, API keys and anything else that differs per server are **not committed**. Put them in `htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env.php` (in `.gitignore`; copy `env.php.dist`) or in environment variables, and read them with `feggEnv('NAME', $default)`. See [Settings](../first/config.mdx#environments-and-secrets).

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

return array(
    'FEGG_ENV'              => 'production',
    'FEGG_DB_PASSWORD'      => '...',
    'FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SECRET' => '...',
    'FEGG_SMTP_PASSWORD'    => '...',
);
```

- A site without `env.php` runs as **production**: no error details on screen, for anyone. Test servers whose project path matches `site/config/env/paths.php` (the LionHeart develop server) use the committed `site/config/env/staging.php` instead: it holds only test values, never production secrets ([Test servers deployed with git pull](../first/config.mdx#test-servers-deployed-with-git-pull)).
- Environments are chosen by the server's path, never by the request's host name, which the client controls.
- Error details are shown only in [developer mode](../first/config.mdx#developer-mode): a `developer_ip` address **and** `FEGG_ENV` other than `production`. `developer_ip` alone is not a security boundary: behind a proxy or load balancer every request may come from the same address.

## Security headers

Every response (pages, redirects, 404, error pages, the CMS) gets these headers. The bootstrap sends them before the controller runs.

| Header | Default | Setting in `site/config/settings.php` |
| :----- | :------ | :------------------------------------ |
| `X-Content-Type-Options` | `nosniff` | `$settings['security_headers']` |
| `Referrer-Policy` | `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` | `$settings['security_headers']` |
| `X-Frame-Options` | `SAMEORIGIN` (the CMS image and file pickers are iframes) | `$settings['security_headers']`; `$this->allowFraming()` for one response |
| `Strict-Transport-Security` | not sent | `$settings['hsts']` (sent on HTTPS responses only) |
| `Content-Security-Policy` | not sent | `$settings['csp']`; `$settings['csp_report_only'] = true` sends it as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` |

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
// Change or remove a default ('' removes it), or add another header
$settings['security_headers'] = array(
    'Referrer-Policy'    => 'same-origin',
    'Permissions-Policy' => 'camera=(), microphone=()',
);

// Only once the whole site redirects to HTTPS (and every subdomain, with includeSubDomains)
$settings['hsts'] = 'max-age=31536000';

// Try a policy in report-only mode first
$settings['csp'] = "default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; script-src 'self' https://www.google.com https://www.gstatic.com";
$settings['csp_report_only'] = true;
```

- Turn HSTS on only when every page is served over HTTPS: browsers then refuse HTTP for that domain for `max-age` seconds.
- A CSP must list inline scripts and every external service the site uses (analytics, reCAPTCHA, Turnstile, YouTube ...). Watch the browser console in report-only mode before you enforce it.
- A page that other sites may show in a frame (an embeddable widget) calls `$this->allowFraming()` before `displayPage()`. It removes `X-Frame-Options` for that response only.

## Sessions and cookies

- A session starts only when something is written to it (`setSession()`, `setTicket()`, the CMS). Reading does not create one, so ordinary public pages send no `Set-Cookie`.
- The session cookie is `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, and `Secure` when the request is HTTPS (also behind a proxy that sends `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`).
- `session.use_strict_mode` is on: a session ID the server did not issue is replaced.
- Call [regenerateSessionId()](./parameters/session/regeneratesessionid.mdx) after a login. [unsetSession()](./parameters/session/unsetsession.mdx) without a name ends the session and deletes its cookie.
- [setCookie()](./parameters/cookie/setcookie.mdx) sets `HttpOnly` cookies, `SameSite=Strict` by default, `Secure` over HTTPS. [unsetCookie()](./parameters/cookie/unsetcookie.mdx) deletes one.

## Forms

- **Output escaping**: `{{ $x }}` escapes HTML. In URLs and JavaScript use `feggUrl` and `feggJs`; `./fegg-cli template:lint` reports outputs without them. See [Template security](./templates/security.mdx).
- **Double submission**: public forms use [tickets](./others/tickets/setticket.mdx) (`setTicket()` / `useTicket()`). The skeleton's contact form revokes the ticket when a confirmation fails, and sends the auto-reply to exactly one address.
- **CSRF in the CMS**: every POST to a CMS screen must carry the session's token, or it gets a 403. **Every action that changes data must call `$this->requirePost()` first**: the token is only checked on POST, so an action without it can be triggered by a link on another site. A GET then gets a 405, and deletes are small POST forms, not links. See [Controller](./controller.mdx#cms-screens).
- **Actions**: only a controller's own public methods can be called from a URL, never the framework's (`setSession()`, `mail()` ...). See [Controller](./controller.mdx#which-methods-are-actions).
- **Database**: values always go through `?` placeholders. Table, column and order names are checked by `Fegg\Model` and must never come from user input.
- **Spam**: the contact form supports reCAPTCHA and Turnstile. When the service cannot be reached the check fails, it is never skipped (see [Captcha](../library/captcha/index.mdx)).

## CMS login

- After **5 failed logins** for one login ID from one address, or **20** from one address, logins from that address are refused for **15 minutes**. The failures are kept in the core table `cms_login_attempt`: run `fegg-cli migration` after updating the core. Without the table the login works unthrottled and a warning is logged.
- `/cms/login/auth` is POST only. Deleted users (`valid = 0`) cannot log in, and deleting a user ends their sessions. Old password hashes are upgraded at login. An unknown login ID takes as long to check as a wrong password.
- Logout is a POST with the CSRF token (see [Controller](./controller.mdx#login-and-logout)).
- Users are created and their passwords changed with [`fegg-cli user:create` / `user:password`](../first/cli.mdx#cms-users), which never take the password on the command line. `/cms/register/` needs a CMS login and developer mode.
- Basic authentication compares the user and password with `hash_equals()`. With `basic_auth_scope = 1` it is checked before the controller's `__init()` and action, so redirects, downloads and direct output are protected too.

## Uploads

- The extension must be allowed, and script extensions (`php`, `phtml`, `phar`, `cgi` ...) are always refused.
- The content must match the extension (`getimagesize()` for images, `finfo` for known types): a script renamed to `.jpg` is refused.
- Directories are created `0755`, files `0644`. Files are saved under generated names.
- The upload directory below the document root gets an `.htaccess` that stops scripts from running. `fegg-cli doctor` reports a missing `htdocs/up_dir/.htaccess`. On nginx, deny scripts under the upload URL yourself.
- Stored files are sent with `nosniff` and an escaped `Content-Disposition`; HTML, SVG and XML are always attachments.

See [Upload](../library/upload/index.mdx).

## Protecting `_fegg/`

`htdocs/_fegg/` is inside the document root in the standard layout. It is protected by `.htaccess` files, and every PHP file starts with `defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;`. Check a live site with `./fegg-cli doctor https://example.com` after every FTP upload: it also warns about outdated `.htaccess` rules. See [Directory structure](../first/cont.mdx) and [fegg-cli](../first/cli.mdx#doctor).


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/logging ---


# Logging and errors

> The application log, the error page, and requests to other services

## Application log

`Fegg\Log` writes one line per entry to **`htdocs/_fegg/site/data/log/fegg-YYYY-MM-DD.log`**. Call it from anywhere (controllers, your classes in `site/src/`):

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Log;

Log::info('Import finished', array('rows' => $count));
Log::warning('Unknown category {id}', array('id' => $id));

try {
    $this->_import($file);
} catch (\Exception $e) {
    Log::error('Import failed for {file}', array('file' => $name, 'exception' => $e));
}
```

```text title="site/data/log/fegg-2026-11-10.log"
[2026-11-10 12:34:56 +09:00] ERROR Import failed for news.csv {"file":"news.csv","exception":"RuntimeException: Bad line 3 at /path/Import.php:42"} | POST /cms/import/ 203.0.113.5
```

- `{key}` in the message is replaced with `$context['key']`. The context is appended as JSON; an exception is written as its class, message, file and line.
- For web requests the line ends with the method, the URL and the visitor's address.
- The method names are the PSR-3 levels, from lowest to highest: `debug`, `info`, `notice`, `warning`, `error`, `critical`, `alert`, `emergency`. Each returns `true` when the entry was written.
- Never log passwords, tokens or full personal data.

### Settings

| Setting | Default | Remarks |
| :------ | :------ | :------ |
| `$settings['log_level']` | `''` | Lowest level written. `''`: `warning` in production, `debug` elsewhere (`FEGG_ENV`) |
| `$settings['log_retention_days']` | `30` | Files older than this are removed. `0`: keep all |
| `FEGG_LOG_DIR` (`env.php` or environment variable) | `site/data/log` | Directory of the log files |

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
$settings['log_level'] = 'info';
$settings['log_retention_days'] = 90;
```

`site/data/` is not tracked by git and is denied to the web by `htdocs/_fegg/.htaccess`.

## Uncaught errors

An exception or error that no code catches (a `Fegg\Database\QueryException`, a missing template, a PHP error ...) ends the request with **HTTP 500**:

- It is written to the PHP error log and to the application log, with the stack trace and, for errors raised by a template, the `.tpl` file and line (`Template: news/detail.tpl:12`).
- In [developer mode](../first/config.mdx#developer-mode) the details are shown in the browser.
- Everyone else gets the error page `core/template/error/500.html`. What a half-rendered page already printed is discarded.

To use your own design, put a file at **`htdocs/_fegg/site/template/error/500.html`**. It is plain HTML, not a template (no `{{ }}` tags, no includes), so it still works when the templates or the database are what broke. Inline its CSS or link to files under `htdocs/data/`.

Catch an exception only when the page can do something useful with it; otherwise let it reach the 500 page and the log. When you catch `\Throwable`, rethrow `Fegg\Http\Halt` (see [Ending a response](./controller.mdx#ending-a-response)).

## Requests to other services

`Fegg\Http\Client` is a small HTTP client for calling other services. Fegg itself uses it for the captcha checks, oEmbed and `fegg-cli doctor`.

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Http\Client;
use Fegg\Http\ClientException;
use Fegg\Log;

$client = new Client(10, 3);   // seconds for the whole request, seconds to connect

try {
    $response = $client->get('https://api.example.com/items?category=2', array('Accept: application/json'));
    if ($response['status'] === 200) {
        $items = json_decode($response['body'], true);
    }

    $response = $client->post('https://api.example.com/entries', array('name' => $name));
} catch (ClientException $e) {
    Log::warning('API not reachable: {message}', array('message' => $e->getMessage()));
}
```

- `get($url, $headers = array())` and `post($url, $body, $headers = array())` return `array('status' => int, 'body' => string)`. A `$body` array is sent as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`; a string is sent as is (set the `Content-Type` header yourself, for JSON for example).
- A response with an error status (404, 500 ...) is returned normally: check `status`. Only a transport failure (no connection, timeout) throws `Fegg\Http\ClientException`.
- Only `http` and `https` URLs are accepted (anything else throws `InvalidArgumentException`), and redirects are not followed.
- It uses cURL when the extension is installed, otherwise PHP's http stream wrapper.

## Mail

`Fegg\Tool\Mail` sends through sendmail unless `$settings['mail']` selects SMTP, with the password in `env.php` as `FEGG_SMTP_PASSWORD`. See [Mail](../library/mail/index.mdx#smtp).


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/others ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# About Other methods

Other `Fegg\Application` methods you can call with `$this->...` in a controller.

## Config

Load config files from `site/config/` and read the settings.

<CategoryDocCardList label="Config" />

## Ticket

A ticket protects form processing. `setTicket()` creates a random string, stores it in the session and adds it to the form as a hidden field (output with `{{ hidden }}`). When the form is posted, `useTicket()` checks that the posted value matches the session and then removes it, so the same form cannot be processed twice.

<CategoryDocCardList label="Ticket" />

## Redirect

`redirect()` ends the response by throwing `Fegg\Http\Halt`, like `displayPage()`. Never write `exit` in a controller; use `$this->halt()` (see [Ending a response](../controller.mdx#ending-a-response)).

<CategoryDocCardList label="Redirect" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/others/config/loadconfig ---


# Application::loadConfig

> Load a config file

## Description

```php
public array Application::loadConfig( string $name, string $languageCode = '' )
```

Load the config file `{$name}.php`, store its variable in `$this->config[$name]` and return it.

The file must define a variable with the same name as the file (`sample.php` defines `$sample`). It is searched in this order, and the first file found is loaded:

1. `htdocs/_fegg/site/config/{$name}.php`
2. `htdocs/_fegg/core/config/{$name}.php`

So a site file replaces the core file with the same name (for example `validation_error_message.php`). If `global_config_dir` is set in the [settings](../../../first/config.mdx), the file in that directory is loaded first and the file above can overwrite its values.

On a multilingual site (`default_language` and `support_language` are set), `config/{language code}/{$name}.php` is loaded afterwards and can overwrite values for that language.

The result is cached: calling `loadConfig()` again with the same name returns `$this->config[$name]` without reading the file. If no file defines the variable, Fegg prints `Can't Read Config File: {$name}` and stops.

## Arguments

| Name          | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| :------------ | :-------- | :------ | :------ |
| $name         | string    |         | Config file name without `.php` (also the name of the variable the file defines) |
| $languageCode | string    | ''      | Language code for the per-language file. The current language when omitted |

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/sample.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

// The variable name must be the same as the file name
$sample = array();

$sample['foo'] = 'bar';
$sample['baz'] = 'qux';
```

```php title="Controller"
$this->loadConfig('sample');

var_dump($this->config['sample']);
```

```html title="Output"
array(2) { ["foo"]=> string(3) "bar" ["baz"]=> string(3) "qux" }
```

The return value can also be used directly:

```php title="Controller"
$sample = $this->loadConfig('sample');
echo $sample['foo']; // -> bar
```

`site_info.php` and `category_info.php` are loaded by the base controller's `__init()`, so `$this->config['site_info']` and `$this->config['category_info']` are available in every controller that calls `parent::__init()`.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/others/config/getsetting ---


# Application::getSetting

> Get a value from the settings

## Description

```php
public mixed Application::getSetting( string $name )
```

Get a value of `$settings`: the defaults in `core/config/settings.php`, overridden by `site/config/settings.php` (see [Settings](../../../first/config.mdx)). Returns `''` when the key does not exist.

Project-specific keys work too: add them to `site/config/settings.php` and read them with `getSetting()`.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| :---- | :-------- | :------ | :---------- |
| $name | string    |         | Setting key |

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
$settings['developer_ip'] = array('192.168.65.1');

// A project-specific setting
$settings['contact_admin_email'] = 'info@example.com';
```

```php title="Controller"
var_dump($this->getSetting('developer_ip'));      // -> array(1) { [0]=> string(12) "192.168.65.1" }
echo $this->getSetting('contact_admin_email');    // -> info@example.com
echo $this->getSetting('no_such_key');            // -> ''
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/others/redirect/redirect ---


# Application::redirect

> Redirect to another URL and stop

## Description

```php
public void Application::redirect( string $url )
```

Send a `Location` header (HTTP 302) and end the response. Code after `redirect()` is not run.

`redirect()` does not call `exit`: it throws `Fegg\Http\Halt`, which the dispatcher catches before it writes the session and sends the response. There is no need to write `exit` after it. A `catch (\Throwable $e)` around `redirect()` must rethrow `Fegg\Http\Halt` (see [Ending a response](../../controller.mdx#ending-a-response)); `catch (\Exception $e)` is not affected.

- A URL starting with `http://` or `https://` is used as is.
- Any other URL is a path inside the site. A `/` is added at the start if missing, a trailing `/` is added when the path has no extension (to avoid a second redirect, see [URL](../../url.mdx#trailing-slash)), and the current protocol, domain and base path are added in front. This also works when the site is installed in a subdirectory.

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| :--- | :-------- | :------ | :------ |
| $url | string    |         | Absolute URL, or a path inside the site |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
// -> https://example.com/news/
$this->redirect('/news/');

// -> https://example.com/news/ (slashes added)
$this->redirect('news');

// -> https://example.com/contact/result.html
$this->redirect('/contact/result.html');

// -> https://www.example.org/
$this->redirect('https://www.example.org/');
```

Do not pass a URL taken from the request (for example `$this->in('return_to')`) without checking it, or the site becomes an open redirect.

### Inside try / catch

```php title="Controller"
try {
    $this->_save($data);
    $this->redirect('/entry/done/');
} catch (\Fegg\Http\Halt $halt) {
    throw $halt;
} catch (\Throwable $e) {
    \Fegg\Log::error('Entry could not be saved', array('exception' => $e));
    $this->redirect('/entry/error/');
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/others/tickets/setticket ---


# Application::setTicket

> Issue a one-time ticket for a form

## Description

```php
public void Application::setTicket( string $name )
```

Issue a one-time ticket (a random string) for a form. The ticket is stored in the session as `ticket_{$name}`, and the same value is registered as a hidden field named `ticket_{$name}`. Output it in the form with the `{{ hidden }}` template tag.

When the form is submitted, check it with [Application::useTicket](./useticket.mdx). This prevents the same form from being processed twice (reload, double submit) and rejects posts that did not come from your form page.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| :---- | :-------- | :------ | :---------- |
| $name | string    |         | Ticket name |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
public function confirm()
{
    $this->setTicket('contact');
    $this->displayPage('contact/confirm');
}
```

```php title="Controller"
echo $this->getSession('ticket_contact');
```

```html title="Output"
670f2f1232f30d4d716c1bc9c8a58388
```

```html title="Template: contact/confirm.tpl"
<form method="post" action="{{ base }}/contact/result.html">
    {{ hidden }}
    <button type="submit">Send</button>
</form>
```

```html title="Output"
<form method="post" action="/contact/result.html">
    <input type="hidden" name="ticket_contact" value="670f2f1232f30d4d716c1bc9c8a58388">
    <button type="submit">Send</button>
</form>
```

`{{ hidden }}` outputs every value registered with `setHidden()`, so other hidden values appear there too.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/others/tickets/useticket ---


# Application::useTicket

> Check a one-time ticket and use it up

## Description

```php
public bool Application::useTicket( string $name )
```

Check the ticket issued by [Application::setTicket](./setticket.mdx). Returns `true` only when the POST parameter `ticket_{$name}` has the same value as the ticket in the session. The ticket is then removed from the session, so a second submit of the same form returns `false`.

The ticket is read from **POST** only.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| :---- | :-------- | :------ | :---------- |
| $name | string    |         | Ticket name |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
public function result()
{
    if (!$this->useTicket('contact')) {
        // Reloaded, submitted twice, or not posted from the form
        $this->redirect('/contact/');
    }

    // send the mail ...
    $this->displayPage('contact/result');
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# About Parameters

`Fegg\Application`, the class every controller extends, has helper methods for POST, GET, SESSION and COOKIE. Call them with `$this->...` in a controller.

## POST/GET

Get request data. Values are returned as sent: validate them, and let the template escape them on output.

<CategoryDocCardList label="POST/GET" />

## SESSION

Set and read SESSION data. Never call `session_start()` yourself: Fegg starts the session **only when something is written to it** (`setSession()`, `setTicket()`, the CMS). Reading with `getSession()` or `{{ $session }}` on a request without a session cookie returns nothing and starts nothing, so ordinary public pages send no `Set-Cookie` and can be cached.

- The session cookie is `HttpOnly` and `SameSite=Lax`. It is `Secure` when the request is HTTPS (also behind a proxy that sends `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`).
- `session.use_strict_mode` is on: a session ID the server did not issue is replaced by a new one.
- The session is written when the response ends: after `displayPage()`, `redirect()`, `$this->halt()` or the end of the method (see [Ending a response](../controller.mdx#ending-a-response)).

<CategoryDocCardList label="SESSION" />

## COOKIE

Set and read COOKIE data. Cookies set with `setCookie()` are `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict` by default and `Secure` over HTTPS.

<CategoryDocCardList label="COOKIE" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/cookie/setcookie ---


# Application::setCookie

> Set COOKIE data

## Description

```php
public void Application::setCookie( string $name, string $value, int $expire = '', string $path = '/', string $sameSite = 'Strict' )
```

Set COOKIE data. The cookie is always **`HttpOnly`** (JavaScript cannot read it). It is **`Secure`** (sent back over HTTPS only) when the request is HTTPS, or when `$sameSite` is `None`, which browsers require. Over plain HTTP, on a local development server for example, it works without `Secure`.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default                | Remarks |
| :------ | :-------- | :--------------------- | :------ |
| $name   | string    |                        | Cookie name |
| $value  | string    |                        | Value |
| $expire | int       | ''                     | Lifetime in **seconds from now**. When omitted (or `0`), 1 week (604800 seconds) |
| $path   | string    | '/'                    | Path where the cookie is valid |
| $sameSite | string  | 'Strict'               | `SameSite` attribute: `Strict`, `Lax` or `None` |

## Example

If you omit the third argument, the cookie expires **after 1 week**.

```php title="Controller"
$this->setCookie('foo', 'bar');
```

Set the lifetime in seconds with the third argument.

```php title="Controller"
// This cookie expires after 60 seconds.
$this->setCookie('foo', 'bar', 60);
```

Set the path with the fourth argument and the `SameSite` attribute with the fifth.

```php title="Controller"
// This cookie is only valid under "/test/".
$this->setCookie('foo', 'bar', 0, '/test/');

// Sent back when the visitor follows a link from another site (SameSite=Lax)
$this->setCookie('campaign', 'spring', 86400 * 30, '/', 'Lax');
```

:::note

Fegg 4 passed SameSite inside the path (`'/test/; SameSite=Lax'`). That form still works: the attribute is taken out of the path and used as `$sameSite`.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/cookie/getcookie ---


# Application::getCookie

> Get COOKIE data

## Description

```php
public string|array Application::getCookie( string $name = '' )
```

Get COOKIE data sent by the browser. If the cookie does not exist, returns `''`. If you omit the argument, returns all COOKIE data as an array.

Cookies set with `setCookie()` are sent back by the browser from the **next** request on; `getCookie()` does not see them in the same request.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                 |
| :---- | :-------- | :------ | :-------------------------------------- |
| $name | string    | ''      | Cookie name. All COOKIE data when omitted |

## Example

```php title="Controller - First request"
$this->setCookie('foo', 'bar');
```

```php title="Controller - Second request"
echo $this->getCookie('foo'); // -> bar
```

### Get all cookie data

If you omit the argument, `getCookie` returns all COOKIE data.

```php title="Controller - First request"
$this->setCookie('foo', 'bar');
$this->setCookie('baz', 'qux');
```

```php title="Controller - Second request"
var_dump( $this->getCookie() );
```

```html title="Output"
array(2) { ["foo"]=> string(3) "bar" ["baz"]=> string(3) "qux" }
```

The array also contains the other cookies of the site, such as the session cookie.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/cookie/unsetcookie ---


# Application::unsetCookie

> Delete a cookie

## Description

```php
public void Application::unsetCookie( string $name )
```

Delete the cookie: it is sent again with an expiry date in the past (path `/`), so the browser drops it. It is also removed from `getCookie()` for the rest of the current request. From the next request on, the browser no longer sends it.

Only cookies with the path `/` are deleted. Fegg 4 set an empty cookie for seven days instead.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| :---- | :-------- | :------ | :---------- |
| $name | string    |         | Cookie name |

## Example

```php title="Controller - First request"
$this->setCookie('foo', 'bar');
```

```php title="Controller - Second request"
echo $this->getCookie('foo'); // -> bar
$this->unsetCookie('foo');
```

```php title="Controller - Third request"
echo $this->getCookie('foo'); // -> ''
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/post_get/in ---


# Application::in

> Get request data (POST/GET)

## Description

```php
public string|array Application::in( string $name = '', string $method = '' )
```

Get request data from `$_GET` and `$_POST`.

- With `$name`, returns the value of that parameter (a string, or an array for parameters like `ids[]`). If the parameter is missing or an empty string, returns `''`.
- Without `$name`, returns all parameters as an array (`''` when there are none).
- If GET and POST have a parameter with the same name, POST wins.

The value is returned as it was sent. It is **not** escaped: validate it before use, pass it to the database only through `?` placeholders, and let the template escape it on output.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                              |
| :------ | :-------- | :------ | :------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| $name   | string    | ''      | Request parameter name. All parameters when omitted                  |
| $method | string    | ''      | `'get'` or `'post'` (case-insensitive) to read only one of them. Both when omitted |

## Example

```php title="Controller - GET"
// https://example.com/?foo=get

echo $this->in('foo');         // -> get
echo $this->in('foo', 'get');  // -> get
echo $this->in('foo', 'post'); // -> '' (empty string)
```

```php title="Controller - POST"
// https://example.com/ : POST('foo' => 'post')

echo $this->in('foo');         // -> post
echo $this->in('foo', 'get');  // -> ''
echo $this->in('foo', 'post'); // -> post
```

If POST and GET have a parameter with the same name, POST wins.

```php title="Controller - GET and POST"
// https://example.com/?foo=get : POST('foo' => 'post')

echo $this->in('foo');         // -> post
echo $this->in('foo', 'get');  // -> get
echo $this->in('foo', 'post'); // -> post
```

You can get all request data by omitting the first argument.

```php title="Controller"
// https://example.com/ : POST('foo' => 'bar', 'baz' => 'qux')

var_dump( $this->in() );
```

```html title="Output"
array(2) { ["foo"]=> string(3) "bar" ["baz"]=> string(3) "qux" }
```

A typical use is the page number of a list:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/News.php"
$this->page['data'] = $this->_model
    ->listMax(20)
    ->current($this->in('p'))
    ->order('post_date', 'desc')
    ->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/session/setsession ---


# Application::setSession

> Set SESSION data (and start the session)

## Description

```php
public void Application::setSession( string $name, mixed $value )
```

Set SESSION data. Starts the session if it has not been started, so you never call `session_start()` yourself. Writing is what starts a session: a page that never calls `setSession()` (or `setTicket()`) sends no session cookie.

The session cookie is `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, and `Secure` over HTTPS. The data is saved when the response ends (`displayPage()`, `redirect()`, `$this->halt()`, or the end of the method).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| :----- | :-------- | :------ | :---------- |
| $name  | string    |         | Session key |
| $value | mixed     |         | Value       |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$this->setSession('foo', 'bar');
$this->setSession('cart', array('item_id' => 3, 'quantity' => 1));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/session/getsession ---


# Application::getSession

> Get SESSION data

## Description

```php
public mixed Application::getSession( string $name = '' )
```

Get SESSION data. If the key does not exist, returns `''`. If you omit the argument, returns all SESSION data as an array.

`getSession()` does not create a session: when the request has no session cookie (and nothing was written to the session yet), it returns `''` (or `array()` without an argument) and no cookie is sent. An existing session is opened to read it.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                     |
| :---- | :-------- | :------ | :------------------------------------------ |
| $name | string    | ''      | Session key. All SESSION data when omitted  |

## Example

```php title="Controller - First request"
$this->setSession('foo', 'bar');
```

```php title="Controller - Second request"
echo $this->getSession('foo'); // -> bar
echo $this->getSession('none'); // -> '' (empty string)
```

### Get all session data

If you omit the argument, `getSession` returns all SESSION data.

```php title="Controller - First request"
$this->setSession('foo', 'bar');
$this->setSession('baz', 'qux');
```

```php title="Controller - Second request"
var_dump( $this->getSession() );
```

```html title="Output"
array(2) { ["foo"]=> string(3) "bar" ["baz"]=> string(3) "qux" }
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/session/unsetsession ---


# Application::unsetSession

> Unset SESSION data

## Description

```php
public void Application::unsetSession( string $name = '' )
```

Remove one key from the SESSION data. If you omit the argument, `unsetSession` removes all SESSION data, destroys the session and deletes the session cookie (use it to log a user out). Without a session it does nothing.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                          |
| :---- | :-------- | :------ | :----------------------------------------------- |
| $name | string    | ''      | Session key. Destroys the whole session and its cookie when omitted |

## Example

```php title="Controller - First request"
$this->setSession('foo', 'bar');
```

```php title="Controller - Second request"
echo $this->getSession('foo'); // -> bar
```

```php title="Controller - Third request"
$this->unsetSession('foo');
echo $this->getSession('foo'); // -> ''
```

### Remove all session data

If you omit the argument, `unsetSession` removes all SESSION data, destroys the session and deletes the session cookie.

```php title="Controller - First request"
$this->setSession('foo', 'bar');
$this->setSession('baz', 'qux');
```

```php title="Controller - Second request"
$this->unsetSession();
echo $this->getSession('foo'); // -> ''
echo $this->getSession('baz'); // -> ''
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/parameters/session/regeneratesessionid ---


# Application::regenerateSessionId

> Give the session a new ID

## Description

```php
public void Application::regenerateSessionId()
```

Replace the current session ID with a new one and delete the old session file (`session_regenerate_id(true)`). The SESSION data is kept. Starts the session if it has not been started.

Call it whenever the user's privileges change, typically right after a successful login, so that a session ID obtained before the login cannot be used afterwards (session fixation).

## Arguments

None.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
// _findUser() is your own private method that checks the login ID and password
$user = $this->_findUser($this->in('login_id', 'post'), $this->in('password', 'post'));
if ($user) {
    $this->regenerateSessionId();
    $this->setSession('user_id', $user['user_id']);
    $this->redirect('/mypage/');
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates ---


import { CategoryDocCardList, SiblingDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# About Template

## About Template

Fegg has its own template engine that keeps the application code (PHP) apart from the display (HTML/CSS). A template (`.tpl`) is HTML with `{{ ... }}` tags. Fegg compiles it to PHP, caches the result and runs it when a controller displays the page.

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl"
{{ transclude '/template/siteframe' }}

{{ section contents }}
<h1>{{ $page.data.title }}</h1>
<p>{{ $page.data.post_date|feggDate:'Y/m/d' }}</p>
{{ end section contents }}
```

## Where templates live

| Directory | Owner |
| --- | --- |
| `htdocs/_fegg/site/template/` | The project. Put your templates here. |
| `htdocs/_fegg/core/template/` | The core (CMS screens). Never edit it: it is replaced by `fegg-cli core:update`. |

Templates are looked up in `site/template/` first, then in `core/template/`. To change a core template, copy it to the same path under `site/template/` and edit the copy: the project file wins.

Template names never include the `.tpl` extension. A controller renders `site/template/news/detail.tpl` with:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/News.php"
$this->displayPage('news/detail');
```

When the site is multi-language (`default_language` and `support_language` are set in the settings), the language code is put in front of the name: `news/detail` is read from `site/template/<language code>/news/detail.tpl`.

### Paths in `include` and `transclude`

- A path starting with `/` is relative to the template root (`site/template/`, then `core/template/`). It works the same in a template at any depth. **Prefer it.**
- Any other path is relative to the directory of the template that contains the tag (`./parts/card`, `../template/siteframe`).

```html
{{ transclude '/template/siteframe' }}      site/template/template/siteframe.tpl
{{ transclude '/cms/frame/siteframe' }}     core/template/cms/frame/siteframe.tpl (CMS screens)
{{ include head '/include/head/about' }}    site/template/include/head/about.tpl
{{ include './parts/card' }}                parts/card.tpl next to this template
```

## Cache

Compiled templates are cached in `htdocs/_fegg/site/data/cache/template/`, which must be writable by the web server. A cache file is rebuilt automatically when the template, one of its `transclude` parents or the template compiler changes (any change of modification time or size), so you never need to clear it by hand.

## Checking templates

After writing or editing a template, run:

```bash
./fegg-cli template:lint
```

It compiles every template without running it and reports unknown tags, unclosed blocks, missing `include` / `transclude` targets, undefined modifiers and unsafe outputs, with the `.tpl` line. See [Lint and errors](./lint.mdx).

## Display methods

Display methods render a template from a controller. Use `displayPage` for pages: it sets the variables templates expect and protects against clickjacking.

<CategoryDocCardList label="Display methods" />

## Template helper methods

Template helper methods pass special values (site information, hidden inputs, HTTP headers) from the controller.

<CategoryDocCardList label="Helper methods" />

## Template rules

The tags you can write in a template.

<CategoryDocCardList label="Rules" />

## Modifiers, security and lint

Modifiers transform a value before it is output. The security page explains which escaping each place in the HTML needs, and the lint page lists every check of `fegg-cli template:lint` and how template errors are reported.

<SiblingDocCardList ids={["modifiers", "security", "lint"]} />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/modifiers ---


# Modifiers

Modifiers transform a value before it is output.

## How to use modifiers

A modifier follows the variable after a pipe (`|`). Arguments follow the modifier name after colons (`:`). Modifiers can be chained: they are applied from left to right, each one receiving the result of the previous one.

```html
{{ $page.post_date|feggDate:'Y/m/d' }}
{{ $page.text|lineStrimWidth:80|br }}
```

- Any PHP function works as a modifier: `{{ $x|name:arg1:arg2 }}` calls `name($x, arg1, arg2)`.
- Arguments are PHP expressions: quote strings (`'Y/m/d'`), and use template variables freely (`feggDate:$format`). A `:` or `|` inside a quoted string is part of the string.
- The result is HTML-escaped like any output, unless `noescape` is in the chain.
- Modifiers only run when the variable is set: `{{ $missing|feggDefault:'-' }}` outputs nothing, so use `{{ if }}` for defaults. Arrays are passed to modifiers (`{{ $tags|feggImplode:', ' }}`); when the result is still an array, nothing is output.
- An unknown modifier is reported by `fegg-cli template:lint` (see [Lint and errors](./lint.mdx)).

## Built into the template engine

### noescape

Outputs the value without HTML escaping. Use it only for trusted HTML (CMS rich text written by administrators, messages from your own config). See [Security](./security.mdx).

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['foo'] = '<p>bar</p>';
```

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.foo }}
{{ $page.foo|noescape }}
```

```html title="Output"
&lt;p&gt;bar&lt;/p&gt;
<p>bar</p>
```

### br

Escapes the value, then converts newlines to `<br />` (`nl2br`).

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['foo'] = "First line.\nSecond line.";
```

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.foo|br }}
```

```html title="Output"
First line.<br />
Second line.
```

### replace

`replace:'pattern':'replacement'` replaces with `mb_ereg_replace()`. The pattern is a regular expression, so escape special characters (`'\.'`).

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.tel|replace:'-':'' }}
```

## Fegg modifiers

Defined in `htdocs/_fegg/core/functions/modifier.php`.

| Modifier | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `feggDate:'Y/m/d'` | Formats a date string or a Unix timestamp with the `date()` format. Also accepts `x` / `X` (Japanese day of the week: `火` / `火曜日`) and `V` / `v` (Japanese era and year: `令和` / `7`). Outputs nothing for an empty or invalid date |
| `feggImplode:', '` | Joins an array with the glue |
| `feggDefault:'-'` | The replacement when the value is an empty string or an empty array. Does not apply to unset variables |
| `feggCheck:'value':'yes':'no'` | `'yes'` when the value is identical (`===`) to `'value'`, otherwise `'no'` |
| `feggReplace:'a':'b'` | `str_replace('a', 'b', $value)` |
| `feggPregReplace:'/a/':'b'` | `preg_replace('/a/', 'b', $value)`. An optional third argument is the maximum number of replacements |
| `feggUrlParam:$params` | Appends query parameters (an array or a string) to a URL, with `?` or `&` |
| `feggUrl` | A URL safe for `href` / `src`: any scheme other than `http`, `https`, `mailto` and `tel` (`javascript:`, `data:` …) becomes `#` |
| `feggJs` | The value as a JavaScript literal (string, number, array, object, null) with `<`, `>`, `&`, `'` and `"` encoded. Do not put quotes around it. See [Security](./security.mdx) |

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['post_date'] = '2025-04-01 10:00:00';
$this->page['category'] = '1';
$this->page['data']['link'] = 'javascript:alert(1)';
$this->page['query'] = array('category' => 2, 'p' => 3);
$this->setSiteinfo('prefix', '/news/');
```

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.post_date|feggDate:'Y年n月j日(x)' }}
<li class="c-tab{{ $page.category|feggCheck:'1':' is-active':'' }}">
<a href="{{ $page.data.link|feggUrl }}">Link</a>
<a href="{{ $site.prefix|feggUrlParam:$page.query }}">Search</a>
```

```html title="Output"
2025年4月1日(火)
<li class="c-tab is-active">
<a href="#">Link</a>
<a href="/news/?category=2&amp;p=3">Search</a>
```

### Joining an array

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.tags|feggImplode:', ' }}
```

## CMS modifiers

Defined in `htdocs/_fegg/core/functions/cms-modifier.php` and `model.php`.

| Modifier | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `getImageUrl` | URL of a CMS image from its ID |
| `getImageUrl:320` | Resized to a width of 320px |
| `getImageUrl:780:360` | Resized to 780 × 360px. A third argument changes the crop mode in the file name (default `'i'`) |
| `getImageUrlFromDB`, `getImageUrlFromDB:320`, `getImageUrlFromDB:780:360` | URL that generates the image on every request (`/cms/image/get/...`) |
| `getFileUrl` | URL of a CMS file from its ID |
| `getFileRawUrl` | Direct URL of the stored file (looks the file up in `cms_file`) |
| `byteConvert` | A byte count as a readable size: `1536` → `1.5 KB` |
| `lineStrimWidth:40` | Removes tags and newlines, then shortens the text to a display width of 40, adding `…`. A second argument replaces `…` |
| `url2link` | Turns URLs in text into `<a>` links. The result is HTML: see the warning below |
| `feggCategoryLabel:'news'` | Label of a category ID, from the list `news` of `site/config/category_info.php` |

```html title="Template"
<img src="{{ $page.data.image_id|getImageUrl:780:360 }}" alt="">
<a href="{{ $page.data.file_id|getFileUrl }}">Download</a>
<span class="c-label">{{ $post.category|feggCategoryLabel:'news' }}</span>
<p>{{ $post.body|lineStrimWidth:80 }}</p>
```

:::warning

`url2link` returns HTML and does not escape the rest of the text, so it needs `noescape`. Use it only on trusted text (written by administrators in the CMS), never on user input.

:::

## Project modifiers

Put your own modifiers in `htdocs/_fegg/site/functions/*.php`. These files are loaded automatically after the core's. Every file under `htdocs/_fegg/` starts with the direct-access guard.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/functions/modifier.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

function priceFormat($value)
{
    return number_format((int) $value) . ' yen';
}
```

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.price|priceFormat }}
```

The skeleton defines `isNew($date, $gap = 2)`: true when the date is less than `$gap` days old. Use it in a condition: `{{ if isNew($post.post_date) }}NEW{{ end }}`.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/security ---


# Security

> Escaping by context, and templates as code

## Escaping by context

`{{ $x }}` escapes the value with `htmlspecialchars` (`ENT_QUOTES`). That is right for HTML text and for **quoted** attribute values, but not enough in URLs and JavaScript. Use the modifier that matches the place:

| Where the value goes | Write |
| --- | --- |
| HTML text, quoted attribute values | `{{ $x }}` |
| `<textarea>` | `{{ $x }}` (the browser shows the original text) |
| `href` / `src` / `action` / `formaction` taking a URL from data | `href="{{ $x\|feggUrl }}"` |
| Inside `<script>` | `var x = {{ $x\|feggJs\|noescape }};` (no quotes around it) |
| Event attributes (`onclick` …) | `onclick="go({{ $x\|feggJs }})"` (no quotes around it, keep the escaping) |
| Trusted HTML (CMS rich text, messages from your config) | `{{ $x\|noescape }}` |

Always quote attribute values (`class="{{ $x }}"`, not `class={{ $x }}`).

### URLs: `feggUrl`

HTML escaping does not stop a `javascript:` URL. `feggUrl` returns `#` for any URL whose scheme is not `http`, `https`, `mailto` or `tel` (also when the scheme is disguised with spaces or control characters). Relative URLs are kept.

```html title="Template"
<a href="{{ $page.data.link|feggUrl }}">{{ $page.data.link_label }}</a>
```

URLs built by the CMS modifiers (`getImageUrl`, `getImageUrlFromDB`, `getFileUrl`, `getFileRawUrl`) and values from `$site` (your configuration) do not need it.

### JavaScript: `feggJs`

`feggJs` turns any value (string, number, array, object, null) into a JavaScript literal and encodes `<`, `>`, `&`, `'` and `"`, so the value can neither end the string nor close the `<script>` element. It writes the quotes itself: do not add your own.

```html title="Inside a script element: add noescape"
<script>
    var title = {{ $page.title|feggJs|noescape }};
</script>
```

```html title="In an event attribute: keep the escaping"
<button type="button" onclick="showItem({{ $item.id|feggJs }})">Show</button>
```

In an attribute, the browser decodes the HTML escaping before it runs the JavaScript, so the normal `{{ }}` escaping must stay: never add `noescape` there.

:::tip

Arrays and objects work too, so a whole data set can go to a script in one line:

```html title="Template"
<script>
    var items = {{ $page.items|feggJs|noescape }};   // [{"id":1,"title":"..."}, ...]
</script>
```

:::


### `noescape`

`noescape` outputs the value as it is. Use it only for HTML you trust: CMS rich text written by administrators, validation messages from your config, or the result of `feggJs` inside `<script>`. Never use it on `$in` (request parameters) or anything a visitor can type.

## Templates are code

`{{ code }}`, conditions, `{{ assign }}` and modifiers run arbitrary PHP. Treat templates like PHP files:

- Only developers may edit templates. Never let site users upload or edit `.tpl` files.
- Never build a template name, an `include` path or a modifier name from user input.
- A controller that picks the template from its URL arguments must only be reachable through `site/config/route.php`. Implement `Fegg\Http\RouteOnly`, as the skeleton's `Site\Controller\Page` does:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/route.php"
$route['about/'] = 'page/render/about';   // site/template/about.tpl
```

`/page/render/...` typed in the browser is then a 404.

## What lint checks

`./fegg-cli template:lint` reports outputs that lack the right modifier:

- an output in `<script>` without `feggJs` and `noescape` (scripts with a non-JavaScript `type`, such as `text/template`, are skipped);
- an output in an `on...` attribute without `feggJs`, or with `noescape`;
- an output at the start of an `href`, `src`, `action` or `formaction` value without `feggUrl` (or a CMS URL modifier).

Outputs of `$site` values are exempt from these checks. See [Lint and errors](./lint.mdx).


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/lint ---


# Lint and errors

> Check templates with `fegg-cli template:lint`, and find the template line of an error

## template:lint

```bash
./fegg-cli template:lint                       # every template in site/template and core/template
./fegg-cli template:lint path/to/file.tpl ...  # only the given files
```

The command compiles the templates without running them and prints one line per problem, as `file:line: message`, using the line of the `.tpl` file. It ends with `OK: n template(s)`, or with `NG: n problem(s) in m template(s)` and exit status 1, so it can be used in CI. A file given on the command line must be under a template directory.

Run it after every template change. `composer test` runs it too, on every template of the repository.

```text title="Example output"
htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:2: text outside {{ section }} in a template with {{ transclude }}: it is output before the parent template
htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:6: unknown tag {{ endif }} (it is output as text)
htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:8: include target not found: ./missing
htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:9: unknown modifier "fegDate" (define it in site/functions/*.php)
NG: 4 problem(s) in 1 template(s)
```

## What it checks

| Check | Example message |
| --- | --- |
| Tags no rule understands. They would be output as text | `unknown tag {{ endif }} (it is output as text)` |
| Unclosed blocks (`if`, `foreach`, `loop`, `section`) | `{{ foreach }} is never closed` |
| Extra closing tags, `else` outside an `if` | `{{ end }} without an open block`, `{{ else }} outside an {{ if }}` |
| A closing tag that does not match its block (it still closes the open block) | `{{ end foreach }} closes the {{ if }} opened on line 1` |
| PHP that does not parse after compiling (a broken expression in `if`, `assign`, `code` …) | `compiled PHP does not parse: syntax error, ...` |
| Missing `transclude` parent | `transclude target "/no/such/frame" not found` |
| Missing `include`, `include head`, `include foot` and `include html` targets (literal paths only) | `include target not found: ./missing` |
| Modifiers that are not defined PHP functions | `unknown modifier "fegDate" (define it in site/functions/*.php)` |
| Text outside `{{ section }}` in a template that uses `transclude` | `text outside {{ section }} in a template with {{ transclude }}: ...` |
| An output in `<script>` without `feggJs` and `noescape` | `... is in <script>: write {{ $value\|feggJs\|noescape }} (no quotes around it)` |
| An output in an `on...` attribute without `feggJs`, or with `noescape` | `... is in an event attribute: write {{ $value\|feggJs }} (no quotes around it, no noescape)` |
| An output starting an `href`, `src`, `action` or `formaction` value without `feggUrl` | `... starts a URL: write {{ $value\|feggUrl }} so javascript: URLs are refused` |

The last three checks skip `$site` values (configuration), the CMS URL modifiers (`getImageUrl`, `getImageUrlFromDB`, `getFileUrl`, `getFileRawUrl`) for URLs, and `<script>` elements whose `type` is not JavaScript (such as `text/template`). See [Security](./security.mdx).

## Errors at runtime

### `TemplateException`

These problems throw `Fegg\Template\TemplateException`:

- the template given to `displayPage()` / `fetchPage()` / `displayTemplate()` / `fetchTemplate()` does not exist;
- an `{{ include }}` target or the `{{ transclude }}` parent does not exist;
- blocks are unbalanced (an unclosed block, an extra `{{ end }}`, an `{{ else }}` outside an `{{ if }}`);
- the cache file cannot be written (check that `htdocs/_fegg/site/data/cache/template/` is writable).

Uncaught, the exception becomes an HTTP 500 and is written to the PHP error log. Nothing of the half-rendered page is sent. The message names the template and the line, for example `{{ if }} is never closed (htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:4)`.

Catch it when a missing template is a normal case:

```php
try {
    $this->displayPage('campaign/current');
} catch (\Fegg\Template\TemplateException $e) {
    $this->displayPage('not_found');
}
```

### Errors inside a template

A template runs as PHP compiled into the cache (`htdocs/_fegg/site/data/cache/template/*.cache.php`). The compiled code keeps the line numbers of the `.tpl` file, and Fegg maps errors back to the template: the error log gets a `Template: <file>.tpl:<line>` line.

```text title="PHP error log"
Fegg: Uncaught ErrorException: Undefined array key "x" in /path/to/htdocs/_fegg/site/data/cache/template/@news@detail.1a2b3c4d.cache.php:12
Template: htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:12
Stack trace:
...
```

In [developer mode](../../first/config.mdx#developer-mode) (a `developer_ip` address on a server whose `FEGG_ENV` is not `production`) the browser also shows the error, the `Template:` line and the stack trace. Everyone else gets the error page (see [Logging and errors](../logging.mdx)).

## Pitfalls

- **Closing tags**: `{{ endif }}`, `{{ /if }}` and `{{ endforeach }}` are not tags; they are output as text. Use `{{ end }}` (any block), `{{ end foreach }}` or `{{ end section name }}`.
- **Inside `{{ section }}`**, `{{ assign }}`, `{{ include head }}` and `{{ include foot }}` only affect the section, because it runs as a function. Write them at the top level of the template.
- **Text outside sections**: in a template that uses `{{ transclude }}`, everything outside `{{ section }}` blocks is output before the parent layout.
