- **Loop variables** share the namespace of `$page`, `$site` …: `{{ foreach $list as $page }}` overwrites `$page`.
- **Concatenation**: in tags, `$a.b` is always array access. Write `$a . $b`, with spaces, to concatenate.
- **Arrays need a modifier**: `{{ $array }}` outputs nothing; `{{ $array|feggImplode:', ' }}` or `{{ $array|feggJs|noescape }}` turns it into text.
- **Defaults for unset values**: `{{ $missing|feggDefault:'-' }}` outputs nothing, because output is skipped for unset values before the modifier runs. Use `{{ if $x }}...{{ else }}-{{ end }}`.
- **Null-safe only in some places**: an unset variable reads as `null` in `if`, `else if`, `assign` and `loop`. In modifier arguments and `{{ code }}`, it raises a warning on PHP 8, which Fegg turns into an error.
- **Relative paths**: a path without `/` in `include` / `transclude` is relative to the template that contains the tag. Counting `../` is the classic mistake: prefer `/`-rooted paths.
- **Literal braces**: text that contains `{{` (Vue templates, copied text) is read as a tag. Write `{-{` and `}-}` (see [Literal braces](./rules/literal_braces.mdx)).


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/helpers/setsiteinfo ---


# Application::setSiteinfo

> Set website information (title, description, keywords …)

## Description

```php
public void Application::setSiteinfo( string $id, mixed $value )
```

Sets one entry of the site information. Templates read it as `$site` (set by [Application::fetchPage](../methods/fetchpage.mdx)).

The base controller (`Fegg\Controller\Controller::__init()`) already loads every entry of `htdocs/_fegg/site/config/site_info.php` this way, so `$site.title`, `$site.description` … have their default values on every page. Call `setSiteinfo()` in a controller to change a value for one page. A template can also change it with `{{ assign }}` before its `{{ transclude }}` (see [Assign](../rules/assign.mdx)).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $id    | string    |         | Key |
| $value | mixed     |         | Value (a string or an array) |

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/site_info.php"
$site_info = array(
    'title'       => 'Example Inc.',
    'description' => '',
    // ...
);
```

```php title="Controller"
$this->setSiteinfo('title', 'News | Example Inc.');
```

```html title="Template"
<title>{{ $site.title }}</title>
```

```html title="Output"
<title>News | Example Inc.</title>
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/helpers/sethidden ---


# Application::setHidden

> Set values for hidden inputs

## Description

```php
public void Application::setHidden( mixed $name, [ mixed $value = "" ] )
public void Application::unsetHidden( string $name )
```

`setHidden()` stores a value that the `{{ hidden }}` tag prints as `<input type="hidden">`. `unsetHidden()` removes one again.

Names and values are HTML-escaped (a value that is already escaped is not escaped twice). An array value is printed as one input per element, with names such as `tags[0]` and `tags[1]`.

See also [Hidden](../rules/hidden.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type      | Default | Remarks |
| ------ | -------------- | ------- | ------- |
| $name  | string / array |         | Hidden name, or an array `name => value` to set several at once |
| $value | string / array | ""      | Hidden value (ignored when `$name` is an array) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$this->setHidden('internal_id', $internalId);
```

```html title="Template"
{{ hidden }}
```

```html title="Output"
<input type="hidden" name="internal_id" value="12">
```

### Assigning multiple values

```php title="Controller"
$this->setHidden(array(
    'foo'  => 'bar',
    'tags' => array('a', 'b'),
));
```

```html title="Template"
{{ hidden }}
```

```html title="Output"
<input type="hidden" name="foo" value="bar"><input type="hidden" name="tags[0]" value="a"><input type="hidden" name="tags[1]" value="b">
```

### Removing a value

```php title="Controller"
$this->unsetHidden('internal_id');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/helpers/sethtmlheader ---


# Application::setHtmlHeader

> Send an HTTP response header

## Description

```php
public void Application::setHtmlHeader( string $header )
```

Sends an HTTP response header (it calls PHP's `header()`). Call it before the page is output, for example before [Application::displayPage](../methods/displaypage.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $header | string    |         | Header line, such as `Content-Type: application/json` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$this->setHtmlHeader('Content-Type: text/javascript; charset=UTF-8');
echo $this->fetchPage('data/config_js');
```

Sends the `Content-Type: text/javascript; charset=UTF-8` header, then outputs the template.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/methods/displaypage ---


# Application::displayPage

> Display a page with the standard template variables and end the response

## Description

```php
public void Application::displayPage( string $template )
```

Renders the template with [Application::fetchPage](./fetchpage.mdx) (so `$page`, `$site`, `$in` … are available), outputs the page and ends the response by throwing `Fegg\Http\Halt` (not `exit`; see [Ending a response](../../controller.mdx#ending-a-response)). Code after `displayPage()` does not run. A `catch (\Throwable $e)` around it must rethrow `Fegg\Http\Halt`.

The security headers (`X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy` ...) are sent for every response before the controller runs, not by `displayPage()` (see [Security](../../security.mdx#security-headers)). To let other sites show one page in a frame, call `$this->allowFraming()` before `displayPage()`.

This is the method to use for every page. The site-wide Basic authentication (`basic_auth_scope = 1`) is checked earlier, before the controller runs.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $template | string    |         | Template name relative to the template root, without `.tpl` (`news/detail`) |

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/News.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

class News extends Controller
{
    public function detail($id = '')
    {
        $this->page['title'] = 'Hello';
        $this->displayPage('news/detail');
    }
}
```

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl"
<h1>{{ $page.title }}</h1>
```

```html title="Output"
<h1>Hello</h1>
```

## Errors

When the template does not exist, `Fegg\Template\TemplateException` is thrown. Uncaught, it becomes an HTTP 500 that is written to the PHP error log; nothing of the half-rendered page is sent. Catch it when a missing template is expected:

```php
try {
    $this->displayPage('campaign/current');
} catch (\Fegg\Template\TemplateException $e) {
    $this->displayPage('not_found');
}
```

:::warning

Never build the template name from user input without checking it. A controller that chooses a template from its URL arguments should only be reachable through `site/config/route.php` (implement `Fegg\Http\RouteOnly`, like the skeleton's `Site\Controller\Page`).

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/methods/fetchpage ---


# Application::fetchPage

> Return a page rendered with the standard template variables as a string

## Description

```php
public string Application::fetchPage( string $template )
```

Collects the standard template variables (below), renders the template with [Application::fetchTemplate](./fetchtemplate.mdx) and returns the result instead of outputting it. [Application::displayPage](./displaypage.mdx) uses it.

Use it when you need the page as a string: to post-process it, or as the body of a mail. Unlike `displayPage()`, it does not end the response. The security headers do not depend on which method you use (see [Security](../../security.mdx#security-headers)).

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $template | string    |         | Template name relative to the template root, without `.tpl` |

## Template variables

| Variable | Content |
| --- | --- |
| `$page` | `$this->page` of the controller: the page data |
| `$site` | Site information: `site/config/site_info.php` (loaded by the base controller) plus values set with [setSiteinfo](../helpers/setsiteinfo.mdx) |
| `$in` | Request parameters, `$_GET` and `$_POST` (`$this->in()`). Not escaped: always output them with the default escaping |
| `$config` | Loaded config files (`$this->config`) |
| `$session` | Session data, plus `session_id` and `session_name` while a session is active. Reading it does not start a session: without a session cookie it is empty |
| `$cookie` | Cookie data |
| `$className` | Controller class name without namespace (`News`) |
| `$languageCode` | Current language code |
| `$define` | User-defined PHP constants |
| `$hiddenForTemplate` | Values set with [setHidden](../helpers/sethidden.mdx), printed by the `{{ hidden }}` tag |

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Foo.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

class Foo extends Controller
{
    // URL: https://example.com/foo/bar/?get=param
    public function bar()
    {
        $this->page['baz'] = 'qux';
        echo $this->fetchPage('foo/bar');
    }
}
```

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/foo/bar.tpl"
Baz param : {{ $page.baz }}
$_GET[get] param : {{ $in.get }}
Controller name : {{ $className }}
```

```html title="Output"
Baz param : qux
$_GET[get] param : param
Controller name : Foo
```

:::note

The site-wide Basic authentication (`basic_auth_scope = 1`) is checked before the controller runs, so it also protects actions that only call `fetchPage()`.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/methods/displaytemplate ---


# Application::displayTemplate

> Output a template with only the variables you pass

## Description

```php
public void Application::displayTemplate( string $template, [ array $assignedValue = array() ] )
```

Finds the template (`site/template/` first, then `core/template/`), compiles it when the cache is missing or out of date, and outputs it. Only the variables in `$assignedValue` are available: `$page`, `$site`, `$in` … are **not** set. Use [Application::displayPage](./displaypage.mdx) for pages.

The template engine itself uses this method for `{{ include }}` and `{{ transclude }}`. Inside a template, a name not starting with `/` is relative to the directory of the current template; from a controller, names are relative to the template root.

## Arguments

| Name           | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| -------------- | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $template      | string    |         | Template name without `.tpl` (`foo/bar`) |
| $assignedValue | array     | array() | Variables for the template: the key `title` becomes `$title` |

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Foo.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

class Foo extends Controller
{
    public function bar()
    {
        $this->displayTemplate('foo/bar', array(
            'title' => 'Hello',
            'items' => array('a', 'b'),
        ));
    }
}
```

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/foo/bar.tpl"
<h1>{{ $title }}</h1>
{{ foreach $items as $item }}<p>{{ $item }}</p>{{ end foreach }}
{{ if $page.baz }}not shown: $page is not set{{ end }}
```

```html title="Output"
<h1>Hello</h1>
<p>a</p><p>b</p>
```

:::note

Do not include the `.tpl` extension in the name. A missing template throws `Fegg\Template\TemplateException` (see [Lint and errors](../lint.mdx)).

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/methods/fetchtemplate ---


# Application::fetchTemplate

> Return a template rendered with only the variables you pass, as a string

## Description

```php
public string Application::fetchTemplate( string $template, [ array $assignedValue = array() ] )
```

Same as [Application::displayTemplate](./displaytemplate.mdx), but returns the output as a string instead of printing it. Leading blank lines are removed from the result.

The name is always relative to the template root, even when `fetchTemplate` is called while another template is running. If rendering fails, the partial output is discarded and the exception is thrown again.

Typical uses are mail bodies and HTML fragments for Ajax responses.

## Arguments

| Name           | Data type | Default | Remarks |
| -------------- | --------- | ------- | ------- |
| $template      | string    |         | Template name without `.tpl` |
| $assignedValue | array     | array() | Variables for the template: the key `name` becomes `$name` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$body = $this->fetchTemplate('mail/notice', array(
    'name' => $this->in('name'),
));
```

```text title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/mail/notice.tpl"
A message from {{ $name }}.
```

:::note

Do not include the `.tpl` extension in the name. `$page`, `$site`, `$in` … are only set by [Application::fetchPage](./fetchpage.mdx): pass what the template needs in `$assignedValue`.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/variable ---


# Variable

> Output variables

## Description

```html
{{ $var }}
{{ $var.key.sub }}
{{ $var|modifier }}
```

`{{ $var }}` outputs a variable. Array elements are separated with a period: `$var.key.sub` is `$var['key']['sub']`. Numeric keys and keys that are PHP keywords work too (`$rows.0.name`, `$item.new`, `$site.default`). Square brackets take an expression as the key: `$page.data[$id]`, `$map[$key].name`.

Values from the controller are set in `$this->page` and read as `$page` in the template. The other variables available in a page are listed in [Application::fetchPage](../methods/fetchpage.mdx#template-variables).

The output is escaped with `htmlspecialchars` (`ENT_QUOTES`), which is right for HTML text and quoted attribute values. Other places (URLs, JavaScript) need a modifier: see [Security](../security.mdx). To output trusted HTML as it is, use the `noescape` modifier (see [Modifiers](../modifiers.mdx)).

Nothing is output (and no error is raised) when the variable is not set, or when it is an array and no modifier is applied. Modifiers receive arrays, so `{{ $tags|feggImplode:', ' }}` and `{{ $items|feggJs|noescape }}` work; a chain whose result is still an array outputs nothing.

Inside a tag, `$a.b` is always array access. To concatenate strings in an expression (`if`, `assign` …), write the PHP operator with spaces: `$a . $b`.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['title'] = 'Hello';
$this->page['rows'] = array(
    array('name' => 'first'),
);
$this->displayPage('template');
```

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.title }}
{{ $page.rows.0.name }}
{{ $page.missing }}
```

```html title="Output"
Hello
first

```

### Escaping and `noescape`

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['foo'] = '<p>bar</p>';
```

```html title="Template"
{{ $page.foo }}
{{ $page.foo|noescape }}
```

```html title="Output"
&lt;p&gt;bar&lt;/p&gt;
<p>bar</p>
```

:::warning

Use `noescape` only for HTML you trust (CMS rich text written by administrators, messages from your own config). Never use it on request parameters (`$in`) or other user input.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/assign ---


# Assign

> Set a variable in the template

## Description

```html
{{ assign $var = expression }}
{{ assign $var.key = expression }}
```

Sets a template variable to the value of a PHP expression. The expression may use template variables (`$page.title`), string literals, operators and PHP functions, but cannot contain `}`. Nothing is output.

A variable that is not set reads as `null` in the expression, without a warning.

A common use is to change site information (`$site`) before `{{ transclude }}`, so that the parent layout sees the new value.

## Example

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/about.tpl"
{{ assign $site.title = 'About | ' . $site.title }}
{{ transclude '/template/siteframe' }}

{{ section contents }}
{{ assign $format = 'Y/m/d' }}
<p>{{ $page.updated|feggDate:$format }}</p>
{{ end section contents }}
```

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/template/siteframe.tpl"
<title>{{ $site.title }}</title>
```

```html title="Output"
<title>About | Example Inc.</title>
```

:::note

Inside a `{{ section }}`, `assign` only changes the variable for that section: a section runs as a function. To change a value the layout reads (such as `$site.title`), assign it at the top level of the template, outside every section.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/if ---


# If

> if, else if, else

## Description

```html
{{ if condition }}
{{ else if condition }}
{{ else }}
{{ end }}
```

Outputs a part of the template only when a condition is true. The condition is a PHP expression: comparison and logical operators (`===`, `>`, `&&`, `||`, `!` …) and PHP functions (`is_array`, `count` …) work, with template variables written as usual (`$page.foo`).

- `{{ if $var }}` with a single variable checks `isset($var) && $var`: it is false when the variable is not set.
- In any other expression, a variable that is not set reads as `null` without a warning, so `{{ if $page.count > 0 }}` is safe when `count` is missing.
- `{{ elseif }}` (without a space) is accepted as well as `{{ else if }}`.
- `{{ end }}` closes the block. `{{ else }}` or `{{ else if }}` outside an `if`, a missing `{{ end }}` or an extra one is an error reported with its line (see [Lint and errors](../lint.mdx)).

## Example

```html title="Template"
{{ if $page.foo === 'bar' }}
    foo is bar
{{ else if $page.foo === 'baz' }}
    foo is baz
{{ else }}
    foo is neither bar nor baz
{{ end }}
```

### Checking whether a value is set

```html title="Template"
{{ if $page.image_id }}
<img src="{{ $page.image_id|getImageUrl }}" alt="">
{{ end }}

{{ if !isset($page.data) || count($page.data) === 0 }}
<p>No news yet.</p>
{{ end }}
```

### Using a function

```html title="Template"
{{ if isNew($post.post_date) }}<span class="c-label">NEW</span>{{ end }}
```

`isNew()` is defined by the skeleton in `htdocs/_fegg/site/functions/modifier.php`. Functions defined there can be called in any expression.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/loop ---


# Loop

> foreach, loop

## Description

### foreach

```html
{{ foreach $array as $key => $value }}
{{ end foreach }}

{{ foreach $array as $value }}
{{ end }}
```

Repeats the block for every element of an array, like PHP's `foreach`. The key is optional. The block is closed by `{{ end foreach }}` or simply `{{ end }}`.

- When the array is not set, the block is skipped without a warning.
- `$foreachIndex` counts the iterations from 0. In nested loops, the inner loop has its own `$foreachIndex` and the outer value is restored when the inner loop ends. After a loop, `$foreachIndex` holds the number of iterations.
- The key and value variables live in the same namespace as `$page`, `$site` …: `{{ foreach $list as $page }}` overwrites `$page`. Pick other names.

### loop

```html
{{ loop $i = 1 to 10 }}
{{ end }}
```

Counts from the first bound to the second, both included: `{{ loop $i = 1 to 10 }}` is `for ($i = 1; $i <= 10; $i++)`. Each bound is a number or a variable (`$page.pager.last_page`), not an expression. A bound that is not set reads as `null`. Close the block with `{{ end }}`.

## Example

### foreach

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['list'] = array('foo' => 'bar', 'baz' => 'qux');
```

```html title="Template"
<ul>
    {{ foreach $page.list as $key => $value }}
    <li>{{ $foreachIndex }} : {{ $key }} => {{ $value }}</li>
    {{ end foreach }}
</ul>
```

```html title="Output"
<ul>
    <li>0 : foo => bar</li>
    <li>1 : baz => qux</li>
</ul>
```

### Nested foreach

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['rows'] = array(array('a', 'b'), array('c', 'd', 'e'));
```

```html title="Template"
{{ foreach $page.rows as $row }}[{{ foreach $row as $cell }}{{ $cell }}{{ end }}:{{ $foreachIndex }}]{{ end }}
```

```html title="Output"
[ab:0][cde:1]
```

### loop

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['max'] = 5;
```

```html title="Template"
<ul>
    {{ loop $i = 1 to $page.max }}
    <li>{{ $i }}</li>
    {{ end }}
</ul>
```

```html title="Output"
<ul>
    <li>1</li>
    <li>2</li>
    <li>3</li>
    <li>4</li>
    <li>5</li>
</ul>
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/include ---


# Include

> include, include head, head, include foot, foot, include html

## Description

```html
{{ include '/path/to/template' }}
```

Renders another template at this place, with the same variables. Variables the included template assigns do not come back to the including one.

```html
{{ include head '/path/to/template' }}
{{ head }}

{{ include foot '/path/to/template' }}
{{ foot }}
```

`{{ include head }}` and `{{ include foot }}` do not output anything where they are written. They queue a template that is rendered later where the layout writes `{{ head }}` (in `<head>`) or `{{ foot }}` (before `</body>`). A page uses them to add its own CSS or JavaScript to the shared layout. When several templates queue partials (a page and the layouts it transcludes), the partials of the outer layouts come first.

```html
{{ include html '/data/html/banner.html' }}
```

`{{ include html }}` includes a file under the document root (`htdocs/`) with PHP's `include`. The path starts with `/` and has its extension. It is not a template: `{{ }}` tags in it are not compiled.

### Paths

- Template paths have no `.tpl` extension.
- A path starting with `/` is relative to the template root (`site/template/`, then `core/template/`). **Prefer it**: it works the same from any directory.
- A path without `/` is relative to the directory of the template that contains the tag (`./parts/card`, `../include/head/news`). For `include head` / `include foot`, it stays relative to the template that queued it.
- A missing target throws `Fegg\Template\TemplateException` (HTTP 500). `./fegg-cli template:lint` reports missing targets before you deploy (see [Lint and errors](../lint.mdx)).

## Example

### include

```html title="site/template/parts/card.tpl"
<div class="c-card">{{ $page.title }}</div>
```

```html title="site/template/news/detail.tpl"
<p>Before</p>
{{ include '/parts/card' }}
```

```html title="Output"
<p>Before</p>
<div class="c-card">Hello</div>
```

### include head / include foot

```html title="site/template/include/head/about.tpl"
<link rel="stylesheet" href="{{ base }}/data/css/about.css">
```

```html title="site/template/include/foot/about.tpl"
<script src="{{ base }}/data/js/about.js"></script>
```

```html title="site/template/about.tpl"
{{ transclude '/template/siteframe' }}

{{ include head '/include/head/about' }}
{{ include foot '/include/foot/about' }}

{{ section contents }}
<p>About us</p>
{{ end section contents }}
```

```html title="site/template/template/siteframe.tpl"
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="ja">
<head>
    <meta charset="UTF-8">
    <title>{{ $site.title }}</title>
    {{ head }}
</head>
<body>
    {{ section contents }}
    {{ end section contents }}
    {{ foot }}
</body>
</html>
```

```html title="Output"
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="ja">
<head>
    <meta charset="UTF-8">
    <title>Example Inc.</title>
    <link rel="stylesheet" href="/data/css/about.css">
</head>
<body>
    <p>About us</p>
    <script src="/data/js/about.js"></script>
</body>
</html>
```

:::note

Write `{{ include head }}` and `{{ include foot }}` at the top level of the template, not inside a `{{ section }}`: a section runs as a function, so a partial queued inside it is lost.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/transclude ---


# Transclude

> transclude, section

## Description

```html
{{ transclude '/path/to/layout' }}

{{ section name }}
{{ end section name }}
```

`{{ transclude }}` makes the template inherit a layout (the parent template). The parent declares named `{{ section }}` blocks; the child fills them, and the parent prints each one where it is declared.

- In the parent, a section is a placeholder. Its content is the default, printed when the child does not define the section.
- In the child, a section defines the content. The section name must match the parent's.
- Inheritance works through any number of levels: a child can fill a section declared by its parent or by any layout further up.
- A template has one `{{ transclude }}`. Paths follow the [include rules](./include.mdx#paths): prefer `/`-rooted paths such as `'/template/siteframe'`. A missing parent throws `Fegg\Template\TemplateException` with the line of the tag.
- Close a section with `{{ end section name }}` (recommended) or `{{ end section }}`.

Things to know:

- Text outside any `{{ section }}` in a child template is output **before** the parent layout. Keep everything inside sections (`fegg-cli template:lint` warns about stray text).
- A section runs as a function. `{{ assign }}`, `{{ include head }}` and `{{ include foot }}` written inside it only affect that section. Put them at the top level of the template, next to `{{ transclude }}`.
- A section the parent does not declare is printed where the child defines it.

## Example

```html title="site/template/template/siteframe.tpl"
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="ja">
<head>
    <meta charset="UTF-8">
    <title>{{ $site.title }}</title>
</head>
<body>
    <main>
        {{ section contents }}
        <p>Default content</p>
        {{ end section contents }}
    </main>
</body>
</html>
```

```html title="site/template/index.tpl"
{{ transclude '/template/siteframe' }}

{{ section contents }}
<p>This is the index content.</p>
{{ end section contents }}
```

```html title="Output"
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="ja">
<head>
    <meta charset="UTF-8">
    <title>Example Inc.</title>
</head>
<body>
    <main>
        <p>This is the index content.</p>
    </main>
</body>
</html>
```

### Multiple levels

```html title="site/template/base.tpl"
<html>
<body>
    {{ section header }}<header>Default header</header>{{ end section header }}
    {{ section body }}{{ end section body }}
</body>
</html>
```

```html title="site/template/layout/two_columns.tpl"
{{ transclude '/base' }}

{{ section body }}
<div class="l-columns">
    <aside>Menu</aside>
    <div class="l-columns__main">
        {{ section main }}{{ end section main }}
    </div>
</div>
{{ end section body }}
```

```html title="site/template/news/detail.tpl"
{{ transclude '/layout/two_columns' }}

{{ section header }}<header>News</header>{{ end section header }}

{{ section main }}
<p>This is the news content.</p>
{{ end section main }}
```

```html title="Output (whitespace simplified)"
<html>
<body>
    <header>News</header>
    <div class="l-columns">
        <aside>Menu</aside>
        <div class="l-columns__main">
            <p>This is the news content.</p>
        </div>
    </div>
</body>
</html>
```

`news/detail` fills `header`, which is declared two levels up in `base`, and `main`, which is declared by its direct parent.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/url ---


# Base URL

> base, base_url, current_url

## Description

```html
{{ base }}
{{ base_url }}
{{ current_url }}
```

| Tag | Output | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `{{ base }}` | URL path of the site root, without a trailing slash. Empty when the site is at the document root | (empty), `/sub` |
| `{{ base_url }}` | Scheme, host (and port) plus `{{ base }}`: the absolute URL of the site root | `https://example.com`, `https://example.com/sub` |
| `{{ current_url }}` | Absolute URL of the current request, including the query string | `https://example.com/news/?p=2` |

All three are HTML-escaped.

Start every link and asset path with `{{ base }}/`, so the site keeps working when it is installed in a subdirectory. Use `{{ base_url }}` and `{{ current_url }}` where an absolute URL is required (canonical, OGP).

## Example

```html title="Template"
<link rel="stylesheet" href="{{ base }}/data/css/style.css">
<a href="{{ base }}/news/">News</a>
<img src="{{ base }}/data/img/common/pc/logo.png" alt="">

<link rel="canonical" href="{{ current_url }}">
<meta property="og:image" content="{{ base_url }}/data/img/ogp.png">
```

```html title="Output (site at https://example.com/, page /news/?p=2)"
<link rel="stylesheet" href="/data/css/style.css">
<a href="/news/">News</a>
<img src="/data/img/common/pc/logo.png" alt="">

<link rel="canonical" href="https://example.com/news/?p=2">
<meta property="og:image" content="https://example.com/data/img/ogp.png">
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/checked_selected ---


# Checked/Selected

> checked, selected

## Description

```html
{{ checked key=$optionValue value=$currentValue }}
{{ selected key=$optionValue value=$currentValue }}
{{ checked key=1 value=$currentValue typecheck=true }}
```

Prints ` checked` (or ` selected`, with a leading space) when the current value matches the value of this input or option:

- `key` is the value of this `<input>` / `<option>`: a variable, a number or a quoted string.
- `value` is the current value, usually from the request (`$in.category`) or the stored data (`$page.data[$id]`).
- It matches when `value == key`, or when `value` is an array that contains `key` (multiple checkboxes, multiple select).
- Add `typecheck=true` to compare with `===` (and a strict `in_array`).
- Nothing is printed when `value` is not set.

Put the tag directly after the other attributes, without a space before it: the output starts with its own space.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['current'] = 'value';
```

```html title="Template"
<label><input type="radio" name="radio" value="value"{{ checked key='value' value=$page.current }}> value</label>
<label><input type="radio" name="radio" value="other"{{ checked key='other' value=$page.current }}> other</label>

<select name="select">
    <option value="value"{{ selected key='value' value=$page.current }}>value</option>
    <option value="other"{{ selected key='other' value=$page.current }}>other</option>
</select>
```

```html title="Output"
<label><input type="radio" name="radio" value="value" checked> value</label>
<label><input type="radio" name="radio" value="other"> other</label>

<select name="select">
    <option value="value" selected>value</option>
    <option value="other">other</option>
</select>
```

### Multiple checkboxes

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['data']['colors'] = array('red', 'blue');
$this->page['colors'] = array('red', 'green', 'blue');
```

```html title="Template"
{{ foreach $page.colors as $color }}
<label><input type="checkbox" name="colors[]" value="{{ $color }}"{{ checked key=$color value=$page.data.colors }}> {{ $color }}</label>
{{ end foreach }}
```

```html title="Output"
<label><input type="checkbox" name="colors[]" value="red" checked> red</label>
<label><input type="checkbox" name="colors[]" value="green"> green</label>
<label><input type="checkbox" name="colors[]" value="blue" checked> blue</label>
```

See [Options](./options.mdx) to build all the `<option>` tags of a `<select>` from an array.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/options ---


# Options

> Make option tags

## Description

```html
{{ options source=$list selected=$currentValue }}
```

Prints one `<option>` for each element of `source`: the array key is the `value` attribute and the array value is the label. Both are HTML-escaped.

The option whose key equals `selected` (compared as strings) gets the `selected` attribute. `selected` is optional. Nothing is printed when `source` is empty.

Write the arguments without spaces inside them (`source=$page.categories`, not an expression). For a multiple select, use [checked/selected](./checked_selected.mdx) in a `foreach` instead.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$this->page['categories'] = array(
    '1' => 'News',
    '2' => 'Events & Seminars',
);
```

```html title="Template"
<select name="category">
    <option value="">All</option>
    {{ options source=$page.categories selected=$in.category }}
</select>
```

```html title="Output (request ?category=2)"
<select name="category">
    <option value="">All</option>
    <option value='1'>News</option><option value='2' selected>Events &amp; Seminars</option>
</select>
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/hidden ---


# Hidden

> Output hidden inputs

## Description

```html
{{ hidden }}
```

Prints an `<input type="hidden">` for every value set with `$this->setHidden()` in the controller. Names and values are HTML-escaped. Array values become one input per element (`tags[0]`, `tags[1]` …).

Put it inside the `<form>`. It is typically used to carry an ID or a one-time ticket from the input screen to the confirmation and save steps.

See also [Application::setHidden](../helpers/sethidden.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$this->setHidden('internal_id', 12);
```

```html title="Template"
<form method="post" action="{{ base }}/contact/confirm/">
    {{ hidden }}
    <button type="submit">Send</button>
</form>
```

```html title="Output"
<form method="post" action="/contact/confirm/">
    <input type="hidden" name="internal_id" value="12">
    <button type="submit">Send</button>
</form>
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/code ---


# Code

> Run a PHP statement

## Description

```html
{{ code statement }}
```

Runs PHP statements in the template. Nothing is output unless the statement itself prints something.

- Template variables are written as usual (`$page.data`, `$categories`). A variable assigned in `{{ code }}` can be used by the following tags.
- Several statements can be separated with `;`. The code cannot contain `{` or `}`, so blocks such as `if (...) { }` are not possible: use `{{ if }}` and `{{ foreach }}` instead.
- Unlike `{{ if }}` and `{{ assign }}`, the code is not null-safe: on PHP 8, reading a variable that is not set raises a warning, which Fegg's error handler turns into an exception (HTTP 500).

Keep logic in the controller. `{{ code }}` is for small things such as calling a helper function; for a value, prefer [`{{ assign }}`](./assign.mdx).

## Example

```html title="Template"
{{ code $categories = feggCategories('news') }}
<select name="category">
    {{ foreach $categories as $key => $label }}
    <option value="{{ $key }}"{{ selected key=$key value=$in.category }}>{{ $label }}</option>
    {{ end foreach }}
</select>
```

`feggCategories()` returns the list `news` of `site/config/category_info.php`.

```html title="Template"
<a href="{{ $site.prefix }}?p={{ $page.pager.next_page }}&{{ code echoSearchQuery() }}">Next</a>
```

`echoSearchQuery()` prints the current request parameters (without `p`) as a query string.

:::warning

Templates are code: `{{ code }}` runs any PHP. Only developers may edit templates. See [Security](../security.mdx).

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/call ---


# Call

> Run a controller method

## Description

```html
{{ call 'controller/method' }}
```

Creates a new instance of a controller and runs one of its methods at this place. Whatever the method prints is part of the page.

- The path is written in single quotes. The first segments name the controller, like a URL: `news` is `Site\Controller\News`, `cms/news` is `Site\Controller\Cms\News` (then `Fegg\Controller\...` of the core).
- The next segment is the method; without it, `index()` is called.
- Further segments are ignored: the method is called **without arguments**.
- The controller is resolved when the template is compiled. When no controller matches, the tag is left in the page as text and `fegg-cli template:lint` reports it as an unknown tag.
- Only the constructor runs before the method, not `__init()`. Have the method print a fragment (for example `echo $this->fetchTemplate(...)`). Do not call `displayPage()`: it ends the request.

## Example

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Banner.php"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

class Banner extends Controller
{
    public function latest()
    {
        echo $this->fetchTemplate('parts/banner', array(
            'text' => 'Campaign now on',
        ));
    }
}
```

```html title="htdocs/_fegg/site/template/parts/banner.tpl"
<p class="c-banner">{{ $text }}</p>
```

```html title="Template"
{{ call 'banner/latest' }}
```

```html title="Output"
<p class="c-banner">Campaign now on</p>
```

:::tip

To share a piece of HTML, `{{ include }}` is simpler. Load data in the page's own controller when you can, and keep `{{ call }}` for fragments that need their own controller code.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/comment ---


# Comment

> Template comments

## Description

```html
{{* comment *}}
```

Everything between `{{*` and `*}}` is removed when the template is compiled: it never reaches the browser, unlike an HTML comment. A comment may span several lines, and tags inside it are ignored, so it can also disable a part of the template.

The newlines of a comment are kept, so errors and `fegg-cli template:lint` still report the right `.tpl` line after a multi-line comment.

## Example

```html title="Template"
<p>Visible</p>
{{* TODO: show the campaign banner from April *}}
{{*
{{ if $page.campaign }}
<p>{{ $page.campaign.title }}</p>
{{ end }}
*}}
<p>Also visible</p>
```

```html title="Output"
<p>Visible</p>





<p>Also visible</p>
```

The lines of the comments remain as empty lines.

:::note

`<!-- ... -->` is an ordinary HTML comment: it is sent to the browser, and the tags inside it still run.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/basic/templates/rules/literal_braces ---


# Literal braces

> Output `{{` and `}}` as text

## Description

```html
{-{ ... }-}
{--{ ... }--}
```

Fegg treats every `{{ ... }}` as a tag. To output literal double braces, for example a Vue or Handlebars template inside the page, or copy that contains them, write `{-{` for `{{` and `}-}` for `}}`. The text between them is not compiled.

`{-{` also removes the spaces directly before it, and `}-}` the spaces directly after it (`a {-{ x }-} b` outputs `a{{ x }}b`). `{--{` and `}--}` work the same but remove all whitespace there, newlines included.

Single braces (`{`, `}`) in HTML, CSS and JavaScript need nothing special.

## Example

```html title="Template"
<div id="app">
    <p>{-{ message }-}</p>
</div>
<script>
    Vue.createApp({ data() { return { message: 'Hello' }; } }).mount('#app');
</script>
```

```html title="Output"
<div id="app">
    <p>{{ message }}</p>
</div>
<script>
    Vue.createApp({ data() { return { message: 'Hello' }; } }).mount('#app');
</script>
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/first/cont ---


# Directory structure

Fegg 5 separates, by directory, the **core** shared by every project from the **site** that belongs to one project.

There is only one rule: **do not edit `htdocs/_fegg/core/` or `htdocs/cms/`.** `fegg-cli core:update` replaces them. If you edit them, `fegg-cli core:status` lists the changed files and `core:update` stops. Anything you want to change goes on the `site/` side (see [Overriding the core](#overriding-the-core)).

```yaml
# Project root (does not need to be uploaded to the server)
fegg-cli                  # project: CLI entry point (path definitions only)
docs/ tests/ package.json composer.json ...

htdocs/                   # document root
    index.php             # project: web entry point (path definitions only)
    .htaccess             # project: template is _fegg/core/dist/htaccess.dist
    cms/                  # core: CMS admin assets (css/ is built by npm run build:scss)
    data/                 # project: site assets (css, js, images)
    up_dir/               # project: files uploaded from the CMS

    _fegg/                # never readable from the web
        .htaccess         # core: deny all
        core/             # core: DO NOT EDIT (replaced on update)
            src/          # Fegg\             Application, Model, Database\Pdo, Validation, Cms\Helper, Tool\* ...
            controller/   # Fegg\Controller\  Controller (base class), Cms\Login, Cms\Media, Cms\Image ...
            template/cms/ # CMS foundation templates
            functions/    # template modifiers (feggDate, getImageUrl, ...)
            config/       # default settings, YAML for the core tables (cms_user, cms_file, cms_image)
            bin/          # fegg-cli commands
            bootstrap/    # web and CLI start-up, autoloader
            vendor/       # PHPMailer, Spyc
            dist/         # templates for the project-owned entry files
            VERSION  manifest.json  UPGRADE.md

        site/             # project: edit freely (never touched by updates)
            src/          # Site\             project classes (create it when you need it)
            controller/   # Site\Controller\  Controller (project base class), Index, News, Cms\News ...
            template/     # site templates (.tpl), CMS templates for post types
            functions/    # project template modifiers (loaded automatically)
            config/       # settings.php, env.php (per server, not committed), route.php, database_config.php, site_info.php, cms_menu.php ...
                database/ # YAML table definitions for the project's tables
            data/         # db/, log/, cache/, core-backup/ (not tracked by git)
```

The skeleton's sample code (`site/controller/Index.php`, `News.php`, `Contact.php`, `Cms/News.php`, ...) is project code. Change or delete it as the project needs.

## Namespaces

Classes are loaded by the core's own autoloader (`core/bootstrap/autoload.php`). Composer is not required; in the skeleton it is only used for the test suite.

| Namespace          | Directory          |
| ------------------ | ------------------ |
| `Fegg\`            | `core/src/`        |
| `Fegg\Controller\` | `core/controller/` |
| `Fegg\Command\`    | `core/bin/`        |
| `Site\`            | `site/src/`        |
| `Site\Controller\` | `site/controller/` |

Sub directories are namespaces: `site/src/Shop/Cart.php` is `Site\Shop\Cart`, `site/controller/Cms/News.php` is `Site\Controller\Cms\News`.

Every PHP file under `htdocs/_fegg/` must start with `defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;`. It stops the file from doing anything when it is requested directly from a URL.

## Overriding the core

The site is searched first, then the core. This works like a WordPress child theme.

| What          | How                                                                                         | Example                                                                     |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Templates     | Put a file at the same path under `site/template/`                                          | `site/template/cms/login.tpl` is used instead of `core/template/cms/login.tpl` |
| Config files  | Put a file with the same name in `site/config/`                                             | `site/config/validation_error_message.php` replaces the core's messages     |
| Settings      | Write only the keys you want to change in `site/config/settings.php`                        | `$settings['developer_ip'] = array(...);`                                   |
| CMS screens   | Put a class that extends the core controller in `site/controller/`, with only the methods you change | See [Controller](../basic/controller.mdx#extending-a-core-controller) |
| CMS menu      | Add one block to `site/config/cms_menu.php`                                                 | When you add a post type                                                    |
| DB tables     | Put a YAML file in `site/config/database/` (a file with the same name wins over the core's) | `cms_news.yaml`                                                             |

## Entry files and layout

`htdocs/index.php` and `fegg-cli` belong to the project. They only define three paths and then load the core:

```php title="htdocs/index.php"
<?php
define('FEGG_HTML_DIR', __DIR__);                 // document root
define('FEGG_CORE_DIR', __DIR__ . '/_fegg/core'); // core
define('FEGG_SITE_DIR', __DIR__ . '/_fegg/site'); // site

require FEGG_CORE_DIR . '/bootstrap/web.php';
```

`fegg-cli` defines the same three constants and requires `core/bootstrap/cli.php`. Always keep the paths in the two files consistent. Templates for both are in `core/dist/`.

| Layout                                                             | Entry file                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Standard: `htdocs/_fegg/`                                          | `core/dist/index.php.dist`                                                   |
| `_fegg/` placed next to the document root (outside of it)          | `core/dist/index.outside-docroot.php.dist`                                   |
| The document root has a different name (`public_html`, etc.)       | Keep the standard one. Only fix `FEGG_HTML_DIR` in `fegg-cli`                |
| Installed in a subdirectory (`https://example.com/sub/`)           | Keep the standard one. The URL base is detected from `SCRIPT_NAME`           |

## Protecting `_fegg/`

In the standard layout `_fegg/` is inside the document root. It is protected by several layers, so that one missing layer is caught by the next:

1. `htdocs/_fegg/.htaccess` denies all requests.
2. `htdocs/.htaccess` returns 403 for `/_fegg/`, dotfiles and hidden directories (`.git/`; `.well-known/` stays reachable), and denies `.db`, `.sqlite`, `.yaml`, `.tpl`, `.log`, `.md`, `.json` and `.dist` files.
3. Every PHP file under `_fegg/` and every compiled template starts with `defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;`.
4. `fegg-cli doctor https://example.com` checks over HTTP that nothing under `_fegg/` and no database file can be read (see [fegg-cli](./cli.mdx#doctor)). The CMS dashboard also warns about problems it can detect from the files, such as a missing `.htaccess`.
5. If the server allows it, put the SQLite database outside the document root (see [Settings](./config.mdx#database)).

On nginx, where `.htaccess` is not used, add equivalent rules:

```nginx
location ~ (^|/)_fegg(/|$) { deny all; }
location ~ /\.(?!well-known/) { deny all; }
location ~* ^/up_dir/.*\.(php\d?|phtml|phar|pht|cgi|pl|py|sh)$ { deny all; }
location ~* \.(tpl|db|sqlite|ya?ml|log|md|json|dist)$ { deny all; }
```

:::caution

Many FTP clients do not transfer dotfiles (`.htaccess`) by default. After uploading, always run `./fegg-cli doctor <URL>` from your local machine.

:::


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/first/config ---


# Settings

## Basic settings

The defaults are in `htdocs/_fegg/core/config/settings.php`. Do not edit that file (it is replaced on update). Put the values for your project in **`htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php`**.

The core file is loaded first, then the site file. Only the keys the site file sets are changed, so write one key per line and never reassign the whole array (`$settings = array(...)` would drop the defaults).

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

// Run mode  1: running, empty: under maintenance
$settings['run_mode'] = '1';

// Maintenance page URL
$settings['maintenance_page_url'] = '';

// Developer IP addresses (multiple allowed). Error details only outside production (FEGG_ENV)
$settings['developer_ip'] = array('192.168.65.1', '203.0.113.10');

// Basic authentication scope (0: none, 1: whole site, 2: partial)
#$settings['basic_auth_scope'] = 0;
#$settings['basic_auth_user'] = '';
#$settings['basic_auth_password'] = '';

// HSTS, once the whole site is served over HTTPS
#$settings['hsts'] = 'max-age=31536000';
```

Per-server values and secrets (the environment, database passwords, API keys) do not belong here: see [Environments and secrets](#environments-and-secrets).

| Key                    | Default                         | Remarks |
| :--------------------- | :------------------------------ | :------ |
| `run_mode`             | `'1'`                           | `'1'`: running. Empty: under maintenance. During maintenance every request that does not come from `developer_ip` is redirected to `maintenance_page_url`, or gets a short maintenance message when it is empty. |
| `maintenance_page_url` | `''`                            | Redirect target during maintenance |
| `developer_ip`         | `array()`                       | Addresses that can see error details outside production (see [Developer mode](#developer-mode)) and that bypass maintenance |
| `timezone`             | `''`                            | Time zone of the application, set once at startup. `''`: `FEGG_DEFAULT_TIMEZONE` (`Asia/Tokyo`) |
| `log_level`            | `''`                            | Lowest level written to the application log (`debug`, `info`, `notice`, `warning`, `error` ...). `''`: `warning` in production, `debug` elsewhere. See [Logging and errors](../basic/logging.mdx) |
| `log_retention_days`   | `30`                            | Days to keep the application log files (`0`: keep all) |
| `mail`                 | `array('transport' => 'sendmail')` | Outgoing mail of `Fegg\Tool\Mail`. For SMTP see [Mail](../library/mail/index.mdx#smtp) |
| `security_headers`     | `array()`                       | Changes the default security headers: `'Header-Name' => 'value'`, `''` removes one. See [Security](../basic/security.mdx#security-headers) |
| `hsts`                 | `''`                            | `Strict-Transport-Security` value sent on HTTPS responses, e.g. `'max-age=31536000'`. `''`: not sent |
| `csp`                  | `''`                            | `Content-Security-Policy` value. `''`: not sent |
| `csp_report_only`      | `false`                         | `true`: send the policy as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` (reports only, blocks nothing) |
| `default_language`     | not set                         | Language code used when none is detected, e.g. `'ja'`. Only for multilingual sites |
| `support_language`     | not set                         | Supported language codes, e.g. `array('ja', 'en')`. Only for multilingual sites |
| `basic_auth_scope`     | `0`                             | `0`: no Basic authentication. `1`: every request handled by Fegg, checked before the controller runs. `2`: only where the controller calls `$this->basicAuth()` |
| `basic_auth_user`      | `''`                            | Basic authentication user |
| `basic_auth_password`  | `''`                            | Basic authentication password (compared with `hash_equals()`) |
| `basic_auth_message`   | not set                         | Realm shown in the browser's login dialog |
| `basic_auth_error_message` | not set                     | Text sent with the 401 response when the login fails (default `Authorization Required`) |
| `template_dir`         | `site/template`, `core/template` | Template directories, searched in this order |
| `template_ext`         | `'tpl'`                         | Template file extension |
| `template_cache_dir`   | `site/data/cache/template`      | Compiled template cache |
| `global_config_dir`    | `''`                            | Optional directory searched by `loadConfig()` before `site/config/`. End it with `/` |

Read a setting from a controller with [`$this->getSetting()`](../basic/others/config/getsetting.mdx).

## Environments and secrets

Values that differ per server (the environment, the database, API keys, passwords) are **not** written in `settings.php` or any other committed file. They are read with `feggEnv()`:

```php
$secret = feggEnv('FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SECRET', '');   // feggEnv(string $name, mixed $default = null)
```

`feggEnv()` returns the first of:

1. the environment variable with that name (`SetEnv` in Apache, `fastcgi_param` in nginx, Docker `environment` ...),
2. the key in **`htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env.php`**,
3. the key in the committed environment file **`htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env/<name>.php`** chosen for this server by its path (see [Test servers deployed with git pull](#test-servers-deployed-with-git-pull)),
4. `$default`.

`SetEnv` reaches only the web. `fegg-cli` (and a `fegg-cli migration` run after a deploy) sees only its own shell's environment and the files, so keep database settings in a file when the server runs `fegg-cli`.

`env.php` returns a PHP array. It is in `.gitignore` and is created on each server by copying `site/config/env.php.dist`:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

return array(
    // production | staging | development
    'FEGG_ENV' => 'development',

    // Database. Default: SQLite file in site/data/db/db.db
    // 'FEGG_DB_DSN'      => 'mysql:host=127.0.0.1;port=3306;dbname=example;charset=utf8mb4',
    // 'FEGG_DB_USER'     => '',
    // 'FEGG_DB_PASSWORD' => '',

    // Contact form spam protection
    'FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SITE_KEY' => '',
    'FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SECRET'   => '',
    'FEGG_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY' => '',
    'FEGG_TURNSTILE_SECRET'   => '',
);
```

| Name | Used for |
| :--- | :------- |
| `FEGG_ENV` | `production` (default, also for unknown values), `staging` or `development`. Available as the constant `FEGG_ENV` |
| `FEGG_DB_DSN`, `FEGG_DB_USER`, `FEGG_DB_PASSWORD`, `FEGG_DB_SLAVE_DSN` | Database connection (see [Database](#database)) |
| `FEGG_DB_PATH` | SQLite file path, when `FEGG_DB_DSN` is not set |
| `FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SITE_KEY`, `FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SECRET`, `FEGG_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`, `FEGG_TURNSTILE_SECRET` | Contact form spam protection (see [Captcha](../library/captcha/index.mdx)) |
| `FEGG_SMTP_PASSWORD` (`FEGG_SMTP_HOST`, `FEGG_SMTP_USERNAME`) | SMTP sending (see [Mail](../library/mail/index.mdx#smtp)) |
| `FEGG_LOG_DIR` | Directory of the application log instead of `site/data/log` |
| `FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SCORE_THRESHOLD` | reCAPTCHA score needed (default `0.5`; `1.0` rejects every form, to check the error message) |
| `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_LOGIN`, `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_NAME`, `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_PASSWORD_HASH` (or `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_PASSWORD`) | First CMS user, created by [`fegg-cli migration`](./cli.mdx#migration) |
| `FEGG_ENV_NAME` | Environment variable only: chooses `site/config/env/<name>.php` instead of `paths.php` |

Add your own keys the same way and read them with `feggEnv('NAME', $default)`.

:::warning

**A site without `env.php` and without a matching environment file runs as production.** On your machine, create `env.php` with `'FEGG_ENV' => 'development'`. Otherwise developers do not see error details there. Test servers on the LionHeart develop server run as `staging` without any file of their own (next section).

:::

`FEGG_ENV` decides:

- whether [developer mode](#developer-mode) is possible (not in production),
- whether the contact form sends the administrator mail to the test address (outside production),
- the default `log_level` (`warning` in production, `debug` elsewhere),
- the Fegg 4 constant `FEGG_TEST_ENVIRONMENT`, which is `true` outside production. It no longer looks at the host name.

### Test servers deployed with git pull

On the LionHeart develop server (`http://p83.<project>.develop.lionheart.co.jp/`, project directory `/var/www/vhosts/<project>/p83`) a deploy is `git pull` followed by `fegg-cli migration`. Nothing can be placed on the server by hand, so the test server's settings are committed:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env/paths.php"
return array(
    // project root pattern (fnmatch; * and ? do not cross /) => environment file name
    '/var/www/vhosts/*/p[0-9]*' => 'staging',
);
```

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env/staging.php"
return array(
    'FEGG_ENV' => 'staging',
    // first CMS user, created by fegg-cli migration (see below)
    'FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_LOGIN'         => 'admin',
    'FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_PASSWORD_HASH' => '$2y$10$...',   // ./fegg-cli user:hash
    // Turnstile test keys: always pass (2x00000000000000000000AB / 2x0000000000000000000000000000000AA always fail)
    'FEGG_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY' => '1x00000000000000000000AA',
    'FEGG_TURNSTILE_SECRET'   => '1x0000000000000000000000000000000AA',
);
```

Both files come with the skeleton. The project root (the directory of `fegg-cli`) is matched against `paths.php`; the first match chooses `site/config/env/<name>.php`. Any other path (production, your machine) does not match and behaves as before.

- **Why the path:** the host name of a request is chosen by the client. Matching it would let a request to production with `Host: x.develop.lionheart.co.jp` switch production to the test settings (test captcha keys that always pass, the test administrator). The path is set by the server, and `fegg-cli` sees the same path as the web.
- **Check it:** the first line of `fegg-cli migration` in the deploy log names the environment and its file, e.g. `Environment: staging (site/config/env/staging.php, /var/www/vhosts/*/p[0-9]*) / DB: sqlite:...`.
- **First CMS user:** `fegg-cli migration` creates `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_LOGIN` only while `cms_user` has no users at all. It never changes an existing user, so it is safe on every deploy: a password changed in the CMS stays, and once any user exists, a deleted initial user does not come back. Commit the hash (`./fegg-cli user:hash`), not the password, and change the password after the first login.
- **Captcha:** the Turnstile test keys need no host name registration. reCAPTCHA v3 has no test keys: use a key registered for `develop.lionheart.co.jp`. `'FEGG_RECAPTCHA_SCORE_THRESHOLD' => '1.0'` shows the rejection.

:::warning

Everyone who can read the repository can read these files. Put only values made for the test server there, never production keys or passwords. `env.php` on a server and environment variables still win over them.

:::

### Developer mode

Fegg runs in developer mode (the constant `FEGG_DEVELOPER` is `'1'`) only when **both** are true:

- the request comes from an address in `developer_ip`, and
- `FEGG_ENV` is not `production`.

In developer mode:

- PHP errors are displayed.
- An uncaught exception shows the message, the file and line, the template line (`Template: <file>.tpl:<line>`), the stack trace and `$this->page`. Other visitors get the error page (see [Logging and errors](../basic/logging.mdx)). In both cases the error is written to the PHP error log and the application log.
- The 404 page shows which controller file Fegg tried to load.

On a production server developers see the same error page as everyone; the details are in the logs. `developer_ip` alone is not a security boundary: behind a proxy or load balancer every request may come from the same address.

Requests from `developer_ip` also reach the site during maintenance (`run_mode` empty), in every environment.

## Database

The connection is set in **`htdocs/_fegg/site/config/database_config.php`**. The skeleton's file reads it from `feggEnv()`, so the file itself is the same on every server. Without settings, it is the SQLite file `htdocs/_fegg/site/data/db/db.db`.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env.php (MySQL)"
'FEGG_DB_DSN'      => 'mysql:host=127.0.0.1;port=3306;dbname=example;charset=utf8mb4',
'FEGG_DB_USER'     => 'example',
'FEGG_DB_PASSWORD' => '...',
```

| Name | Remarks |
| :--- | :------ |
| `FEGG_DB_DSN` | PDO DSN. Default: `sqlite:` + `FEGG_DB_PATH` |
| `FEGG_DB_USER`, `FEGG_DB_PASSWORD` | User and password (MySQL) |
| `FEGG_DB_SLAVE_DSN` | Read-only replica. Default: the same as the master |
| `FEGG_DB_PATH` | SQLite file. Default `site/data/db/db.db`. As an environment variable it wins over `FEGG_DB_DSN` in `env.php` (the test suite uses it) |

- `master` is used for writes and `slave` for reads. With one database both point to the same DSN.
- If the server allows it, put the SQLite file **outside the document root** with `FEGG_DB_PATH` (or `FEGG_DB_DSN`).
- MySQL / MariaDB are supported, including `fegg-cli migration`. See [Database configuration](../library/database/configuration.mdx#mysql).

Tables are defined in YAML files (`core/config/database/` for the CMS tables, `site/config/database/` for the project's tables) and created or changed with `./fegg-cli migration` (see [fegg-cli](./cli.mdx#migration)). Every table needs the columns `valid`, `created` and `modified`.

## Other files in `site/config/`

| File                  | Remarks |
| :-------------------- | :------ |
| `route.php`           | URL routing (see [URL](../basic/url.mdx#routing)) |
| `site_info.php`       | Site title, description, OGP image, `picture` breakpoints, ... Loaded by the base controller and available in templates as `$site` |
| `category_info.php`   | Labels for categories and the CMS visibility states. Loaded by the base controller into `$this->config['category_info']` |
| `cms_menu.php`        | CMS side menu. Add one block per post type |
| `env.php`             | Per-server settings and secrets, read with `feggEnv()`. Not committed; copy `env.php.dist` (see [Environments and secrets](#environments-and-secrets)) |
| `database_config.php` | Database connection, from `feggEnv()` (see [Database](#database)) |
| `upload_dir.php`      | Path and URL of the CMS upload directory (`htdocs/up_dir`) |
| `database/*.yaml`     | Table definitions for `fegg-cli migration` |
| `define.php`          | Optional. Project constants, loaded right after the core starts (web and CLI) |
| `fegg_update.php`     | Optional. Repository used by `fegg-cli core:update` (see [Updating the core](./update.mdx)) |

Any other file in `site/config/` can be loaded with [`$this->loadConfig()`](../basic/others/config/loadconfig.mdx). A file with the same name as one in `core/config/` (for example `validation_error_message.php`) replaces the core's file.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/first/cli ---


# fegg-cli

`fegg-cli` is the command line entry point in the project root. Run it from there:

```bash
./fegg-cli <command> [arguments]
```

Like `htdocs/index.php`, the file itself only defines the paths (`FEGG_HTML_DIR`, `FEGG_CORE_DIR`, `FEGG_SITE_DIR`). The commands are in `htdocs/_fegg/core/bin/`. Commands exit with code `1` when they find a problem, so they can be used in CI.

| Command                                         | What it does |
| :---------------------------------------------- | :----------- |
| `migration`                                     | Create or change the tables (SQLite or MySQL) from the YAML schema files |
| `migration:db2schema <file>`                    | Write the structure of the current database to a YAML file |
| `db:schema [table] [--json]`                    | List tables and columns from the YAML schema files |
| `template:lint [file ...]`                      | Check templates without running them |
| `user:create LOGIN [NAME]`                      | Add a CMS user |
| `user:password LOGIN`                           | Set a new password for a CMS user |
| `user:list`                                     | List the CMS users |
| `user:hash`                                     | Print a password hash for `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_PASSWORD_HASH` |
| `core:status`                                   | Show the core version and core files changed in this project |
| `core:update [vX.Y.Z] [--dry-run] [--force]`    | Update the core from the skeleton repository |
| `doctor [URL]`                                  | Check that `_fegg/` cannot be read from the web |

Running a command group without a sub command prints its help: `./fegg-cli core`, `./fegg-cli db`, `./fegg-cli template`, `./fegg-cli user`, `./fegg-cli migration:help`, `./fegg-cli doctor:help`.

## migration

```bash
./fegg-cli migration
```

Compares the table definitions in the YAML files with the database (SQLite, or MySQL / MariaDB) and applies the difference (adds tables, columns and indexes).

- The core's files (`core/config/database/`: `cms_user`, `cms_file`, `cms_image`, `cms_login_attempt`) are read first, then the project's (`site/config/database/`). A project file with the same name replaces the core's file.
- Starts with one line naming the environment, its file and the database, e.g. `Environment: staging (site/config/env/staging.php, /var/www/vhosts/*/p[0-9]*) / DB: sqlite:...` (see [Environments and secrets](./config.mdx#test-servers-deployed-with-git-pull)).
- Prints `Fegg-cli migration diff : Not changed` when the database already matches, `Fegg-cli migration diff : Finished migration` after applying changes.
- Then creates the first CMS user when `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_LOGIN` is set and `cms_user` has no users at all, with `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_PASSWORD_HASH` (or `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_PASSWORD`) and `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_NAME`. An existing user is never changed and a deleted one is not created again, so this is safe on every deploy. Without a usable password it exits with 1 after the migration.
- `fegg-cli core:update` runs it automatically after updating the core.
- On MySQL the YAML types are mapped to MySQL types and changed tables are altered in place. See [Database configuration](../library/database/configuration.mdx#mysql).
- `/cms/migrate/` in the CMS shows the same pending changes and applies them with its "Run migration" button (POST), for servers where you cannot run `fegg-cli`.

:::danger

The YAML files are the single source of truth. A table that exists in the database but in no YAML file is **dropped**, and a column that is not in the YAML is **removed with its data**. Run it against a copy first when you are not sure that every table is defined.

:::

To create a YAML file from an existing database (for example when you start writing schema files for an old project):

```bash
./fegg-cli migration:db2schema htdocs/_fegg/site/config/database/dump.yaml
```

All tables are written to the one file. Split it into one file per table if you keep it.

## db:schema

```bash
./fegg-cli db:schema             # every table
./fegg-cli db:schema cms_news    # one table
./fegg-cli db:schema --json      # as JSON
```

Lists the tables, columns (type, primary key, autoincrement, not null, default) and indexes defined in `core/config/database/` and `site/config/database/`. It reads the YAML files, not the database, so it works without a database. It also warns when a table is missing one of the columns every table needs (`valid`, `created`, `modified`).

```text title="Output"
cms_news  (htdocs/_fegg/site/config/database/cms_news.yaml)
  news_id              INTEGER    primary key, autoincrement, not null
  visible              BOOLEAN
  category             INTEGER
  title                TEXT
  ...
  valid                BOOLEAN
  post_date            DATETIME
  created              DATETIME
  modified             DATETIME
  index NEWS_ID unique (news_id)
```

## template:lint

```bash
./fegg-cli template:lint                                         # every template in site/template and core/template
./fegg-cli template:lint htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl
```

Checks templates without running them and reports each problem with the `.tpl` file and line: unknown tags, unclosed blocks or extra `{{ end }}`, missing `include` / `transclude` targets, undefined modifiers, and output in URL attributes, `<script>` or event attributes that is not escaped for that context.

```text title="Output (example)"
htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:12: unknown modifier "nosuch" (define it in site/functions/*.php)
htdocs/_fegg/site/template/news/detail.tpl:20: {{ $page.data.url }} starts a URL: write {{ $value|feggUrl }} so javascript: URLs are refused
NG: 2 problem(s) in 1 template(s)
```

Prints `OK: <n> template(s)` when there is nothing to fix. Run it after every template change.

## CMS users

```bash
./fegg-cli user:create admin "Site administrator"   # LOGIN [NAME]; NAME defaults to LOGIN
./fegg-cli user:password admin                      # set a new password
./fegg-cli user:list                                # user_id, login and name of every CMS user
```

Manage the CMS users (table `cms_user`). `user:create` and `user:password` ask for the password twice without showing it. It must have at least 8 characters, and it is stored with `password_hash()`.

The password is never an argument (it would stay in the shell history). To pass it from a script, or where there is no terminal, give it on standard input with `--password-stdin`:

```bash
printf '%s\n' "$CMS_PASSWORD" | ./fegg-cli user:create admin --password-stdin
```

`user:create` refuses a login that already exists; use `user:password` for it.

`user:hash` prints `password_hash()` of a password (asked without echo, or `--password-stdin`) for `FEGG_CMS_INITIAL_PASSWORD_HASH` in a committed environment file.

:::note

Fegg 4 created users with the URL `/cms/login/create/<name>/<login>/<password>`. It is gone in Fegg 5. `/cms/register/` still exists in the CMS, but it now needs a CMS login as well as [developer mode](./config.mdx#developer-mode) (a `developer_ip` address on a server that is not production), so on a production server use `fegg-cli`.

:::

## core:status

```bash
./fegg-cli core:status
```

Prints the core version and compares the core files with `core/manifest.json`. `No changes to core files.` means the core is untouched and can be updated. Otherwise it lists the changed files; move those changes to overrides in `site/` (see [Directory structure](./cont.mdx#overriding-the-core)).

## core:update

```bash
./fegg-cli core:update --dry-run    # show the difference only
./fegg-cli core:update              # update to the latest release tag
./fegg-cli core:update v5.1.0       # update to a given version
```

Replaces the core with a release of the skeleton repository. See [Updating the core](./update.mdx) for details.

| Option      | Remarks |
| :---------- | :------ |
| `vX.Y.Z`    | Version to install. The latest tag when omitted |
| `--dry-run` | Show what would change without changing anything |
| `--force`   | Overwrite core files that were changed in the project |

`core:manifest` also exists, but it is for skeleton maintainers only (it rebuilds `core/manifest.json`).

## doctor

```bash
./fegg-cli doctor                        # check the .htaccess files on disk
./fegg-cli doctor https://example.com    # also request the live site
```

Without a URL, it checks the files on disk:

- `htdocs/_fegg/.htaccess` denies access,
- `htdocs/.htaccess` has the rule for `_fegg/`,
- the upload directory `htdocs/up_dir/` has its `.htaccess` that stops uploaded scripts from running (see [Security](../basic/security.mdx#uploads)).

With a URL, it also requests files under `_fegg/` and the SQLite database (when it is under the document root) on the live site and reports any that can be read.

Prints `OK: https://example.com does not expose _fegg/`, or `NG` with the list of problems (exit code `1`). You can run it from your local machine against the production URL, which is the easiest check after an FTP upload.

It also prints `Warning:` lines, without failing, for outdated rules in `htdocs/.htaccess`: a `<IfModule mod_php5.c>` block (ignored by PHP 7 and 8), and Apache 2.2 `Order` / `Deny from` lines (an HTTP 500 on Apache 2.4 without `mod_access_compat`; use `Require all denied`). Compare the file with `core/dist/htaccess.dist`.

## install:class

```bash
./fegg-cli install:class <owner>/<repository>
```

Clones a Fegg library repository (from GitHub, or a full `https://` / `git:` URL) and copies its `src/` directory into `htdocs/_fegg/site/lib/`. This is the Fegg 4 library mechanism: classes in `site/lib/` are only loaded through `getClass()`, which is deprecated in Fegg 5. For new code, put namespaced classes in `site/src/` instead.

## Developer commands in the skeleton

The skeleton repository also has Composer scripts for the test suite and code checks. Composer is only used for development; the application never loads `vendor/`.

| Command                    | What it does |
| :------------------------- | :----------- |
| `composer test`            | PHPUnit: unit and HTTP integration tests (PHP 7.4 to 8.5). `test:unit` and `test:integration` run one suite |
| `composer test:mysql`      | The MySQL tests, against the server given in `FEGG_TEST_MYSQL_DSN`, `FEGG_TEST_MYSQL_USER` and `FEGG_TEST_MYSQL_PASSWORD` (skipped without them) |
| `composer analyse`         | PHPStan (level 5, with a baseline) |
| `composer cs:check`        | PSR-12 check of `core/src` and the tests (`composer cs:fix` applies the fixes) |

The integration tests run with `FEGG_ENV=development` and a throwaway SQLite database, so they never touch `site/data/db/db.db`.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/first/update ---


# Updating the core

Because the core (`htdocs/_fegg/core/`) and the project (`htdocs/_fegg/site/`) are separate, security fixes and new features of Fegg can be rolled out to each project by replacing the core, the same way you update WordPress itself.

## Routine updates

```bash
./fegg-cli core:status              # current version, and whether core files were changed
./fegg-cli core:update --dry-run    # show the difference to the latest release (changes nothing)
./fegg-cli core:update              # update to the latest tag (vX.Y.Z)
./fegg-cli core:update v5.1.0       # update to a given version
npm run build:scss                  # rebuild the CMS CSS if htdocs/cms/scss changed
```

- The release is fetched with `git`. If you can clone from GitHub over SSH (`git clone git@github.com:...` works for you), no token is needed.
- Only core files are replaced: `htdocs/_fegg/core/`, `htdocs/_fegg/.htaccess` and `htdocs/cms/`. `site/`, `htdocs/index.php`, `htdocs/.htaccess` and `fegg-cli` are not changed.
- If core files were edited in the project, the command lists them and stops. Move those changes to overrides in `site/` (see [Directory structure](./cont.mdx#overriding-the-core)) and run it again. Add `--force` only if you really want to overwrite them.
- The replaced files are kept in `htdocs/_fegg/site/data/core-backup/<old version>-<timestamp>/`.
- After the update, the template cache is cleared and `fegg-cli migration` runs automatically.
- Finally, the `core/UPGRADE.md` sections for every version crossed are printed. They list what you have to change by hand (entry files, `.htaccess`, deprecated APIs). Read them.

Run the tests and check the site, then commit. `git status` should show only core files.

### Changing the source repository

By default the release is fetched from `git@github.com:lionheart-group/lh-fegg-skelton.git`. To use another repository (a fork, or HTTPS), set it in the project:

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/fegg_update.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

$fegg_update['repository'] = 'https://github.com/lionheart-group/lh-fegg-skelton.git';
```

The environment variable `FEGG_UPDATE_REPO` takes precedence over this file.

## When the server has no SSH (FTP-only hosting)

1. Run `./fegg-cli core:update` in your local working copy, then run the tests and check that everything works.
2. Commit the changed files.
3. Upload `htdocs/_fegg/core/` and `htdocs/cms/` (including the built CSS) over FTP. Upload `htdocs/_fegg/.htaccess` only if it changed.
4. `fegg-cli migration` cannot run on the server. For versions that change tables, either upload a database file built locally or open `/cms/migrate/` in the CMS.
5. Check the live site with `./fegg-cli doctor https://example.com`.

## Merging upstream with git

You can also update by adding the skeleton repository as `upstream` and merging a release tag:

```bash
git remote add upstream git@github.com:lionheart-group/lh-fegg-skelton.git
git fetch upstream --tags
git merge v5.1.0
```

If the project only edited `site/`, there are no conflicts in the core. The skeleton's sample files in `site/` (sample controllers and templates) may also have changed; in that case keep the project's version.

## Versions

| Version         | Meaning |
| :-------------- | :------ |
| Patch (5.0.x)   | Fixes inside the core. `core:update` alone is enough |
| Minor (5.x.0)   | New features. Optional work for projects is described in `UPGRADE.md` |
| Major (x.0.0)   | Changes that require fixes to project code |

The full text, including the release procedure for skeleton maintainers, is in [docs/updating.md](https://github.com/lionheart-group/lh-fegg-skelton/blob/master/docs/updating.md) of the skeleton repository.


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/first/migration ---


# Migrating a Fegg 3 / 4 project

Fegg 5 separates the core from the project code and moves classes into namespaces. Projects built on v3 or v4 are migrated by hand. Once migrated, they are updated with `fegg-cli core:update` like any Fegg 5 project.

This page is a summary. The full steps, with the commands and the complete path tables, are in [docs/migration-v3-v4-to-v5.md](https://github.com/lionheart-group/lh-fegg-skelton/blob/master/docs/migration-v3-v4-to-v5.md) of the skeleton repository.

Work on a separate branch and test against a copy of the production database.

## 1. Find the changes the project made to the core

Find the skeleton version the project started from (`version` in `package.json`, or `@version` in `fegg/Application.php`). Check out the skeleton at that tag, copy the project's core files over it (`fegg/`, `code/lib/`, `code/application/cms/`, `code/template/cms/`, `htdocs/cms/`, `htdocs/index.php`, `htdocs/.htaccess`) and look at `git diff`. Keep the list; each item is moved to `site/` in step 3.

## 2. Move files into the new structure

| v3 / v4                                                         | Fegg 5                                                          |
| :-------------------------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `fegg/*.php`, `code/lib/` (DB_pdo, LH_FW, Validation, Tool, ...) | `htdocs/_fegg/core/` (use the new version)                       |
| Project values in `fegg/settings.php`                           | `htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php`                          |
