# Pdo::pragma

> Set pragma query

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::pragma( string $table, string $mode )
```

Builds an SQLite `PRAGMA` on `$table`. Read the rows with [all](./all.mdx) after [execute](./execute.mdx). SQLite only.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks             |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ------------------- |
| $table | string    |         | Table name          |
| $mode  | string    |         | Pragma mode (below) |

### Pragma mode

| Mode         | Query                                                 |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| `table`      | `PRAGMA table_info("table")` (also any unknown mode)  |
| `index_list` | `PRAGMA index_list("table")`                          |
| `index`      | `PRAGMA index_info("table")`                          |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Columns of cms_news: cid, name, type, notnull, dflt_value, pk
$columns = $db->pragma('cms_news', 'table')->execute()->all();

// Indexes of cms_news: seq, name, unique, origin, partial
$indexes = $db->pragma('cms_news', 'index_list')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setgroup ---


# Pdo::setGroup

> Set group query

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setGroup( string $query )
```

Sets GROUP BY. The text is written into the SQL as it is: never put user input in it. Another call appends its text directly, without a comma, so give every column in one call.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                          |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------- |
| $query | string    |         | Columns, separated by commas     |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Select category, count(*) AS n From "cms_news" Where "cms_news".valid = 1 Group By category
$rows = $db->setItem('category, count(*) AS n')->setGroup('category')->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setitem ---


# Pdo::setItem

> Set query item values

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setItem( string $query, [ array $parameter = '' ] )
```

Sets the columns of a query.

- Without `$parameter`: the columns of a SELECT (`'news_id, title'`, expressions allowed).
- With an associative array: the values of an INSERT / UPDATE, matched to the columns in `$query` by name. A listed column missing from the array is written as `''`.
- With a list: the values in the order of the columns in `$query`.

For INSERT and UPDATE, prefer [setValues](./setvalues.mdx): it writes only the given columns, can store NULL and checks the column names.

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks                          |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------- |
| $query     | string    |         | Columns, separated by commas     |
| $parameter | array     | ''      | Values (by name or by position)  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Select news_id, title From "cms_news" Where "cms_news".valid = 1
$rows = $db->setItem('news_id, title')->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();

// Insert Into "cms_news" ("title", "category", modified, created, valid) Values (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
$db->setItem('title, category', array('title' => 'Sample', 'category' => 2))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setvalues ---


# Pdo::setValues

> Set the values to write from an associative array

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setValues( array $values )
```

Sets the values of an INSERT or UPDATE from `column => value` pairs. Unlike [setItem](./setitem.mdx), only the given columns are written and `null` is stored as NULL. `Model::insertRow()` and `Model::updateRow()` use it.

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` for an empty array, a column name that is not a plain name (letters, digits and `_`, optionally `table.column`), or a value that is an array or an object.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                       |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------- |
| $values | array     |         | Values (`column => value`)    |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Insert Into "cms_news" ("title", "image_id", modified, created, valid) Values ('Sample', NULL, ...)
$db->setValues(array('title' => 'Sample', 'image_id' => null))->insert('cms_news')->execute();

// Update "cms_news" Set "title"= ?, modified = ? Where news_id = ?
$db->setValues(array('title' => 'New title'))->setWhere('news_id = ?', 1)->update('cms_news')->execute();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setincrement ---


# Pdo::setIncrement

> Add to a numeric column in an UPDATE

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setIncrement( string $column, int|float $by )
```

Adds `"column" = "column" + ?` to an [update](./update.mdx), so the addition happens in the database and simultaneous requests do not lose counts. `Model::increment()` uses it.

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name or `$by` is not an int or a float.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------ |
| $column | string    |         | Numeric column                       |
| $by     | int/float |         | Amount to add; negative to subtract  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Update "cms_news" Set modified = ?, "view_count" = "view_count" + ? Where news_id = ?
$db->setIncrement('view_count', 1)->setWhere('news_id = ?', 5)->update('cms_news')->execute();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setlimit ---


# Pdo::setLimit

> Set limit value

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setLimit( int $limit, [ int $offset = 0 ] )
```

Adds `Limit offset, limit` to a SELECT.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                 |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------- |
| $limit  | int       |         | Maximum number of rows  |
| $offset | int       | 0       | Number of rows to skip  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// ... Order By post_date DESC Limit 20, 10  (rows 21 to 30)
$rows = $db->setOrder('post_date DESC')->setLimit(10, 20)->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setorder ---


# Pdo::setOrder

> Set order value

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setOrder( string $query )
```

Sets ORDER BY. The text is written into the SQL as it is: never put user input in it. Another call appends its text directly, so give every sort key in one call.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                          |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------- |
| $query | string    |         | ORDER BY expression              |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// ... Order By post_date DESC, news_id DESC
$rows = $db->setOrder('post_date DESC, news_id DESC')->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setquery ---


# Pdo::setQuery

> Set query string directly

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setQuery( string $query, [ array $parameter = [] ] )
```

Sets the SQL to run with [execute](./execute.mdx), as it is.

- `$parameter` is the list of values for the `?` placeholders, in order. Arrays are not expanded here (they are in [setWhere](./setwhere.mdx)); write one `?` per value.
- Nothing is added automatically: write `valid = 1` yourself.
- Only queries that start with `SELECT` or `PRAGMA` return rows ([all](./all.mdx), [one](./one.mdx)) and run on a slave. Any other query (including `WITH ... SELECT`) runs on the master as a statement; read [getAffectedRow](./getaffectedrow.mdx) after it.

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks                              |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------ |
| $query     | string    |         | SQL with `?` placeholders            |
| $parameter | array     | []      | Values, in placeholder order         |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$rows = $db->setQuery(
    'SELECT category, count(*) AS n FROM cms_news WHERE valid = 1 GROUP BY category HAVING n > ?',
    array(1)
)->execute()->all();

$db->setQuery('UPDATE cms_news SET visible = ? WHERE post_date < ?', array(0, '2020-01-01'))->execute();
$updated = $db->getAffectedRow();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setwhere ---


# Pdo::setWhere

> Set where condition

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setWhere( string $query, [ mixed ...$parameters ] )
```

Adds to the WHERE clause. Give one argument per `?` placeholder, after the query.

- Another call appends its text after a space: write the `AND` / `OR` yourself (`setWhere('AND title = ?', $title)`).
- An array bound to one placeholder expands it: `id = ?` and `id IN (?)` become `id IN (?, ?, ...)`, `<>` / `!=` / `NOT IN` become `NOT IN`, `LIKE ?` becomes `(... LIKE ? OR ... LIKE ?)` and `NOT LIKE ?` becomes `(... NOT LIKE ? AND ...)`. An empty array matches no row (`=`, `IN`, `LIKE`) or every row (`<>`, `NOT IN`, `NOT LIKE`). An array on any other operator (`<`, `BETWEEN` ...) throws `InvalidArgumentException`.
- A `?` inside a quoted string literal (`'?'`) is not a placeholder.

## Arguments

| Name           | Data type | Default | Remarks                                    |
| -------------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| $query         | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders            |
| ...$parameters | mixed     |         | One value (or array of values) per placeholder |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// ... Where category = ? AND title = ? And "cms_news".valid = 1
$rows = $db->setWhere('category = ?', 1)
    ->setWhere('AND title = ?', 'A')
    ->select('cms_news')
    ->execute()
    ->all();

// ... Where post_date >= ? AND news_id IN (?, ?, ?) ...
$rows = $db->setWhere('post_date >= ? AND news_id IN (?)', '2026-01-01', array(1, 2, 3))
    ->select('cms_news')
    ->execute()
    ->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/sethaving ---


# Pdo::setHaving

> Set having condition

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setHaving( string $query, [ mixed ...$parameters ] )
```

Adds to the HAVING clause, used with [setGroup](./setgroup.mdx). Parameters work as in [setWhere](./setwhere.mdx): one per placeholder, and an array expands its placeholder.

## Arguments

| Name           | Data type | Default | Remarks                                    |
| -------------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| $query         | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders            |
| ...$parameters | mixed     |         | One value (or array of values) per placeholder |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Select category, count(*) AS n From "cms_news" Where "cms_news".valid = 1 Group By category Having count(*) > ?
$rows = $db->setItem('category, count(*) AS n')
    ->setGroup('category')
    ->setHaving('count(*) > ?', 3)
    ->select('cms_news')
    ->execute()
    ->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setjoin ---


# Pdo::setJoin

> Set join table

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setJoin( string|array $table, [ string $type = null ] )
```

Adds a JOIN. Follow it with [setOn](./seton.mdx) for the condition. `$table` is a table name or `array('table', 'alias')`; the names are quoted as they are.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type    | Default | Remarks                                                           |
| ------ | ------------ | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| $table | string/array |         | Table name, or `array('table', 'alias')`                          |
| $type  | string       | null    | `'LEFT'`, `'INNER'` or `'RIGHT'` (anything else: plain `JOIN`)    |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Select cms_news.title, f.org_name From "cms_news" LEFT JOIN "cms_file" AS "f" ON f.file_id = cms_news.file_id AND f.valid = ? Where "cms_news".valid = 1
$rows = $db->setItem('cms_news.title, f.org_name')
    ->setJoin(array('cms_file', 'f'), 'LEFT')
    ->setOn('f.file_id = cms_news.file_id AND f.valid = ?', 1)
    ->select('cms_news')
    ->execute()
    ->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/seton ---


# Pdo::setOn

> Set on query for joined table

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setOn( string $query, [ mixed ...$parameters ] )
```

Adds the ON condition of the last [setJoin](./setjoin.mdx). The first call after `setJoin()` writes `ON`; another call appends its text after a space. Parameters work as in [setWhere](./setwhere.mdx).

Throws `LogicException` when no join was set before.

## Arguments

| Name           | Data type | Default | Remarks                                    |
| -------------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| $query         | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders            |
| ...$parameters | mixed     |         | One value (or array of values) per placeholder |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// ... LEFT JOIN "cms_file" ON cms_file.file_id = cms_news.file_id AND cms_file.type = ? ...
$rows = $db->setJoin('cms_file', 'LEFT')
    ->setOn('cms_file.file_id = cms_news.file_id')
    ->setOn('AND cms_file.type = ?', 'pdf')
    ->select('cms_news')
    ->execute()
    ->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/disabledregularusequery ---


# Pdo::disabledRegularUseQuery

> Disable automatic append query flag

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::disabledRegularUseQuery()
```

Skips every automatic query (for every table and for the table, see [Configuration](../configuration.mdx)) for every later query of this instance. To skip them for one query only, use [unsetRegularUseQuery](./unsetregularusequery.mdx) and [unsetRegularUseQueryForTable](./unsetregularusequeryfortable.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();
$db->disabledRegularUseQuery();

// Select * From "cms_news"  (also the rows with valid = 0)
$rows = $db->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/unsetregularusequery ---


# Pdo::unsetRegularUseQuery

> Disable automatic append query for a current query flag

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::unsetRegularUseQuery()
```

For the next query only, skips the queries added for every table (`regular_use` in `database_regular_use_query.php`, see [Configuration](../configuration.mdx)): `valid = 1` on SELECT and count, `created` / `modified` / `valid` on INSERT and `modified` on UPDATE.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Select * From "cms_news"
$db->unsetRegularUseQuery()->select('cms_news')->execute();

// Select * From "cms_news" Where "cms_news".valid = 1  (the next query is back to normal)
$db->select('cms_news')->execute();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/unsetregularusequeryfortable ---


# Pdo::unsetRegularUseQueryForTable

> Disable automatic append query of the table for a current query flag

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::unsetRegularUseQueryForTable()
```

For the next query only, skips the queries configured for the table (the `table` section of `database_regular_use_query.php`, see [Configuration](../configuration.mdx)). The queries added for every table still apply.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$rows = $db->unsetRegularUseQueryForTable()->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/reset ---


# Pdo::reset

> Forget the query being built

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::reset()
```

Forgets everything set for the next query (table, items, conditions, joins, order, limit ...), turns the reset after [execute](./execute.mdx) back on (see [unsetInitQueryFlag](./unsetinitqueryflag.mdx)) and re-enables the automatic queries unless [disabledRegularUseQuery](./disabledregularusequery.mdx) was called. The result and the last query are kept.

`execute()` already does this after a successful query. A query that throws keeps its state, so call `reset()` after catching a `QueryException` when you reuse the instance. `Fegg\Model` does this by itself.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Database\QueryException;

$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

try {
    $db->setWhere('no_such_column = ?', 1)->select('cms_news')->execute();
} catch (QueryException $e) {
    $db->reset();
}

$rows = $db->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/setinitqueryflag ---


# Pdo::setInitQueryFlag

> Enable reset query flag

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::setInitQueryFlag()
```

Turns the reset after [execute](./execute.mdx) back on (the default), after [unsetInitQueryFlag](./unsetinitqueryflag.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Count and fetch with the same conditions
$db->unsetInitQueryFlag()->setWhere('category = ?', 2);
$count = $db->count('cms_news')->execute()->one();

$db->setInitQueryFlag()->setLimit(10);
$rows = $db->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/unsetinitqueryflag ---


# Pdo::unsetInitQueryFlag

> Disable reset query flag

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::unsetInitQueryFlag()
```

Keeps what was set (items, conditions, joins, order, limit ...) after [execute](./execute.mdx), so the next query can reuse it. `Model::find()` uses it to count and fetch with the same conditions. Turn it back on with [setInitQueryFlag](./setinitqueryflag.mdx), or drop everything with [reset](./reset.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Count and fetch with the same conditions
$db->unsetInitQueryFlag()->setWhere('category = ?', 2);
$count = $db->count('cms_news')->execute()->one();

$db->setInitQueryFlag()->setLimit(10);
$rows = $db->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/execute ---


# Pdo::execute

> Execute built query

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::execute()
```

Builds the query (when a build method such as [select](./select.mdx) was called) or takes the one set with [setQuery](./setquery.mdx), and runs it. SELECT and PRAGMA queries run on a slave and their rows are read with [all](./all.mdx) or [one](./one.mdx); other queries run on the master. Inside a transaction every query runs on the transaction's connection. Afterwards the query being built is forgotten (see [unsetInitQueryFlag](./unsetinitqueryflag.mdx)).

Throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException` when the connection or the query fails. Its message ends with the SQL with the values filled in.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$rows = $db->setWhere('category = ?', 2)->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();

$db->setValues(array('title' => 'Sample'))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
$id = $db->getLastIndexId();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/compile ---


# Pdo::compile

> Compile built query (not execute)

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::compile()
```

Builds the query without running it or forgetting it. Read it with [getQuery](./getquery.mdx) and [getParam](./getparam.mdx), or with the values filled in with [getLastQuery](./getlastquery.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setWhere('category = ?', 2)->setOrder('created desc')->setLimit(10, 20)->select('cms_news')->compile();

echo $db->getQuery();
// -> Select * From "cms_news" Where category = ? And "cms_news".valid = 1 Order By created desc Limit 20, 10
print_r($db->getParam());
// -> Array ( [0] => 2 )
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/all ---


# Pdo::all

> Get all the result of executed select query

## Description

```php
public array Pdo::all( [ string $index = '' ] )
```

Returns the rows of the last SELECT or PRAGMA run with [execute](./execute.mdx), as associative arrays. With `$index`, the rows are keyed by that column.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                         |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |
| $index | string    | ''      | Column to use as the array key  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$rows = $db->setItem('news_id, title')->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
// -> array(0 => array('news_id' => 1, 'title' => 'A'), 1 => array('news_id' => 2, 'title' => 'B'), ...)

$rows = $db->setItem('news_id, title')->select('cms_news')->execute()->all('news_id');
// -> array(1 => array('news_id' => 1, 'title' => 'A'), 2 => array('news_id' => 2, 'title' => 'B'), ...)
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/one ---


# Pdo::one

> Shift the result of executed select query

## Description

```php
public array|null Pdo::one()
```

Returns the first row of the last SELECT or PRAGMA, or `null` when it returned no row.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$row = $db->setWhere('news_id = ?', 1)->select('cms_news')->execute()->one();
if ($row === null) {
    // not found
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/id ---


# Pdo::id

> Get the specific column's list of executed select query

## Description

```php
public array Pdo::id( string $index )
```

Returns the values of one column of every row of the last SELECT, as a list.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ----------- |
| $index | string    |         | Column name |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$titles = $db->setItem('title')->select('cms_news')->execute()->id('title');
// -> array('A', 'B', 'C')
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/simplearray ---


# Pdo::simpleArray

> Get the specific column's key and value array of executed select query

## Description

```php
public array Pdo::simpleArray( string $keyName, string $valueName )
```

Returns `key => value` pairs built from two columns of every row of the last SELECT, for example for `<select>` options.

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks                    |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------- |
| $keyName   | string    |         | Column to use as the key   |
| $valueName | string    |         | Column to use as the value |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$titles = $db->setItem('news_id, title')->select('cms_news')->execute()->simpleArray('news_id', 'title');
// -> array(1 => 'A', 2 => 'B', 3 => 'C')
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/getaffectedrow ---


# Pdo::getAffectedRow

> Get affected row number of executed update query

## Description

```php
public int Pdo::getAffectedRow()
```

Returns the number of rows the last query inserted, updated or deleted. After a SELECT the value depends on the driver (0 on SQLite); count the rows of [all](./all.mdx) instead.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setValues(array('visible' => 0))->setWhere('category = ?', 3)->update('cms_news')->execute();
$updated = $db->getAffectedRow();
// -> 4
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/getlastindexid ---


# Pdo::getLastIndexId

> Get the last inserted id of executed insert query

## Description

```php
public string Pdo::getLastIndexId()
```

Returns the ID generated by the last INSERT on the connection (`PDO::lastInsertId()`), as a string. Call it on the same instance, right after [execute](./execute.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setValues(array('title' => 'Sample'))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
$id = (int) $db->getLastIndexId();
// -> 24
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/getlastquery ---


# Pdo::getLastQuery

> Get the last executed query for debugging

## Description

```php
public string Pdo::getLastQuery()
```

Returns the last query run with [execute](./execute.mdx) (or built with [compile](./compile.mdx)), with the values filled in and quoted. It is for logs and debugging only: never run the result. To get the SQL and the values separately, use [getQuery](./getquery.mdx) and [getParam](./getparam.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setWhere('title = ?', "it's")->select('cms_news')->execute();

echo $db->getLastQuery();
// -> Select * From "cms_news" Where title = 'it''s' And "cms_news".valid = 1
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/getlastquerydebug ---


# Pdo::getLastQueryDebug

> Get the last executed query and parameter separately for debugging

## Description

```php
public array Pdo::getLastQueryDebug()
```

Returns `array('query' => ..., 'parameter' => ...)`: the last query with its `?` placeholders, and the values bound to them.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setWhere('category = ?', 2)->select('cms_news')->execute();

print_r($db->getLastQueryDebug());
```

```php title="Output"
Array
(
    [query] => Select * From "cms_news" Where  category = ?  And "cms_news".valid = 1
    [parameter] => Array
        (
            [0] => 2
        )

)
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/getquery ---


# Pdo::getQuery

> Get the built query

## Description

```php
public string Pdo::getQuery()
```

Returns the last query built by [compile](./compile.mdx) or [execute](./execute.mdx), with its `?` placeholders. [getParam](./getparam.mdx) returns the values.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setWhere('category = ?', 2)->select('cms_news')->compile();

echo $db->getQuery();
// -> Select * From "cms_news" Where category = ? And "cms_news".valid = 1
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/getparam ---


# Pdo::getParam

> Get the values of the built query

## Description

```php
public array Pdo::getParam()
```

Returns the values bound to the `?` placeholders of the last query built by [compile](./compile.mdx) or [execute](./execute.mdx), in order.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setWhere('category = ? AND news_id IN (?)', 2, array(5, 6))->select('cms_news')->compile();

print_r($db->getParam());
// -> Array ( [0] => 2 [1] => 5 [2] => 6 )
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/getreturncode ---


# Pdo::getReturnCode

> Get return code of the last executed query

## Description

```php
public int|null Pdo::getReturnCode()
```

Returns the number of affected rows of the last query that was not a SELECT or PRAGMA (`null` before the first one). Prefer [getAffectedRow](./getaffectedrow.mdx). A failed query throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException`, so there is no error code to check.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setValues(array('title' => 'Sample'))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
$code = $db->getReturnCode();
// -> 1
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/masterserver ---


# Pdo::masterServer

> Connect to master server

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::masterServer()
```

Connects to the master database (see [Configuration](../configuration.mdx)) and runs the next query on it, also when it is a SELECT. Use it to read a row right after writing it when the slaves may lag behind. Throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException` when the connection fails.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->setValues(array('title' => 'Sample'))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
$id = $db->getLastIndexId();

// Read it back from the master
$row = $db->masterServer()->setWhere('news_id = ?', $id)->select('cms_news')->execute()->one();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/slaveserver ---


# Pdo::slaveServer

> Connect to slave server

## Description

```php
public self Pdo::slaveServer()
```

Connects to one of the slave databases, picked at random (see [Configuration](../configuration.mdx)), and runs the next query on it. SELECT and PRAGMA queries already use a slave; this sends another query there. Throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException` when the connection fails.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

// Same as without slaveServer(): a SELECT already uses a slave
$rows = $db->slaveServer()->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/close ---


# Pdo::close

> Disconnect from the database

## Description

```php
public void Pdo::close()
```

Drops the connection this instance used from the shared connections, so the next query (of any instance) opens a new one. The connection of an open transaction is kept. PHP closes connections at the end of the request, so this is rarely needed.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$rows = $db->select('cms_news')->execute()->all();
$db->close();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/closeall ---


# Pdo::closeAll

> Drop every shared connection

## Description

```php
public static void Pdo::closeAll()
```

Drops every shared connection. An open transaction is rolled back. The next query opens a new connection. Useful in long-running commands and tests.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Database\Pdo;

Pdo::closeAll();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/begintransaction ---


# Pdo::beginTransaction

> Begin a transaction

## Description

```php
public void Pdo::beginTransaction()
```

Opens a transaction on the master database. Until [commit](./commit.mdx) or [rollBack](./rollback.mdx), every query of every `Pdo` instance and every model runs inside it.

Throws `LogicException` when a transaction is already open. With models, prefer `Model::transaction()`, which commits, rolls back and handles nesting for you.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Database\Pdo;

$db = new Pdo();
$db->beginTransaction();
try {
    $db->setValues(array('title' => 'A'))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
    $db->setValues(array('title' => 'B'))->insert('cms_lab')->execute();
    $db->commit();
} catch (\Throwable $e) {
    $db->rollBack();
    throw $e;
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/commit ---


# Pdo::commit

> Commit the open transaction

## Description

```php
public void Pdo::commit()
```

Commits the transaction opened with [beginTransaction](./begintransaction.mdx). Any instance can commit it. Throws `LogicException` when no transaction is open.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->beginTransaction();
$db->setValues(array('title' => 'A'))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
$db->commit();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/rollback ---


# Pdo::rollBack

> Roll back the open transaction

## Description

```php
public void Pdo::rollBack()
```

Discards every write since [beginTransaction](./begintransaction.mdx). Any instance can roll it back. Throws `LogicException` when no transaction is open.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

$db->beginTransaction();
try {
    $db->setValues(array('title' => 'A'))->insert('cms_news')->execute();
    $db->commit();
} catch (\Throwable $e) {
    $db->rollBack();
    throw $e;
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/database/methods/intransaction ---


# Pdo::inTransaction

> Check whether a transaction is open

## Description

```php
public bool Pdo::inTransaction()
```

Returns `true` while a transaction opened with [beginTransaction](./begintransaction.mdx) (by any instance, or by `Model::transaction()`) is open.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$db = new \Fegg\Database\Pdo();

if (!$db->inTransaction()) {
    $db->beginTransaction();
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Providing some functions for calculating dates

## How to use

Import `Fegg\Tool\Date` and create it with `new`.

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Tool\Date;

class Event extends Controller
{
    public function index()
    {
        $date = new Date();

        $this->page['deadline'] = $date->addDay(date('Y-m-d H:i:s'), 7, 'Y/m/d');
        $this->page['last_day'] = $date->getTheLastday(date('Y-m-d'));

        $this->displayPage('event');
    }
}
```

## Date formats

The `$dateTime` arguments accept `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` and its shorter forms, with or without the separators (`2024`, `2024/04`, `2024-04-01`, `20240401`, `2024/4/1 9:30`), see [Date::makeupDateFormat](./methods/makeupdateformat.mdx). Other formats are parsed with `strtotime()`. [Date::addDay](./methods/addday.mdx), [Date::addMinute](./methods/addminute.mdx), [Date::addMonth](./methods/addmonth.mdx), [Date::getTheLastday](./methods/getthelastday.mdx) and [Date::getDiff](./methods/getdiff.mdx) (in days) throw `InvalidArgumentException` when the value cannot be parsed.

## Time zone

The time zone of the application is set **once at startup**, from `$settings['timezone']` in `site/config/settings.php` (default `FEGG_DEFAULT_TIMEZONE`, `Asia/Tokyo`):

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
$settings['timezone'] = 'Europe/Paris';
```

`date()`, `Fegg\Tool\Date` and the template modifier `feggDate` all use it. No Fegg method changes it afterwards:

- [Date::getSocialTime](./methods/getsocialtime.mdx) uses its `$timezone` argument for that call only.
- The controller methods `$this->getDatetime($format = 'Y-m-d H:i:s', $timeZone = '')` and `$this->convertDatetime($date, $fromTimezone, $toTimezone)` work on their own `DateTime` objects. Fegg 4 changed the process-wide time zone in them.
- `convertDatetime()` returns `''` for a date it cannot read (Fegg 4 returned `1970-01-01 ...`).

```php title="Controller"
echo $this->getDatetime('Y-m-d H:i', 'UTC');                                        // now, in UTC
echo $this->convertDatetime('2026-04-01 09:00:00', 'Asia/Tokyo', 'Europe/London');  // 2026-04-01 01:00:00
echo $this->convertDatetime('not a date', 'Asia/Tokyo', 'UTC');                     // ''
```

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/addday ---


# Date::addDay

> Add days to a date

## Description

```php
public string Date::addDay( string $dateTime, int $interval, [ string $format = 'Y-m-d H:i:s' ] )
```

Returns `$dateTime` plus `$interval` days, formatted with `date()` format `$format`. Use a negative `$interval` to subtract days.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default       | Remarks                                  |
| --------- | --------- | ------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| $dateTime | string    |               | Base date and time                       |
| $interval | int       |               | Number of days (negative: before)        |
| $format   | string    | 'Y-m-d H:i:s' | `date()` format of the result            |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->addDay('2024-04-01 10:00:00', 7);         // -> "2024-04-08 10:00:00"
echo $date->addDay('2024/04/01', -1, 'Y/m/d');        // -> "2024/03/31"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/addminute ---


# Date::addMinute

> Add minutes to a date

## Description

```php
public string Date::addMinute( string $dateTime, int $interval, [ string $format = 'Y-m-d H:i:s' ] )
```

Returns `$dateTime` plus `$interval` minutes, formatted with `date()` format `$format`. The seconds of `$dateTime` are dropped (set to `00`).

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default       | Remarks                                  |
| --------- | --------- | ------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| $dateTime | string    |               | Base date and time                       |
| $interval | int       |               | Number of minutes (negative: before)     |
| $format   | string    | 'Y-m-d H:i:s' | `date()` format of the result            |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->addMinute('2024-04-01 10:00:30', 90); // -> "2024-04-01 11:30:00"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/addmonth ---


# Date::addMonth

> Add months to a date

## Description

```php
public string Date::addMonth( string $dateTime, int $interval, [ string $format = 'Y-m-d H:i:s' ] )
```

Returns `$dateTime` plus `$interval` months, formatted with `date()` format `$format`. When the day does not exist in the target month, the last day of that month is used. The time is reset to `00:00:00`.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default       | Remarks                                  |
| --------- | --------- | ------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| $dateTime | string    |               | Base date                                |
| $interval | int       |               | Number of months (negative: before)      |
| $format   | string    | 'Y-m-d H:i:s' | `date()` format of the result            |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->addMonth('2024-01-31', 1);            // -> "2024-02-29 00:00:00"
echo $date->addMonth('2024-04-15', -6, 'Y/m/d');  // -> "2023/10/15"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/getdiff ---


# Date::getDiff

> Get the difference between two dates

## Description

```php
public number Date::getDiff( string $fromDateTime, string $toDateTime, [ boolean $month = false ] )
```

Returns `$toDateTime` minus `$fromDateTime` in days. The result is a decimal number when the times of day differ (12 hours is `0.5`). With `$month`, the difference is counted in calendar months, ignoring the days (`2024-01-31` to `2024-02-01` is `1`).

## Arguments

| Name          | Data type | Default | Remarks                                  |
| ------------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| $fromDateTime | string    |         | Start date and time                      |
| $toDateTime   | string    |         | End date and time                        |
| $month        | boolean   | false   | `true`: in months / `false`: in days     |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->getDiff('2024-04-01', '2024-04-10');             // -> 9
echo $date->getDiff('2024-04-10', '2024-04-01');             // -> -9
echo $date->getDiff('2024-01-31', '2024-03-01', true);       // -> 2
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/getwesterncalendar ---


# Date::getWesternCalendar

> Convert a Japanese era date to the Western calendar

## Description

```php
public string Date::getWesternCalendar( string $date )
```

`$date` is the era letter followed by a 2-digit year, month and day (`H310430`). The era letters are `M` (Meiji), `T` (Taisho), `S` (Showa), `H` (Heisei) and `R` (Reiwa). Returns `YYYY-MM-DD`.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                 |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | --------------------------------------- |
| $date | string    |         | Japanese era date, e.g. `R060401`       |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->getWesternCalendar('H310430'); // -> "2019-04-30"
echo $date->getWesternCalendar('R060401'); // -> "2024-04-01"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/getgmtoffset ---


# Date::getGmtOffset

> Get the current GMT offset of a time zone

## Description

```php
public string Date::getGmtOffset( [ string $timeZone = 'Asia/Tokyo' ] )
```

Returns the offset in `+09:00` format.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default      | Remarks                        |
| --------- | --------- | ------------ | ------------------------------ |
| $timeZone | string    | 'Asia/Tokyo' | Time zone identifier           |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->getGmtOffset();                      // -> "+09:00"
echo $date->getGmtOffset('America/Los_Angeles'); // -> "-08:00" (or "-07:00" in summer)
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/getsocialtime ---


# Date::getSocialTime

> Get the elapsed time like social media ("10分前")

## Description

```php
public string Date::getSocialTime( string $date, [ string $timezone = '' ] )
```

Returns the time between `$date` and now in Japanese: `N日前`, `N時間前`, `N分前` or `N秒前` (the largest unit that is 1 or more). Returns an empty string when `$date` is now.

:::note

`$timezone` is the time zone `$date` is written in, for this call only. The time zone of the application (`$settings['timezone']`) is not changed. When it is omitted, the application's time zone is used.

:::

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                               |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------- |
| $date     | string    |         | Date and time                         |
| $timezone | string    | ''      | Time zone of `$date`, e.g. `Asia/Tokyo` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->getSocialTime(date('Y-m-d H:i:s', time() - 600)); // -> "10分前"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/getthelastday ---


# Date::getTheLastday

> Get the last day of the month

## Description

```php
public string Date::getTheLastday( string $dateTime )
```

Returns the last day of the month of `$dateTime` as a 2-digit string.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                 |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------- |
| $dateTime | string    |         | Date in the target month |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->getTheLastday('2024-02-10'); // -> "29"
echo $date->getTheLastday('2024/04');    // -> "30"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/date/methods/makeupdateformat ---


# Date::makeupDateFormat

> Normalize a date to `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`

## Description

```php
public string Date::makeupDateFormat( string $date )
```

Accepts a date with or without the separators `-`, `/` and `:`, and with the time or a part of the date omitted. The missing parts are filled with `01` (month, day) or `00` (time), and every part is zero-padded. A value that does not match is returned unchanged.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks         |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | --------------- |
| $date | string    |         | Date and time   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Date;

$date = new Date();

echo $date->makeupDateFormat('2024');          // -> "2024-01-01 00:00:00"
echo $date->makeupDateFormat('2024/4/1');      // -> "2024-04-01 00:00:00"
echo $date->makeupDateFormat('2024/4/1 9:30'); // -> "2024-04-01 09:30:00"
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Providing some functions for managing files/directories

## How to use

Import `Fegg\Tool\File` and create it with `new`.

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Tool\File;

class Export extends Controller
{
    public function index()
    {
        $file = new File();

        $file->createDirectory(FEGG_SITE_DIR . '/data/export');
        $file->writeFile(FEGG_SITE_DIR . '/data/export/list.csv', "id,title\n", 'w');
    }
}
```

:::note

Most methods do nothing (no error, no return value) when the source file or directory does not exist. Check the result yourself, e.g. with `file_exists()`, when it matters.

:::

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/copyall ---


# File::copyAll

> Copy all the files in a directory to another directory

## Description

```php
public void File::copyAll( string $fromDirectory, string $toDirectory )
```

Copies every file directly under `$fromDirectory` to `$toDirectory` and sets the permission of the copied files to `0666`. Both directories must exist; otherwise nothing happens. Subdirectories are not copied, use [File::copyDirectory](./copydirectory.mdx) for them.

## Arguments

| Name           | Data type | Default | Remarks                              |
| -------------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------ |
| $fromDirectory | string    |         | Source directory                     |
| $toDirectory   | string    |         | Destination directory (must exist)   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->copyAll('path/to/foo', 'path/to/bar');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/copydirectory ---


# File::copyDirectory

> Copy the contents of a directory to another directory

## Description

```php
public void File::copyDirectory( string $fromDirectory, string $toDirectory, [ boolean $recursiveCallFlag = false ] )
```

Copies the files under `$fromDirectory` to `$toDirectory`. The destination directory must already exist. When `$recursiveCallFlag` is `true`, subdirectories are created in the destination and copied as well.

## Arguments

| Name               | Data type | Default | Remarks                                       |
| ------------------ | --------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| $fromDirectory     | string    |         | Source directory                              |
| $toDirectory       | string    |         | Destination directory (must exist)            |
| $recursiveCallFlag | boolean   | false   | `true`: also copy subdirectories recursively  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();

// Only the files directly under path/to/foo
$file->copyDirectory('path/to/foo', 'path/to/bar');

// Including subdirectories
$file->copyDirectory('path/to/foo', 'path/to/bar', true);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/copyfile ---


# File::copyFile

> Copy a file

## Description

```php
public void File::copyFile( string $source, string $dest )
```

Copies `$source` to `$dest` and sets the permission of the copy to `0666`. Nothing happens when `$source` does not exist.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks          |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------- |
| $source | string    |         | Source file      |
| $dest   | string    |         | Destination file |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->copyFile('path/to/foo.txt', 'path/to/bar.txt');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/createdirectory ---


# File::createDirectory

> Create a directory

## Description

```php
public void File::createDirectory( string $directory, [ string $permission = '0777' ] )
```

Creates `$directory` when it does not exist yet. Parent directories are not created, so the parent must exist.

:::note

The created directory always gets the permission `0777`. The `$permission` argument is currently ignored.

:::

## Arguments

| Name        | Data type | Default | Remarks                                  |
| ----------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| $directory  | string    |         | Directory to create                      |
| $permission | string    | '0777'  | Permission (currently ignored, see note) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->createDirectory('path/to/new_directory');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/movefile ---


# File::moveFile

> Move (rename) a file

## Description

```php
public void File::moveFile( string $from, string $to )
```

Moves `$from` to `$to` with `rename()`. Nothing happens when `$from` does not exist.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$file = new File();
$file->moveFile('path/to/foo.txt', 'path/to/bar.txt');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/readfile ---


# File::readFile

> Read a file and return its contents

## Description

```php
public string File::readFile( string $fileName )
```

Returns the contents of `$fileName`, or an empty string when the file does not exist.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks      |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------------ |
| $fileName | string    |         | File to read |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$contents = $file->readFile('path/to/foo.txt');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/removedirectory ---


# File::removeDirectory

> Remove a directory with all its contents

## Description

```php
public void File::removeDirectory( string $directory )
```

Deletes every file and subdirectory under `$directory` recursively, then removes `$directory` itself. Nothing happens when it is not a directory.

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks             |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------- |
| $directory | string    |         | Directory to remove |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->removeDirectory($this->config['upload_dir']['path'] . '/image/' . $id . '/');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/removeexpiredfile ---


# File::removeExpiredFile

> Remove the files older than the specified minutes

## Description

```php
public boolean File::removeExpiredFile( string $path, int $min )
```

Deletes the files directly under `$path` whose modification time is `$min` minutes ago or older. Subdirectories are not touched. The return value is always `false`.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                          |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| $path | string    |         | Target directory                                 |
| $min  | int       |         | Age in minutes after which a file is deleted     |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();

// Delete temporary files older than one day
$file->removeExpiredFile('path/to/tmp', 60 * 24);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/removefile ---


# File::removeFile

> Remove files

## Description

```php
public void File::removeFile( string $file )
```

Deletes the files matching `$file`. The path is passed to `glob()`, so wildcards such as `*` can be used. Directories are not removed.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------------- |
| $file | string    |         | File path or `glob()` pattern          |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->removeFile('path/to/foo.txt');

// Remove all the log files
$file->removeFile('path/to/log/*.log');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/touchdirectory ---


# File::touchDirectory

> Change the modification time of the files in a directory

## Description

```php
public void File::touchDirectory( string $directory, string $datetime )
```

Sets the modification time of every file and subdirectory under `$directory` (recursively) to `$datetime`. `$datetime` is parsed with `strtotime()`; nothing happens when it cannot be parsed.

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks                                |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------------- |
| $directory | string    |         | Target directory                       |
| $datetime  | string    |         | Date and time (any `strtotime()` format) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();
$file->touchDirectory('path/to/foo', '2024-04-01 10:00:00');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/file/methods/writefile ---


# File::writeFile

> Write data to a file

## Description

```php
public void File::writeFile( string $file, string $data, [ string $writeOption = 'a' ] )
```

Opens `$file` with the `fopen()` mode `$writeOption`, writes `$data` and sets the permission of the file to `0666`. The default mode `a` appends to the file; use `w` to overwrite it.

## Arguments

| Name         | Data type | Default | Remarks                                    |
| ------------ | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| $file        | string    |         | File to write                              |
| $data        | string    |         | Data to write                              |
| $writeOption | string    | 'a'     | `fopen()` mode (`a`: append, `w`: overwrite) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\File;

$file = new File();

// Append a line
$file->writeFile('path/to/foo.log', date('Y-m-d H:i:s') . " done\n");

// Overwrite the file
$file->writeFile('path/to/foo.txt', 'New contents', 'w');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Providing some functions for sending email

`Fegg\Tool\Mail` is a wrapper of the bundled [PHPMailer](https://github.com/PHPMailer/PHPMailer). Emails are sent as plain text, in UTF-8 with base64 encoding. By default they go through sendmail (the `sendmail_path` of php.ini is used when PHPMailer's default sendmail path does not exist); they can also be sent over [SMTP](#smtp).

## How to use

Import `Fegg\Tool\Mail` and create it with `new`. A failed send throws `PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception`, so wrap the sending in `try` / `catch`.

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Tool\Mail;
use PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception;

class Contact extends Controller
{
    public function post()
    {
        $body = $this->fetchPage('/contact/mail/to_admin');

        try {
            $mail = new Mail('Mail subject', $body);
            $mail->send('admin@example.com', 'Sender name', 'from@example.com');
        } catch (Exception $e) {
            // Failed to send: $e->getMessage()
        }
    }
}
```

The contact form of the skeleton (`htdocs/_fegg/site/controller/Contact.php`) is a complete example, including [Mail::setDebugFlag](./methods/setdebugflag.mdx) to write the emails to a log file instead of sending them.

## SMTP

To send through an SMTP server instead of sendmail, set `$settings['mail']` in `site/config/settings.php`, and put the password in `site/config/env.php` (or an environment variable) as `FEGG_SMTP_PASSWORD`. Every `Mail` object then uses it, the contact form included.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/settings.php"
$settings['mail'] = array(
    'transport'  => 'smtp',
    'host'       => 'smtp.example.com',
    'port'       => 587,
    'encryption' => 'tls',
    'username'   => 'user@example.com',
);
```

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/site/config/env.php"
'FEGG_SMTP_PASSWORD' => '...',
```

| Key          | Default       | Remarks |
| :----------- | :------------ | :------ |
| `transport`  | `'sendmail'`  | `'smtp'` to use SMTP |
| `host`       | `'localhost'` | SMTP server. `FEGG_SMTP_HOST` in `env.php` overrides it |
| `port`       | `587`         | Port |
| `encryption` | `'tls'`       | `'tls'`: STARTTLS. `'ssl'`: SMTPS (usually port 465). `''`: none |
| `username`   | `''`          | Login user; without it no authentication is used. `FEGG_SMTP_USERNAME` in `env.php` overrides it |

The connection times out after 10 seconds. Never write the password in `settings.php`, which is committed.

:::note

The PHPMailer instance is available as `$mail->_mailer` when you need a setting this wrapper does not cover.

:::

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />

# Mail::pushHeader

> Add custom header

## Description

```php
public self Mail::pushHeader( mixed $key, [ string $val = null ] )
```

Line breaks in the header name and value are removed.

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                                     |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| $key | mixed     |         | Header name, or an array of name => value   |
| $val | string    | null    | Header value when `$key` is a string        |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
// Same with

    'X-Foo' => 'bar',
    'X-Baz' => 'qux',
));
```

# Mail::pushAttached

> Add attachment file

## Description

```php
public self Mail::pushAttached( string $filePath, [ string $fileName = null ] )
```

Nothing is added when `$filePath` does not exist. Attachments are sent with [Mail::send](./send.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                     |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| $filePath | string    |         | Attachment file path                                        |
| $fileName | string    | null    | File name shown in the email / if omitted, the base name of `$filePath` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');

```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/send ---


# Mail::send

> Send email

## Description

```php
public boolean Mail::send( mixed $to, [ string $from_name = null, string $from_ad = null ] )
```

Sends the email with its attachments and returns `true` on success. A failed send (including an invalid address) throws `PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception`.

When the debug flag is enabled with [Mail::setDebugFlag](./setdebugflag.mdx), the email is not sent: the whole message is written to the log file with [Mail::log](./log.mdx) instead.

:::note

`$from_ad` is required in practice: without a valid sender address the send throws.

Each call sends only to the addresses given to it, so one `Mail` object can send the same message to several people in turn. To send one email to several recipients, pass them as an array.

:::

## Arguments

| Name       | Data type | Default | Remarks                                         |
| ---------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| $to        | mixed     |         | Recipient address, or an array of addresses     |
| $from_name | string    | null    | Sender name                                     |
| $from_ad   | string    | null    | Sender address (also used as the envelope sender) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;
use PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception;

try {
    $mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
    $mail->send('mail@example.com', 'John Doe', 'from@example.com');

    // Several recipients
    $mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
    $mail->send(array('foo@example.com', 'bar@example.com'), 'John Doe', 'from@example.com');
} catch (Exception $e) {
    // Failed to send: $e->getMessage()
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/log ---


# Mail::log

> Append a message to the log file

## Description

```php
public boolean Mail::log( string $message )
```

Appends a separator line and `$message` to the log file set by [Mail::setDebugFlag](./setdebugflag.mdx) (`/tmp/sendmaillog` by default). Nothing is written when the file or its directory is not writable. The return value is always `true`.

[Mail::send](./send.mdx) calls this method with the whole message when the debug flag is enabled.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | ----------- |
| $message | string    |         | Log message |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail();
$mail->setDebugFlag(true, FEGG_SITE_DIR . '/data/log/mail.log');
$mail->log('Contact form posted');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/mail/methods/setdebugflag ---


# Mail::setDebugFlag

> Set debug flag and log file name

## Description

```php
public self Mail::setDebugFlag( boolean $is_debug, string $log_name )
```

When `$is_debug` is `true`, [Mail::send](./send.mdx) does not send the email but writes the whole message to `$log_name`.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                    |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------- |
| $is_debug | boolean   |         | `true`: write a log instead of sending |
| $log_name | string    |         | Log file path              |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\Mail;

$mail = new Mail('Mail subject', 'Mail body');
$mail->setDebugFlag(true, FEGG_SITE_DIR . '/data/log/contact_admin.log');

// Written to the log file instead of being sent
$mail->send('mail@example.com', 'John Doe', 'from@example.com');
```

import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Read and write database tables with `?` placeholders

`Fegg\Model` builds SQL with `?` placeholders on top of [Fegg\Database\Pdo](../database/index.mdx). It is the normal way for a controller to read and write the database. SQLite is the default; the connection is set in `site/config/database_config.php` (see [Configuration](../database/configuration.mdx)).

:::note

The Fegg 4 class name `Model` still works through a compatibility layer, but every use is reported as `Fegg deprecated: ...` in the PHP error log, and it will be removed in Fegg 6. Write `use Fegg\Model;` at the top of the controller.

:::

## How to use

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Model;

class News extends Controller
{
    public function __init()
    {
        parent::__init();

        $this->_model = new Model(false, array(
            'table'   => 'cms_news',
            'id'      => 'news_id',
            'visible' => 'visible',
        ));
    }

    public function index()
    {
        $this->page['data'] = $this->_model
            ->where('category = ?', 2)
            ->order('post_date', 'desc')
            ->listMax(20)
            ->current($this->in('p'))
            ->find();
        $this->page['pager'] = $this->_model->getPager();

        $this->displayPage('news');
    }
}
```

## Tables

Tables are defined in YAML: `htdocs/_fegg/site/config/database/*.yaml` (project) and `htdocs/_fegg/core/config/database/*.yaml` (CMS core tables). `./fegg-cli migration` applies them to the database.

Use `./fegg-cli db:schema` to see the tables and columns (`./fegg-cli db:schema cms_news` for one table, `--json` for JSON). It reads the YAML files, so no database is needed, and it warns about tables that lack the columns below.

### Required columns

Every table used with `Fegg\Model` needs these columns. The model fills them by itself (see `core/config/database_regular_use_query.php`):

| Column     | Type       | Set by                                                                                         |
| ---------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `valid`    | `BOOLEAN`  | `1` on insert, `0` by [delete](./methods/delete.mdx). Reads only return rows with `valid = 1` |
| `created`  | `DATETIME` | insert                                                                                         |
| `modified` | `DATETIME` | insert and update                                                                              |

## Creating a model

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',      // table name
    'id'      => 'news_id',       // primary key
    'visible' => 'visible',       // optional: public flag column
    'bind'    => array('category' => 'INTEGER', 'post_date' => 'DATETIME'), // casts on write
));
```

- The first argument is `$is_admin`. With `false`, reads only return rows whose `visible` column is 1 (public pages). The CMS uses `true` to see unpublished rows. See [Model::\_\_construct](./methods/construct.mdx).
- `bind` types are `INTEGER`, `FLOAT`, `DATE` and `DATETIME` (case-insensitive). Any other type throws `InvalidArgumentException`. See [Model::addBindParam](./methods/addbindparam.mdx).
- Table and column names (`table`, `id`, `visible`, and the names given to `order()`, `join()`, `whereIn()` and the other helpers) must be plain identifiers: letters, digits and `_`, optionally `table.column` (`news_id`, `cms_news.post_date`). Anything else throws `InvalidArgumentException`. Never build names from user input.

## Reading

```php title="Controller"
$rows = $model
    ->where('category = ?', 2)
    ->whereLike('title', 'news')                 // contains; % and _ match literally
    ->whereIn('news_id', array(1, 2, 3))
    ->order('post_date', 'desc')
    ->listMax(20)->current($this->in('p'))       // page size and page number
    ->find();                                    // array of rows (associative arrays)
$pager = $model->getPager();                     // current_page, last_page, number_of_records ...

$rows  = $model->order('post_date', 'desc')->limit(5)->all();      // no paging
$row   = $model->id($id);                                          // one row by primary key, or null
$row   = $model->where('title = ?', $title)->one();                // first match, or null
$title = $model->where('news_id = ?', $id)->value('title');        // one value, or null
$ids   = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->column('news_id');      // array(3, 5, ...)
$count = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->count();                // int
$found = $model->where('title = ?', $title)->exists();             // bool
```

| Method                                            | Returns                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [find](./methods/find.mdx)                        | One page of rows; [getPager](./methods/getpager.mdx) has the links     |
| [all](./methods/all.mdx)                          | Every matching row ([limit](./methods/limit.mdx) caps it)              |
| [one](./methods/one.mdx) / [id](./methods/id.mdx) | The first row, or `null`                                               |
| [value](./methods/value.mdx)                      | The first column of the first row, or `null`                           |
| [column](./methods/column.mdx)                    | One column of every row, optionally keyed by another column            |
| [count](./methods/count.mdx)                      | Number of rows (of groups, with `group()`)                             |
| [exists](./methods/exists.mdx)                    | `true` when a row matches                                              |
| [toSql](./methods/tosql.mdx)                      | The SELECT `all()` would run, with the values filled in; runs nothing  |

### find() or all()

`find()` pages: set the page size with [listMax](./methods/listmax.mdx) and the page with [current](./methods/current.mdx), then read the links with `getPager()`. `all()` does not page; [limit](./methods/limit.mdx) caps it. Do not use `listMax(9999)` to get everything, and do not combine `limit()` with `find()` (it throws `LogicException`).

### Conditions are reset after every query

`where()`, `order()`, `select()`, `limit()` and the other chain methods only apply to the next query. After every query (`find()`, `all()`, `one()`, `count()`, `insertRow()` ...), failed ones included, the model forgets them, so each query starts clean. [Model::reset](./methods/reset.mdx) does the same by hand.

The write methods ([updateRowsWhere](./methods/updaterowswhere.mdx), [deleteWhere](./methods/deletewhere.mdx) ...) take their condition as arguments. Conditions chained with `where()` are not used by them (and are cleared).

## Writing

```php title="Controller"
$id  = $model->insertRow(array('title' => $title, 'category' => 2, 'content' => null));
$n   = $model->updateRow(array('title' => $title), $id);                 // rows updated
$n   = $model->updateRowsWhere(array('visible' => 0), 'category = ?', 3);
$ids = $model->insertRows(array($row1, $row2));      // one transaction: all rows or none
$n   = $model->delete($id);                          // soft delete: valid = 0
$n   = $model->erase($id);                           // removes the row
```

- [insertRow](./methods/insertrow.mdx), [updateRow](./methods/updaterow.mdx) and [updateRowsWhere](./methods/updaterowswhere.mdx) write only the given keys; `null` is stored as NULL. Do not pass `created`, `modified` or `valid` unless you mean to override them.
- Update and delete methods return the number of affected rows.
- [delete](./methods/delete.mdx) and [deleteWhere](./methods/deletewhere.mdx) are soft deletes (`valid = 0`; reads no longer see the row). [erase](./methods/erase.mdx) and [eraseWhere](./methods/erasewhere.mdx) remove the row.
- `updateWhere()`, `deleteWhere()`, `eraseWhere()` and `updateRowsWhere()` refuse an empty condition. Pass `'1 = 1'` to really affect every row.
- The older [insert](./methods/insert.mdx) and [update](./methods/update.mdx) still work, but they write `''` for every listed column missing from the data and cannot store NULL.
- [transaction](./methods/transaction.mdx) runs several writes, of any number of models, all or nothing.

## Errors

A failed query or connection throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException`. Its message ends with the SQL and its parameters (`(SQL: Select ... Where no_such_column = 'x')`); `getSql()` and `getParameters()` return them separately. Uncaught, it becomes an HTTP 500 and is written to the PHP error log and the [application log](../../basic/logging.mdx). Fegg 4 printed the error and called `exit` instead.

Catch it only when the page can do something useful, for example with a duplicate key:

```php title="Controller"
try {
    $model->insertRow($row);
} catch (\Fegg\Database\QueryException $e) {
    // ...
}
```

Wrong use of the builder (an array bound to `<`, an invalid column name, an update without a condition) throws `InvalidArgumentException` with a message saying what to do instead.

## Debugging

[toSql](./methods/tosql.mdx) returns the SELECT a chain would run, without running it. [debug](./methods/debug.mdx) prints the last query. Both fill in the values for reading.

```php title="Controller"
echo $model->where('category = ?', 2)->order('post_date', 'desc')->toSql();
// Select * From "cms_news" Where category = 2 AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1 Order By post_date DESC
```

## Raw SQL

For what the model cannot express, use [Fegg\Database\Pdo](../database/index.mdx) directly. It shares the connection (and an open transaction) with the models. Raw queries get no automatic `valid` / `created` / `modified` handling.

## Methods

--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/construct ---


# Model::\_\_construct

> Model class constructor

## Description

```php
public Model::__construct( [ boolean $is_admin = false, array $options = [] ] )
```

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `table`, `id` or `visible` is not a plain name, or when a `bind` type is unknown.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                                                             |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| $is_admin | boolean   | false   | `false`: reads only return rows whose `visible` column is 1 (public pages). `true`: every row (CMS) |
| $options  | array     | []      | Model options (below)                                                                               |

### Options

| Key       | Data type | Remarks                                                                                           |
| --------- | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `table`   | string    | Table name. Same as [setTableName](./settablename.mdx)                                            |
| `id`      | string    | Primary key column. Same as [setPrimary](./setprimary.mdx)                                        |
| `visible` | string    | Public flag column (optional). Same as [setVisible](./setvisible.mdx)                             |
| `bind`    | array     | Column types cast on write (`column => type`). Same as [setBindParam](./setbindparam.mdx)         |

`table`, `id` and `visible` must be plain names: letters, digits and `_`, optionally `table.column`. Other keys are ignored.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Model;

// For public pages (rows whose visible column is 0 are not returned)
$model = new Model(false, array(
    // Operate on the `cms_news` table
    'table'   => 'cms_news',

    // Primary key is `news_id`
    'id'      => 'news_id',

    // Public flag is `visible`
    'visible' => 'visible',

    // Cast these columns on insert and update
    'bind'    => array(
        'category'  => 'INTEGER',
        'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
        'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
        'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
    ),
));

// For CMS pages (every row, visible or not)
$admin = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/settablename ---


# Model::setTableName

> Set Table Name

## Description

```php
public self Model::setTableName( string $table )
```

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$table` is not a plain name (letters, digits and `_`).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks           |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ----------------- |
| $table | string    |         | Target table name |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->setTableName('cms_news');

// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table' => 'cms_news',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/gettablename ---


# Model::getTableName

> Get Table name

## Description

```php
public string Model::getTableName()
```

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table' => 'cms_news',
));

echo $model->getTableName();
// -> 'cms_news'
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/setprimary ---


# Model::setPrimary

> Set Primary ID name

## Description

```php
public self Model::setPrimary( string $id )
```

The primary key is used by [id](./id.mdx), [updateRow](./updaterow.mdx), [delete](./delete.mdx), [erase](./erase.mdx) and the other methods that take an ID. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$id` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                 |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------- |
| $id  | string    |         | Primary key column name |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->setPrimary('news_id');

// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'id' => 'news_id',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/getprimary ---

```php
public string|null Model::getPrimary()
```

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'id' => 'news_id',
));

echo $model->getPrimary();
// -> 'news_id'
```
