# Model::setVisible

> Set Visible flag name

## Description

```php
public self Model::setVisible( string|null $visible )
```

When the model was created with `$is_admin = false`, reads only return rows whose visible column is 1. `null` or `''` removes the flag. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$visible` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type   | Default | Remarks                  |
| -------- | ----------- | ------- | ------------------------ |
| $visible | string/null |         | Public flag column name  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->setVisible('visible');

// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'visible' => 'visible',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/getvisible ---

```php
public string|null Model::getVisible()
```

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

echo $model->getVisible();
// -> 'visible'
```

# Model::setBindParam

> Set Bind Parameter types

## Description

```php
public self Model::setBindParam( array $bind )
```

Replaces every bind type set before. See [addBindParam](./addbindparam.mdx) for the types and what they do.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                         |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |
| $bind | array     |         | Bind types (`column => type`)   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->setBindParam(array(
    'category'  => 'INTEGER',
    'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
    'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
    'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
));

// Same as the following
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'bind' => array(
        'category'  => 'INTEGER',
        'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
        'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
        'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
    ),
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/addbindparam ---


# Model::addBindParam

> Add Bind Parameter types

## Description

```php
public self Model::addBindParam( string $column, string $type )
```

The value of the column is cast to the type before it is written by [insertRow](./insertrow.mdx), [insertRows](./insertrows.mdx), [updateRow](./updaterow.mdx), [updateRowsWhere](./updaterowswhere.mdx), [insert](./insert.mdx), [update](./update.mdx) and [updateWhere](./updatewhere.mdx). `null` values are not cast. Reads are not affected.

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` for an unknown type.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                          |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name                      |
| $type   | string    |         | Bind type (case-insensitive)     |

### Types

| Type       | Cast                                                                                    |
| ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `INTEGER`  | `(int)`                                                                                 |
| `FLOAT`    | `(float)`                                                                               |
| `DATE`     | `Y-m-d`. A number is read as a Unix timestamp, anything else with `strtotime()`         |
| `DATETIME` | `Y-m-d H:i:s`. A number is read as a Unix timestamp, anything else with `strtotime()`   |

A `DATE` / `DATETIME` string that `strtotime()` cannot read is stored as `1970-01-01`; validate dates before writing them.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model();
$model->addBindParam('category', 'INTEGER');
$model->addBindParam('image_id', 'INTEGER');
$model->addBindParam('file_id', 'INTEGER');
$model->addBindParam('post_date', 'DATETIME');

// Same as the following
$model = new Model();
$model->setBindParam(array(
    'category'  => 'INTEGER',
    'image_id'  => 'INTEGER',
    'file_id'   => 'INTEGER',
    'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/getbindparam ---


# Model::getBindParam

> Get Bind Parameter types

## Description

```php
public array Model::getBindParam()
```

The types are returned in upper case.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'bind' => array(
        'category'  => 'integer',
        'post_date' => 'DATETIME',
    ),
));

var_dump($model->getBindParam());
```

```php title="Output"
array(2) {
  ["category"]=> string(7) "INTEGER"
  ["post_date"]=> string(8) "DATETIME"
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/select ---


# Model::select

> Set Select Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::select( string $select )
```

Sets the columns of the next SELECT (default `*`). The text is written into the SQL as it is: never put user input in it. Like every chain method, it is reset after the query.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                     |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| $select | string    |         | Columns or expressions, separated by commas |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Get the `news_id` and `title` columns
$records = $model->select('news_id, title')->find();

// Expressions work too
$records = $model->select('category, count(*) AS n')->group('category')->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/where ---


# Model::where

> Set Where Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::where( string $query, [ mixed $data = [], string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds a condition with `?` placeholders. Values are always bound, never written into the SQL. Several calls are joined with `AND` (or `OR` with `$andOr`).

- `$data` is one value, or an array of values in placeholder order. A query without placeholders (`'note IS NULL'`) needs none.
- An array bound to a single placeholder expands it: `id = ?` and `id IN (?)` become `id IN (?, ?, ...)`, `id <> ?` / `id != ?` / `id NOT IN (?)` become `NOT IN`, `title LIKE ?` becomes `(title LIKE ? OR title LIKE ? ...)` and `title NOT LIKE ?` becomes `(title NOT LIKE ? AND ...)`. An empty array matches no row (`=`, `IN`, `LIKE`) or every row (`<>`, `NOT IN`, `NOT LIKE`). An array on any other operator (`<`, `BETWEEN` ...) throws `InvalidArgumentException` when the query runs.
- A `?` inside a quoted string literal (`'?'`) is not a placeholder.
- An empty `$query` (`''`) adds nothing, which is handy for optional search conditions.

A query with its own `OR` outside brackets is put in brackets when it is joined to other conditions: `where('category = ?', 1)->where('title = ? OR summary = ?', array($q, $q))` is `category = 1 AND (title = ? OR summary = ?)`. Fegg 4 wrote `category = 1 AND title = ? OR summary = ?`, which matched rows of every category. [having](./having.mdx) works the same way.

The conditions the model adds by itself (the visible flag and `valid = 1`) are joined with `AND` after yours. When your conditions have an `OR` outside brackets, the model puts them in brackets first (`Where (a = ? OR b = ?) AND ...`), so hidden and deleted rows never match. To group conditions inside your own, use [whereOpen](./whereopen.mdx) and [whereClose](./whereclose.mdx).

For common cases there are helpers that also check the column name: [whereIn](./wherein.mdx), [whereNotIn](./wherenotin.mdx), [whereLike](./wherelike.mdx), [whereNull](./wherenull.mdx), [whereNotNull](./wherenotnull.mdx) and [whereBetween](./wherebetween.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                           |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| $query | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders (never put user input in it)      |
| $data  | mixed     | []      | One value, or an array of values in placeholder order             |
| $andOr | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`                |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$records = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->find();
// Select * From "cms_news" Where category = 2 AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1 ...

// Several placeholders: an array in placeholder order
$records = $model->where('category = ? AND post_date >= ?', array(2, '2026-01-01'))->find();

// An array on one placeholder: IN (...)
$records = $model->where('news_id IN (?)', array(1, 2, 3))->find();
// ... Where news_id IN (1, 2, 3) AND ...
```

### Complex WHERE query

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 2);
$model->whereOpen();
$model->where('title = ?', 'foo');
$model->where('post_date <= ?', '2021-01-01', 'OR');
$model->whereClose();

// Select *
// From "cms_news"
// Where category = 2
//   AND (title = 'foo' OR post_date <= '2021-01-01')
//   AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1
$records = $model->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/whereopen ---


# Model::whereOpen

> Add open bracket on Where query

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereOpen( [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Opens a bracket in the WHERE clause; close it with [whereClose](./whereclose.mdx). The first condition inside the bracket is not prefixed with `AND` / `OR`.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                                            |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| $andOr | string    | 'AND'   | How the bracket joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 2);
$model->whereOpen();
$model->where('title = ?', 'foo');
$model->where('post_date <= ?', '2021-01-01', 'OR');
$model->whereClose();

// Select *
// From "cms_news"
// Where category = 2
//   AND (title = 'foo' OR post_date <= '2021-01-01')
//   AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1
$records = $model->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/whereclose ---


# Model::whereClose

> Add close bracket on Where query

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereClose()
```

Closes the bracket opened with [whereOpen](./whereopen.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 2);
$model->whereOpen();
$model->where('title = ?', 'foo');
$model->where('post_date <= ?', '2021-01-01', 'OR');
$model->whereClose();

// Select *
// From "cms_news"
// Where category = 2
//   AND (title = 'foo' OR post_date <= '2021-01-01')
//   AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1
$records = $model->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherein ---


# Model::whereIn

> Match a column against a list of values

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereIn( string $column, array $values, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column IN (?, ?, ...)`. An empty array matches no row (`1 = 0`), so an empty selection returns nothing instead of failing. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name (`news_id` or `cms_news.news_id`)        |
| $values | array     |         | Values to match                                      |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Where news_id IN (1, 2, 3) AND ...
$records = $model->whereIn('news_id', array(1, 2, 3))->all();

// ... Where 1 = 0 AND ...  (no rows)
$records = $model->whereIn('news_id', array())->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherenotin ---


# Model::whereNotIn

> Exclude a list of values

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereNotIn( string $column, array $values, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column NOT IN (?, ?, ...)`. An empty array excludes nothing (`1 = 1`). Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name (`news_id` or `cms_news.news_id`)        |
| $values | array     |         | Values to exclude                                    |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Other news than the current one
// ... Where news_id NOT IN (5) AND ...
$records = $model->whereNotIn('news_id', array(5))->order('post_date', 'desc')->limit(3)->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherelike ---


# Model::whereLike

> Search a column for a term

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereLike( string $column, string $term, [ string $mode = 'contains', string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column LIKE ? ESCAPE '!'`. `%` and `_` in `$term` match literally, so user input can be passed as it is. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name or `$mode` is unknown.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default    | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |            | Column name                                          |
| $term   | string    |            | Text to search for                                   |
| $mode   | string    | 'contains' | Where the term must be (below)                       |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'      | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

### Modes

| Mode       | Pattern    |
| ---------- | ---------- |
| `contains` | `%term%`   |
| `prefix`   | `term%`    |
| `suffix`   | `%term`    |
| `exact`    | `term`     |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Where title LIKE '%50!%%' ESCAPE '!' AND ...  (titles containing "50%")
$records = $model->whereLike('title', '50%')->find();

// Titles starting with "News"
$records = $model->whereLike('title', 'News', 'prefix')->find();

// Search title or content
$keyword = $this->in('keyword');
$records = $model
    ->whereOpen()
    ->whereLike('title', $keyword)
    ->whereLike('content', $keyword, 'contains', 'OR')
    ->whereClose()
    ->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherenull ---


# Model::whereNull

> Match rows where a column is NULL

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereNull( string $column, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column IS NULL`. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name                                          |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Where image_id IS NULL AND ...
$records = $model->whereNull('image_id')->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherenotnull ---


# Model::whereNotNull

> Match rows where a column is not NULL

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereNotNull( string $column, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column IS NOT NULL`. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name                                          |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Where image_id IS NOT NULL AND ...
$records = $model->whereNotNull('image_id')->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/wherebetween ---


# Model::whereBetween

> Match a range of values

## Description

```php
public self Model::whereBetween( string $column, mixed $from, mixed $to, [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds `column BETWEEN ? AND ?`. Both ends are included. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                              |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Column name                                          |
| $from   | mixed     |         | Lowest value                                         |
| $to     | mixed     |         | Highest value                                        |
| $andOr  | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// News of January 2026
// ... Where post_date BETWEEN '2026-01-01 00:00:00' AND '2026-01-31 23:59:59' AND ...
$records = $model->whereBetween('post_date', '2026-01-01 00:00:00', '2026-01-31 23:59:59')->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/having ---


# Model::having

> Set Having Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::having( string $query, [ mixed $data = [], string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Adds a HAVING condition, used with [group](./group.mdx). `$data` works as in [where](./where.mdx): one value, or an array of values in placeholder order. With `having()`, [count](./count.mdx) counts the groups that match.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                       |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| $query | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders (never put user input in it)  |
| $data  | mixed     | []      | One value, or an array of values in placeholder order         |
| $andOr | string    | 'AND'   | How it joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR`            |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Categories with more than 5 news
// Select category, count(*) AS n From "cms_news" Where ... Group By category Having n > 5
$records = $model
    ->select('category, count(*) AS n')
    ->group('category')
    ->having('n > ?', 5)
    ->all();
```

### Complex HAVING query

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->select('category, count(*) AS n, max(post_date) AS latest')->group('category');
$model->having('n > ?', 5);
$model->havingOpen();
$model->having('category = ?', 1);
$model->having('latest >= ?', '2026-01-01', 'OR');
$model->havingClose();

// ... Group By category
// Having n > 5 AND (category = 1 OR latest >= '2026-01-01')
$records = $model->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/havingopen ---


# Model::havingOpen

> Add open bracket on Having query

## Description

```php
public self Model::havingOpen( [ string $andOr = 'AND' ] )
```

Opens a bracket in the HAVING clause; close it with [havingClose](./havingclose.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                     |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| $andOr | string    | 'AND'   | How the bracket joins the previous condition: `AND` or `OR` |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->select('category, count(*) AS n, max(post_date) AS latest')->group('category');
$model->having('n > ?', 5);
$model->havingOpen();
$model->having('category = ?', 1);
$model->having('latest >= ?', '2026-01-01', 'OR');
$model->havingClose();

// ... Group By category
// Having n > 5 AND (category = 1 OR latest >= '2026-01-01')
$records = $model->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/havingclose ---


# Model::havingClose

> Add close bracket on Having query

## Description

```php
public self Model::havingClose()
```

Closes the bracket opened with [havingOpen](./havingopen.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->select('category, count(*) AS n, max(post_date) AS latest')->group('category');
$model->having('n > ?', 5);
$model->havingOpen();
$model->having('category = ?', 1);
$model->having('latest >= ?', '2026-01-01', 'OR');
$model->havingClose();

// ... Group By category
// Having n > 5 AND (category = 1 OR latest >= '2026-01-01')
$records = $model->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/order ---


# Model::order

> Set Order Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::order( string $order, [ string $duration = 'ASC' ] )
```

Adds a column to ORDER BY. Call it again for a second sort key.

- `$order` must be a column name (`post_date` or `cms_news.post_date`); anything else, such as `'post_date desc'` or an expression, throws `InvalidArgumentException`. Use [orderRaw](./orderraw.mdx) for expressions.
- `$duration` is `DESC` (case-insensitive) or anything else for `ASC`, so it is safe to pass a sort direction from the request.
- `order('random')` sorts in random order (`ORDER BY RANDOM()`, an SQLite function).

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                         |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |
| $order    | string    |         | Column name, or `'random'`      |
| $duration | string    | 'ASC'   | `'ASC'` or `'DESC'`             |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// ... Order By post_date DESC,news_id DESC
$records = $model->order('post_date', 'desc')->order('news_id', 'desc')->find();

// 3 random news
$records = $model->order('random')->limit(3)->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/orderraw ---


# Model::orderRaw

> Order by an SQL expression

## Description

```php
public self Model::orderRaw( string $expression )
```

Adds an ORDER BY expression as it is. Use it for what [order](./order.mdx) does not accept: `CASE`, functions, several keys in one string. The expression is written into the SQL unchanged: never put user input in it.

## Arguments

| Name        | Data type | Default | Remarks                    |
| ----------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------- |
| $expression | string    |         | ORDER BY expression        |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Category 1 first, then the newest
// ... Order By CASE WHEN category = 1 THEN 0 ELSE 1 END, post_date DESC
$records = $model->orderRaw('CASE WHEN category = 1 THEN 0 ELSE 1 END, post_date DESC')->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/join ---


# Model::join

> Set Join Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::join( string|array $table, [ string $type = 'INNER', string $on = null, mixed $data = [] ] )
```

Joins another table. `$table` is a table name, or `array('table', 'alias')`; both must be plain names, or `InvalidArgumentException` is thrown. `$on` takes `?` placeholders and `$data` works as in [where](./where.mdx).

The automatic `valid = 1` and visible conditions only apply to the model's own table. Add them to `$on` for the joined table when you need them.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type    | Default | Remarks                                                   |
| ------ | ------------ | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| $table | string/array |         | Table name, or `array('table', 'alias')`                  |
| $type  | string       | 'INNER' | `'INNER'`, `'LEFT'` or `'RIGHT'` (anything else: `INNER`) |
| $on    | string       | null    | ON condition with `?` placeholders                        |
| $data  | mixed        | []      | Values for the ON placeholders                            |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Select cms_news.*, f.org_name
// From "cms_news"
// LEFT JOIN "cms_file" AS "f" ON f.file_id = cms_news.file_id AND f.valid = 1
// Where ...
$records = $model
    ->select('cms_news.*, f.org_name')
    ->join(array('cms_file', 'f'), 'LEFT', 'f.file_id = cms_news.file_id AND f.valid = ?', 1)
    ->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/group ---


# Model::group

> Set Group Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::group( string $group )
```

Adds GROUP BY. Several calls are joined with commas. The text is written into the SQL as it is: never put user input in it. With `group()`, [count](./count.mdx) counts the groups.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks         |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | --------------- |
| $group | string    |         | Group column(s) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Select category, count(*) AS n
// From "cms_news"
// Where ...
// Group By category
$records = $model->select('category, count(*) AS n')->group('category')->all();

// Number of categories that have news
$categories = $model->group('category')->count();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/listmax ---


# Model::listMax

> Set ListMax Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::listMax( [ int $listMax = 10 ] )
```

Sets the number of rows per page of [find](./find.mdx). Without it, `find()` returns 10 rows. A value under 1 is treated as 1. Only `find()` uses it: use [limit](./limit.mdx) with [all](./all.mdx) instead of a large `listMax()` to get every row.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks        |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | -------------- |
| $listMax | int       | 10      | Rows per page  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// First page, 5 rows
$records = $model->listMax(5)->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/current ---


# Model::current

> Set Current Page Parameter

## Description

```php
public self Model::current( [ int $current = 1 ] )
```

Sets the page that [find](./find.mdx) returns. A value that is not a number, under 1 or over the last page gives page 1, so the request parameter can be passed as it is.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks             |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------- |
| $current | int       | 1       | Current page number |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Page 2 of 5 rows (rows 6 to 10)
$records = $model->listMax(5)->current(2)->find();

// Page from the URL (?p=3)
$records = $model->listMax(5)->current($this->in('p'))->find();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/limit ---


# Model::limit

> Cap the number of rows

## Description

```php
public self Model::limit( int $count, [ int $offset = 0 ] )
```

Adds `LIMIT` to [all](./all.mdx), [column](./column.mdx) and [toSql](./tosql.mdx); [one](./one.mdx), [value](./value.mdx) and [exists](./exists.mdx) use the offset. [count](./count.mdx) ignores it. [find](./find.mdx) pages with [listMax](./listmax.mdx) and [current](./current.mdx) instead and throws `LogicException` when `limit()` was set.

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$count` is under 1 or `$offset` under 0.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                   |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------- |
| $count  | int       |         | Maximum number of rows    |
| $offset | int       | 0       | Number of rows to skip    |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// The 5 newest
$records = $model->order('post_date', 'desc')->limit(5)->all();

// The next 5 (rows 6 to 10)
$records = $model->order('post_date', 'desc')->limit(5, 5)->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/find ---


# Model::find

> Get one page of rows

## Description

```php
public array Model::find( [ array $options = [] ] )
```

Returns one page of matching rows (an array of associative arrays; an empty array when nothing matches). The page size is set with [listMax](./listmax.mdx) (default 10) and the page with [current](./current.mdx). `find()` counts the matching rows first, so [getPager](./getpager.mdx) can return the page links afterwards.

To get every row without paging, use [all](./all.mdx). `find()` throws `LogicException` when [limit](./limit.mdx) was set.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks                                          |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| $options | array     | []      | Overrides `select`, `list_max` and `current`     |

### Options

| Key        | Remarks                         |
| ---------- | ------------------------------- |
| `select`   | Same as [select](./select.mdx)  |
| `list_max` | Same as [listMax](./listmax.mdx) |
| `current`  | Same as [current](./current.mdx) |

Any other key throws an `Exception`.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$this->page['data'] = $model
    ->where('category = ?', 2)
    ->order('post_date', 'desc')
    ->listMax(20)
    ->current($this->in('p'))
    ->find();
$this->page['pager'] = $model->getPager();

// Same as listMax(20)->current($this->in('p'))
$records = $model->find(array('list_max' => 20, 'current' => $this->in('p')));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/getpager ---


# Model::getPager

> Get variables for making pager

## Description

```php
public array Model::getPager()
```

Returns the page links of the latest [find](./find.mdx) on this model (an empty array before the first `find()`). Each `find()` replaces them.

| Key                 | Remarks                                                |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| `current_page`      | Page returned by `find()`                              |
| `page_min`          | First page number to show (up to 4 before the current) |
| `page_max`          | Last page number to show (up to 4 after the current)   |
| `previous_page`     | Previous page. Only set when there is one              |
| `next_page`         | Next page. Only set when there is one                  |
| `last_page`         | Number of pages                                        |
| `number_of_records` | Number of matching rows                                |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// 23 rows, 5 per page, page 2
$records = $model->listMax(5)->current(2)->find();
$pager = $model->getPager();

print_r($pager);
```

```php title="Output"
Array
(
    [current_page] => 2
    [page_min] => 1
    [page_max] => 5
    [previous_page] => 1
    [next_page] => 3
    [last_page] => 5
    [number_of_records] => 23
)
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/all ---


# Model::all

> Get every matching row

## Description

```php
public array Model::all()
```

Returns every matching row (an array of associative arrays; an empty array when nothing matches), without paging. [limit](./limit.mdx) caps the number of rows. To show a list page by page, use [find](./find.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Every news of category 2
$records = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->order('post_date', 'desc')->all();

// The 5 newest
$records = $model->order('post_date', 'desc')->limit(5)->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/one ---


# Model::one

> Get the first matching row

## Description

```php
public array|null Model::one( [ array $options = [] ] )
```

Returns the first matching row as an associative array, or `null` when nothing matches. [order](./order.mdx) decides which row is first; the offset of [limit](./limit.mdx) is used.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks                                       |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| $options | array     | []      | `array('select' => ...)` overrides [select](./select.mdx) |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// The newest news, or null
$latest = $model->order('post_date', 'desc')->one();

$record = $model->where('title = ?', $title)->one();
if ($record === null) {
    // not found
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/id ---


# Model::id

> Get one record by specified primary ID

## Description

```php
public array|null Model::id( mixed $id )
```

Returns the row whose primary key (see [setPrimary](./setprimary.mdx)) is `$id`, or `null` when there is none. A public model (`$is_admin = false`) also returns `null` for a row that is not visible, and deleted rows (`valid = 0`) are never returned. Conditions chained before it with [where](./where.mdx) also apply.

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks           |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ----------------- |
| $id  | mixed     |         | Target primary ID |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$record = $model->id($this->in('id'));
if ($record === null) {
    // 404
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/value ---


# Model::value

> Get one value

## Description

```php
public mixed Model::value( string $column )
```

Returns the first column of the first matching row, or `null` when nothing matches. `$column` may be an expression such as `'max(post_date)'`; it is written into the SQL as it is, so never put user input in it.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                        |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------ |
| $column | string    |         | Column name or SQL expression  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$title = $model->where('news_id = ?', 5)->value('title');
// -> 'News 5', or null

$latest = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->value('max(post_date)');
// -> '2026-01-23 10:00:00'
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/column ---


# Model::column

> Get one column of every matching row

## Description

```php
public array Model::column( string $column, [ string $key = null ] )
```

Returns the values of `$column` of every matching row, as a list or, with `$key`, keyed by another column. [order](./order.mdx) and [limit](./limit.mdx) apply. Both names must be column names (`title` or `cms_news.title`), or `InvalidArgumentException` is thrown.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                        |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------ |
| $column | string    |         | Column to return               |
| $key    | string    | null    | Column to use as the array key |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$ids = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->column('news_id');
// -> array(2, 5, 8, ...)

$titles = $model->order('news_id')->column('title', 'news_id');
// -> array(1 => 'News 1', 2 => 'News 2', ...)
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/count ---


# Model::count

> Get the number of matching rows

## Description

```php
public int Model::count()
```

Returns the number of matching rows. With [group](./group.mdx) or [having](./having.mdx), it returns the number of groups. [order](./order.mdx) and [limit](./limit.mdx) are ignored.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$count = $model->count();
// -> 23

$count = $model->where('category = ?', 2)->count();
// -> 7

// Number of categories
$count = $model->group('category')->count();
// -> 3
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/exists ---


# Model::exists

> Check whether a row matches

## Description

```php
public bool Model::exists()
```

Returns `true` when at least one row matches.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

if ($model->where('title = ?', $title)->exists()) {
    // a news with this title already exists
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/tosql ---


# Model::toSql

> Get the SELECT a chain would run

## Description

```php
public string Model::toSql()
```

Returns the SELECT that [all](./all.mdx) would run with the conditions set so far, with the values filled in, without running it. Like after a query, the conditions are cleared afterwards. The values are quoted for reading only: never run the result.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

echo $model
    ->where('category = ?', 2)
    ->whereIn('news_id', array(1, 2))
    ->order('post_date', 'desc')
    ->limit(5)
    ->toSql();
```

```sql title="Output"
Select * From "cms_news" Where category = 2 AND news_id IN (1, 2) AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1 Order By post_date DESC Limit 0, 5
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/insertrow ---


# Model::insertRow

> Insert a row from an associative array

## Description

```php
public int|string Model::insertRow( array $row )
```

Inserts one row from `column => value` pairs and returns the new ID (an `int` for integer keys).

- Only the given columns are written; `null` is stored as NULL.
- `created`, `modified` and `valid` are filled automatically. Give them only to override them.
- [bind](./addbindparam.mdx) types are applied.
- Throws `InvalidArgumentException` for an empty array, a column name that is not a plain name, or a value that is an array or an object. A failed insert (for example a duplicate key) throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException`.

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                       |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------- |
| $row | array     |         | Values (`column => value`)    |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$id = $model->insertRow(array(
    'title'     => $this->in('title'),
    'category'  => 2,
    'post_date' => '2026-01-15 10:00:00',
    'image_id'  => null,
));
// -> 24
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/insertrows ---


# Model::insertRows

> Insert several rows in one transaction

## Description

```php
public array Model::insertRows( array $rows )
```

Inserts each row with [insertRow](./insertrow.mdx) inside one [transaction](./transaction.mdx) and returns the new IDs in order. When one row fails, none is inserted and the exception is rethrown. Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when an element of `$rows` is not an array.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                                  |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| $rows | array     |         | List of rows (`column => value` arrays)  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$ids = $model->insertRows(array(
    array('title' => 'First', 'category' => 1),
    array('title' => 'Second', 'category' => 2),
));
// -> array(25, 26)
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/updaterow ---


# Model::updateRow

> Update a row from an associative array

## Description

```php
public int Model::updateRow( array $row, mixed $id )
```

Updates the row whose primary key is `$id` and returns the number of updated rows (0 when there is no such row). Only the given columns are written; `null` is stored as NULL. `modified` is set automatically, and [bind](./addbindparam.mdx) types are applied.

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` like [insertRow](./insertrow.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                       |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------- |
| $row | array     |         | Values (`column => value`)    |
| $id  | mixed     |         | Target primary ID             |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Only title (and modified) are written; the other columns keep their value
// Update "cms_news" Set "title"= 'New title', modified = '2026-11-09 17:40:25' Where news_id = 1
$updated = $model->updateRow(array('title' => 'New title'), 1);
// -> 1
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/updaterowswhere ---


# Model::updateRowsWhere

> Update the rows matching a condition from an associative array

## Description

```php
public int Model::updateRowsWhere( array $row, string $where, [ mixed $params = [] ] )
```

Updates every row matching `$where` and returns the number of updated rows. Columns are written as in [updateRow](./updaterow.mdx). `$params` works as in [where](./where.mdx): one value, or an array of values in placeholder order (an array on a single `IN (?)` placeholder expands it).

The condition is required: an empty `$where` throws `InvalidArgumentException`. Pass `'1 = 1'` to really update every row. Conditions chained with [where](./where.mdx) are not used.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                                |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| $row    | array     |         | Values (`column => value`)                             |
| $where  | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders                        |
| $params | mixed     | []      | One value, or an array of values in placeholder order  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Hide every news of category 3
$updated = $model->updateRowsWhere(array('visible' => 0), 'category = ?', 3);

$updated = $model->updateRowsWhere(array('category' => 1), 'news_id IN (?)', array(4, 5, 6));

// Every row
$updated = $model->updateRowsWhere(array('visible' => 1), '1 = 1');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/increment ---


# Model::increment

> Add to a numeric column

## Description

```php
public int Model::increment( string $column, mixed $id, [ int|float $by = 1 ] )
```

Adds `$by` to `$column` of the row whose primary key is `$id`, in the UPDATE itself (`"column" = "column" + ?`), and returns the number of updated rows. Simultaneous requests do not lose counts, as they would when reading, adding and writing back.

Like every update, it also sets `modified`. Call [unsetRegularUseQuery](./unsetregularusequery.mdx) first to leave `modified` alone (for example for a view counter).

Throws `InvalidArgumentException` when `$column` is not a plain name or `$by` is not a number.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                         |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |
| $column | string    |         | Numeric column                  |
| $id     | mixed     |         | Target primary ID               |
| $by     | int/float | 1       | Amount to add; negative to subtract |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
// The table has a view_count INTEGER column
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Update "cms_news" Set "view_count" = "view_count" + 1 Where news_id = 24
$model->unsetRegularUseQuery()->increment('view_count', 24);

// Subtract 2 (modified is updated)
$model->increment('view_count', 24, -2);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/insert ---


# Model::insert

> Execute Insert data

## Description

```php
public string Model::insert( string $item, array $data )
```

Inserts one row and returns the new ID as a string. For new code, use [insertRow](./insertrow.mdx): `insert()` writes `''` for every column listed in `$item` that is missing from `$data`, and cannot store NULL.

`$data` is an associative array (values matched to `$item` by name) or a list (values in the order of `$item`). `created`, `modified` and `valid` are filled automatically and [bind](./addbindparam.mdx) types are applied.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                         |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |
| $item | string    |         | Columns, separated by commas    |
| $data | array     |         | Values                          |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$item = 'title, content';
$data = array(
    'title'   => 'Sample title',
    'content' => 'Sample content',
);

// Returns the new ID
$insertedId = $model->insert($item, $data);
// -> '24'
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/update ---


# Model::update

> Execute Update the record by specified primary ID

## Description

```php
public int Model::update( string $item, array $data, mixed $id )
```

Updates the row whose primary key is `$id` and returns the number of updated rows. For new code, use [updateRow](./updaterow.mdx): `update()` writes `''` for every column listed in `$item` that is missing from `$data`, and cannot store NULL. `modified` is set automatically and [bind](./addbindparam.mdx) types are applied.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks                         |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |
| $item | string    |         | Columns, separated by commas    |
| $data | array     |         | Values                          |
| $id   | mixed     |         | Target primary ID               |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$item = 'title, content';
$data = array(
    'title'   => 'Sample title',
    'content' => 'Sample content',
);
$updated = $model->update($item, $data, 1);
// -> 1
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/updatewhere ---


# Model::updateWhere

> Execute Update the records by specified condition

## Description

```php
public int Model::updateWhere( string $item, array $data, string $where, mixed $wh_data )
```

Updates every row matching `$where` and returns the number of updated rows. For new code, use [updateRowsWhere](./updaterowswhere.mdx): `updateWhere()` writes `''` for every column listed in `$item` that is missing from `$data`, and cannot store NULL.

`$wh_data` is one value, or an array with one value per placeholder. The condition is required: an empty `$where` throws `InvalidArgumentException` (Fegg 4 updated every row). Pass `'1 = 1'` to really update every row.

## Arguments

| Name     | Data type | Default | Remarks                                      |
| -------- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| $item    | string    |         | Columns, separated by commas                 |
| $data    | array     |         | Values                                       |
| $where   | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders              |
| $wh_data | mixed     |         | Values for the placeholders                  |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$item = 'title, content';
$data = array(
    'title'   => 'Sample title',
    'content' => 'Sample content',
);
$updated = $model->updateWhere($item, $data, 'category = ? AND news_id > ?', array(2, 10));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/delete ---


# Model::delete

> Execute Logical Delete the record by specified primary ID

## Description

```php
public int Model::delete( mixed $id )
```

Soft-deletes the row whose primary key is `$id`: sets `valid = 0` (and `modified`), so reads no longer return it. Returns the number of updated rows. To remove the row from the table, use [erase](./erase.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                    |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | -------------------------- |
| $id  | mixed     |         | Deleting target Primary ID |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$deleted = $model->delete(1);
// -> 1
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/deletewhere ---


# Model::deleteWhere

> Execute Logical Delete the records by specified condition

## Description

```php
public int Model::deleteWhere( string $where, mixed $data )
```

Soft-deletes every row matching `$where` (sets `valid = 0`) and returns the number of updated rows. `$data` is one value, or an array with one value per placeholder.

The condition is required: an empty `$where` throws `InvalidArgumentException` (Fegg 4 deleted every row). Pass `'1 = 1'` to really delete every row.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                          |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------- |
| $where | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders  |
| $data  | mixed     |         | Values for the placeholders      |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$deleted = $model->deleteWhere('category = ?', 1);

// Every row
$deleted = $model->deleteWhere('1 = 1', array());
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/erase ---

```php
public int Model::erase( mixed $id )
```

Removes the row whose primary key is `$id` from the table (`DELETE`) and returns the number of deleted rows. For a soft delete, use [delete](./delete.mdx).

## Arguments

| ---- | --------- | ------- | ------------------------- |
| $id  | mixed     |         | Erasing target Primary ID |

## Example

```php title="Controller"

    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// -> 1
```

# Model::eraseWhere

> Execute Physical Delete the records by specified condition

## Description

```php
public int Model::eraseWhere( string $where, mixed $data )
```

Removes every row matching `$where` from the table (`DELETE`) and returns the number of deleted rows. `$data` is one value, or an array with one value per placeholder.

The condition is required: an empty `$where` throws `InvalidArgumentException` (Fegg 4 deleted every row). Pass `'1 = 1'` to really delete every row.

## Arguments

| Name   | Data type | Default | Remarks                          |
| ------ | --------- | ------- | -------------------------------- |
| $where | string    |         | Condition with `?` placeholders  |
| $data  | mixed     |         | Values for the placeholders      |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

// Remove the rows deleted with delete()
$erased = $model->eraseWhere('valid = ?', 0);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/transaction ---


# Model::transaction

> Run queries all or nothing

## Description

```php
public mixed Model::transaction( callable $callback )
```

Runs `$callback` in a transaction and returns what it returns. The transaction is committed when the callback returns and rolled back when it throws; the exception is then rethrown.

- The callback receives this model.
- Every query inside the callback takes part, whatever model (or [Fegg\Database\Pdo](../../database/index.mdx)) runs it.
- A `transaction()` call inside another one joins the outer transaction.

## Arguments

| Name      | Data type | Default | Remarks                       |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------- |
| $callback | callable  |         | Receives this model           |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Model;

$news = new Model(true, array('table' => 'cms_news', 'id' => 'news_id', 'visible' => 'visible'));
$labs = new Model(true, array('table' => 'cms_lab', 'id' => 'lab_id', 'visible' => 'visible'));

$id = $news->transaction(function (Model $news) use ($labs) {
    $id = $news->insertRow(array('title' => 'A'));
    $labs->insertRow(array('title' => 'B'));   // another model joins the same transaction

    return $id;
});
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/create ---


# Model::create

> Execute Create Table

## Description

```php
public void Model::create( array $fields )
```

Creates the model's table. Tables are normally defined in `site/config/database/*.yaml` and created with `./fegg-cli migration`; use this only for tables made at run time. Throws `Fegg\Database\QueryException` when the query fails.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                  |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| $fields | array     |         | List of column definitions (below)       |

### Column definition

Every key must be present.

| Key          | Data type | Remarks                                                     |
| ------------ | --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| `name`       | string    | Column name                                                 |
| `type`       | string    | Column type                                                 |
| `notnull`    | boolean   | Add NOT NULL                                                |
| `dflt_value` | mixed     | Default value (`null`, `''` or `0`: no default)             |
| `pk`         | boolean   | Add PRIMARY KEY                                             |
| `ai`         | boolean   | Add AUTOINCREMENT                                           |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table' => 'sample',
));

// Create Table "sample" (sample_id INTEGER NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, title TEXT DEFAULT 'none', valid BOOLEAN, created DATETIME, modified DATETIME)
$model->create(array(
    array('name' => 'sample_id', 'type' => 'INTEGER',  'notnull' => true,  'dflt_value' => null,   'pk' => true,  'ai' => true),
    array('name' => 'title',     'type' => 'TEXT',     'notnull' => false, 'dflt_value' => 'none', 'pk' => false, 'ai' => false),
    array('name' => 'valid',     'type' => 'BOOLEAN',  'notnull' => false, 'dflt_value' => null,   'pk' => false, 'ai' => false),
    array('name' => 'created',   'type' => 'DATETIME', 'notnull' => false, 'dflt_value' => null,   'pk' => false, 'ai' => false),
    array('name' => 'modified',  'type' => 'DATETIME', 'notnull' => false, 'dflt_value' => null,   'pk' => false, 'ai' => false),
));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/droptable ---

# Model::pragma

> Execute Pragma

## Description

```php
public array Model::pragma( string $mode )
```

Runs an SQLite `PRAGMA` on the model's table and returns its rows. SQLite only.

## Arguments

| Name  | Data type | Default | Remarks     |
| ----- | --------- | ------- | ----------- |
| $mode | string    |         | Pragma mode |

### Pragma mode

| Mode         | Query                                                 |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| `table`      | `PRAGMA table_info("table")` (also any unknown mode)  |
| `index_list` | `PRAGMA index_list("table")`                          |
| `index`      | `PRAGMA index_info("table")`                          |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table' => 'cms_news',
));

// PRAGMA table_info("cms_news")
$columns = $model->pragma('table');

print_r($columns[0]);
```

```php title="Output"
Array
(
    [cid] => 0
    [name] => news_id
    [type] => INTEGER
    [notnull] => 1
    [dflt_value] =>
    [pk] => 1
)
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/reset ---


# Model::reset

> Reset Model

## Description

```php
public self Model::reset()
```

Forgets the conditions set with the chain methods: where, having, order, join, group, select, listMax, current and limit. The model does this by itself after every query, failed ones included, so call it only to drop a chain you built but did not run. The table, primary key, visible flag, bind types and the pager are kept.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table' => 'cms_news',
    'id'    => 'news_id',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 1)->order('post_date', 'desc');

// Changed our mind: start again
$model->reset();

// Select * From "cms_news" Where 1 = 1 And "cms_news".valid = 1
$records = $model->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/debug ---


# Model::debug

> Print the last executed query

## Description

```php
public void Model::debug()
```

Prints (`echo`) the last query of this model with its values filled in. The values are quoted for reading only: never run the result. To see a SELECT without running it, use [toSql](./tosql.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(false, array(
    'table'   => 'cms_news',
    'id'      => 'news_id',
    'visible' => 'visible',
));

$model->where('category = ?', 1)
    ->listMax(5)
    ->current(2)
    ->find();

$model->debug();
// -> Select * From "cms_news" Where category = 1 AND cms_news.visible = '1' And "cms_news".valid = 1 Limit 5, 5
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/unsetregularusequery ---


# Model::unsetRegularUseQuery

> Skip the automatic queries for the next query

## Description

```php
public self Model::unsetRegularUseQuery()
```

For the next query only, skips the queries added for every table (`regular_use` in `database_regular_use_query.php`, see [Configuration](../../database/configuration.mdx)): the `valid = 1` condition of reads, `created` / `modified` / `valid` on insert and `modified` on update. [disabledRegularUseQuery](./disabledregularusequery.mdx) skips them for good.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table' => 'cms_news',
    'id'    => 'news_id',
));

// Include the rows deleted with delete() (valid = 0)
$records = $model->unsetRegularUseQuery()->all();

// Count a view without changing "modified" (the table has a view_count column)
$model->unsetRegularUseQuery()->increment('view_count', 1);
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/unsetregularusequeryfortable ---


# Model::unsetRegularUseQueryForTable

> Skip the automatic queries of the table for the next query

## Description

```php
public self Model::unsetRegularUseQueryForTable()
```

For the next query only, skips the queries configured for this table (the `table` section of `database_regular_use_query.php`, see [Configuration](../../database/configuration.mdx)). The queries added for every table still apply; see [unsetRegularUseQuery](./unsetregularusequery.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table' => 'cms_news',
    'id'    => 'news_id',
));

$records = $model->unsetRegularUseQueryForTable()->all();
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/model/methods/disabledregularusequery ---


# Model::disabledRegularUseQuery

> Skip the automatic queries for good

## Description

```php
public self Model::disabledRegularUseQuery()
```

Skips every automatic query (for every table and for this table, see [Configuration](../../database/configuration.mdx)) for every later query of this model instance: reads also return rows with `valid = 0`, and inserts and updates no longer fill `created`, `modified` and `valid`. To skip them for one query only, use [unsetRegularUseQuery](./unsetregularusequery.mdx).

## Example

```php title="Controller"
// A table without valid / created / modified columns
$model = new Model(true, array(
    'table' => 'sample_log',
    'id'    => 'log_id',
));
$model->disabledRegularUseQuery();

$records = $model->all();
$model->insertRow(array('message' => 'Hello'));
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/oembed ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Getting the embed URL of a YouTube video etc. through oEmbed

`Fegg\Tool\OEmbed` asks the oEmbed endpoint of the service matching a URL (e.g. a YouTube video page) and extracts the URL to embed. The request is sent with `Fegg\Http\Client` (cURL when it is installed, otherwise PHP's http stream wrapper; 10 second timeout). A transport failure or an answer other than HTTP 200 throws an `Exception`.

## How to use

Import `Fegg\Tool\OEmbed` and create it with `new`, passing the page URL. The constructor throws an `Exception` when the URL is not supported or the request fails.

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Tool\OEmbed;

class Movie extends Controller
{
    public function index()
    {
        try {
            $oembed = new OEmbed('https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xxxxxxxxxxx');
            $this->page['youtube'] = $oembed->media;
        } catch (\Exception $e) {
            $this->page['youtube'] = '';
        }

        $this->displayPage('movie');
    }
}
```

```html title="Template"
{{ if $page.youtube }}
<iframe width="560" height="315" src="{{ $page.youtube|feggUrl }}" frameborder="0" allowfullscreen class="c-youtube"></iframe>
{{ end }}
```

## Properties

The result is read through these read-only properties.

| Property | Data type | Remarks                                                                 |
| -------- | --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| media    | string    | URL to embed: `src` of the `iframe` (video), `src` of the `img` (photo) or `href` of the `a` (link) |
| type     | string    | `video`, `photo` or `link`                                              |
| data     | array     | The whole oEmbed response (`title`, `author_name`, `thumbnail_url`, `html` ...) |
| service  | string    | Matched service name in the configuration, e.g. `youtube`              |
| url      | string    | URL passed to the constructor                                           |

Reading any other property throws an `Exception`.

## Configuration

The services are defined in `htdocs/_fegg/core/config/oembed_schemes.php`. Only YouTube is defined by default.

```php title="htdocs/_fegg/core/config/oembed_schemes.php"
<?php
defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;


$oembed_schemes = array();

$oembed_schemes['youtube'] = array(
    'endpoint' => 'https://www.youtube.com/oembed',
    'schemes' => array(
        '|^https?://.*\\.youtube\\.com/watch.*$|i',
        '|^https?://.*\\.youtube\\.com/v/.*$|i',
        '|^https?://youtu\\.be/.*$|i',
        '|^https?://.*\\.youtube\\.com/playlist\\?list\\=.*$|i',
        '|^https?://youtube\\.com/playlist\\?list\\=.*$|i',
    ),
);
```

`endpoint` is the oEmbed endpoint of the service and `schemes` are regular expressions matching the supported URLs. To add a service, copy the file to `htdocs/_fegg/site/config/oembed_schemes.php` and add an entry there (the file in `site/config/` is used instead of the core's one).

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/oembed/methods/construct ---


# OEmbed::\_\_construct

> Get the oEmbed data of a URL

## Description

```php
public OEmbed::__construct( string $url, [ array $config = array() ] )
```

Finds the service whose `schemes` match `$url`, requests its oEmbed endpoint and reads the embedded HTML. The result is available through the [properties](../index.mdx#properties).

Throws an `Exception` when no service matches `$url`, the service has no endpoint, the request fails, or the response has no `iframe`, `img` or `a` element.

## Arguments

| Name    | Data type | Default | Remarks                                         |
| ------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| $url    | string    |         | URL of the page to embed                        |
| $config | array     | array() | Options sent to the endpoint (see below)        |

| Key       | Data type | Default | Remarks                       |
| --------- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------- |
| maxwidth  | int       | null    | Maximum width of the embed    |
| maxheight | int       | null    | Maximum height of the embed   |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\OEmbed;

$oembed = new OEmbed('https://youtu.be/xxxxxxxxxxx', array('maxwidth' => 640));

echo $oembed->type;          // -> "video"
echo $oembed->media;         // -> "https://www.youtube.com/embed/xxxxxxxxxxx?feature=oembed"
echo $oembed->data['title']; // -> Video title
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/rss ---


import { CategoryDocCardList } from '@site/src/components/CategoryDocCardList';

# Introduction

> Providing some functions for analyzing RSS document

`Fegg\Tool\RSS` loads an RSS 1.0 (RDF) or RSS 2.0 feed and returns its entries as arrays.

## How to use

Import `Fegg\Tool\RSS` and create it with `new`, passing the feed URL.

```php title="Controller"
<?php
namespace Site\Controller;

defined('FEGG_CORE_DIR') || exit;

use Fegg\Tool\RSS;

class Index extends Controller
{
    public function index()
    {
        $rss = new RSS('https://example.com/feed');
        $this->page['posts'] = $rss->fetchAll(10);

        $this->displayPage('index');
    }
}
```

## Entry data

Each entry returned by [RSS::fetch](./methods/fetch.mdx) and [RSS::fetchAll](./methods/fetchall.mdx) is an array with these keys.

| Key         | Data type | Remarks                                                         |
| ----------- | --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| title       | string    | Title                                                           |
| pubDate     | string    | Date as written in the feed (`pubDate`, or `dc:date` for RSS 1.0) |
| datetime    | DateTime  | `pubDate` as a `DateTime` object                                |
| link        | string    | Link URL                                                        |
| description | string    | Description (HTML entities decoded)                             |
| content     | string    | `content:encoded` (HTML entities decoded)                       |
| author      | string    | `dc:creator`                                                    |
| category    | array     | Category names (`category`, or `dc:subject` for RSS 1.0)        |
| tag         | array     | Always an empty array (not implemented)                         |

:::note

- Entries whose title contains `PR:` (advertisements) are skipped.
- Atom feeds are not supported: no entry is returned.
- The feed is loaded with `simplexml_load_file()` in the constructor. A feed that cannot be loaded is not handled, so check the URL beforehand when it may be unavailable.

:::

## Methods

<CategoryDocCardList label="Methods" />


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/rss/methods/construct ---


Loads and parses the feed. The entries can then be read with [RSS::fetch](./fetch.mdx) or [RSS::fetchAll](./fetchall.mdx).

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks      |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ------------ |
| $url | string    |         | RSS Feed URL |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\RSS;

$rss = new RSS('https://example.com/feed');
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/rss/methods/fetch ---


# RSS::fetch

> Fetch the next entry of RSS feed

## Description

```php
public mixed RSS::fetch()
```

Returns the next entry as an array (see [Entry data](../index.mdx#entry-data)), or `false` when there are no more entries.

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\RSS;

$rss = new RSS('https://example.com/feed');

while ($post = $rss->fetch()) {
    // $post['title'], $post['link'], $post['datetime'] ...
}
```


--- https://lionheart-group.github.io/coding-style-guide/docs/fegg/next/library/rss/methods/fetchall ---


# RSS::fetchAll

> Fetch all entries or specified number of entries of RSS feed

## Description

```php
public array RSS::fetchAll( [ int $max = 0 ] )
```

Returns the remaining entries (see [Entry data](../index.mdx#entry-data)) as an array.

## Arguments

| Name | Data type | Default | Remarks                                         |
| ---- | --------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| $max | int       | 0       | Maximum number of entries / `0`: all the entries |

## Example

```php title="Controller"
use Fegg\Tool\RSS;

$rss = new RSS('https://example.com/feed');
$posts = $rss->fetchAll(5);

foreach ($posts as $post) {
    // $post['title'], $post['link'], $post['datetime'] ...
}
```
